Abstract (英文): Few-shot remote sensing scene classification (FSRSSC) aims to recognize novel scene categories from only a few labeled images. Most FSRSSC methods summarize the feature map of an image by global average pooling, which treats the few objects that define an aerial scene and the large background around them alike, and they rarely consider that aerial images have no canonical orientation. To address these issues, we propose RoCoP, a parameter-free component pooling network trained across orientations. RoCoP consists of a feature extractor, a non-negative decomposition module (NDM) and a component scoring module (CSM). NDM factorizes the local descriptors of each image into a few additive components with a deterministic initialization and a single multiplicative update, so that the positions of one object are grouped together. CSM weights each component by its activation magnitude and converts the weights into a soft mask for pooling. Because both modules are invariant to permutations of feature positions, the only rotation-sensitive part of the pipeline is the backbone, which we train to classify randomly rotated views of every image. Experiments on NWPU-RESISC45, AID and UC Merced show that component pooling and rotated-view training are complementary: together they improve a strong transfer-learning baseline by about 2.5 points in 5-way 1-shot accuracy on NWPU-RESISC45 and AID, without adding parameters at inference. Under an identical protocol, RoCoP outperforms re-implemented metric-based and task-adaptive methods, and NDM and CSM also improve existing methods when plugged into them. We further identify the regime in which the method does not apply, namely very small base sets.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Running deep models for medical image analysis at the point of care is challenging with standard architectures: convolutional neural networks (CNNs) are often too heavy for edge devices, while Vision Transformers (ViTs) require massive training datasets to compensate for missing inductive biases. This study investigates how far the dual-token distillation used to train data-efficient ViTs can be pushed when applied across architectures, from a CNN-based teacher into a lightweight ViT student. LiteMed-ViT is introduced as a dual-token ViT featuring a convolutional stem and a class-balanced distillation objective; at 0.234M parameters, LiteMed-ViT carries 47.8x fewer parameters than its ResNet18 teacher at a memory footprint of just 0.89 MB. Evaluated against three compact CNN baselines on three MedMNIST subsets of varying modality and class balance, LiteMed-ViT attains higher accuracy than all three baselines on BloodMNIST (95.05%) and two on PathMNIST (89.15%). Under MedMNIST-C corruptions, it is at least as robust as every compact CNN across all subsets, outperforming them on BloodMNIST and PathMNIST without declining in relative standing as severity increases. Particular attention is devoted to identifying the exact conditions where performance plateaus. On DermaMNIST, LiteMed-ViT's balanced accuracy remains up to 1.9 points below the strongest compact CNNs and does not benefit from higher input resolutions up to 224 pixels, where ResNet18 gains 12.6 points. Stronger teachers do not yield a more accurate student, and overconfident ones lower its DermaMNIST balanced accuracy unless calibrated.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Image tokenizers convert pixels into compact latent sequences for efficient generative modeling. Most approaches arrange latents on a downsampled two-dimensional grid, tying sequence length to image resolution. We compare two variable-length strategies: computationally efficient patch-derived tokens, which inherit explicit spatial organization, and more demanding register tokens, which have no predefined spatial layout. Our reconstruction results reveal that patch-derived tokens perform substantially better under extreme compression, whereas register tokens achieve slightly better reconstruction quality at intermediate budgets. At high token budgets, both approaches achieve similar reconstruction quality. For downstream generative tasks, register tokens outperform patch-derived tokens. Adding representation alignment with external pretrained visual encoders substantially improves the generative performance of patch-derived tokens, allowing them to match or outperform register tokens depending on the token budget. We further find that both approaches learn spatially localized representations. Patch-derived tokens largely retain their explicitly imposed spatial organization, while register tokens develop an implicit two-dimensional spatial structure despite having no predefined layout. Representation alignment reduces this spatial organizational constraint, enabling individual tokens to exert a stronger non-local influence.

</details>

Abstract (英文): High-resolution remote sensing (RS) imagery provides fine spatial detail over large areas, making it essential for fine-grained scene understanding. Referring Remote Sensing Image Segmentation (RRSIS) aims to segment regions described by natural language, yet many existing methods rely on sequential alignment--fusion pipelines with limited joint modeling of cross-scale visual hierarchies and linguistic dependencies. Moreover, dense attention mechanisms struggle with the sparsity and wide spatial dispersion of RS targets, while current datasets offer limited structured evaluation of multi-target references, multi-hop relational reasoning, and multi-granularity descriptions, limiting both model development and evaluation. To address these challenges, we propose a Cross-Scale Sparse Attention Model (CSSAM), which aligns multiscale visual features with complex referring expressions through two key modules. The Cross-scale Modality-aligned Fusion Module (CMFM) performs unified cross-scale cross-modal fusion, while the Dynamic Multimodal Sparse Hybrid Attention (DMSHA) selectively attends to informative visual--textual tokens, reducing dense visual interactions. Furthermore, we introduce M$^{3}$RRSIS-D, a RRSIS dataset explicitly designed around three core reasoning dimensions: multi-target (M$^{1}$), reference-dependent multi-hop (M$^{2}$), and multi-granularity (M$^{3}$) expressions. Extensive experiments on M$^{3}$RRSIS-D and two public RRSIS benchmarks demonstrate that CSSAM achieves competitive performance across accuracy, fine-grained reasoning, and high-IoU localization metrics, validating its effectiveness for complex semantic reasoning in realistic RS scenarios. Code, dataset, and model weights will be released upon acceptance.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Automated prostate lesion detection from diffusion-weighted MR imaging (DWI) conventionally relies on reconstructed images and does not fully use all acquisition information. We present an end-to-end network that uses complex multicoil DWI data and coil sensitivity information for prostate lesion segmentation.
  We included 269 fastMRI prostate examinations with additional voxel-level lesion annotations. Of these, 64 examinations contained PIRADS 4 or 5 lesions. The proposed network, VarNet-Seg, consists of a k-space variational network (VarNet) with six variational cascades and an image-space 3D segmentation U-Net. Comparator models used the same segmentation architecture with fully coil-combined images (CC-Seg), fixed root-sum-of-squares reconstruction (RSS-Seg), or fixed SENSE reconstruction (SENSE-Seg). We performed ablations with end-to-end training and data consistency (DC) enabled or disabled. We evaluated lesion segmentation using Dice and patient-level detection using AUROC in five-fold cross-validation with full sampling, simulated undersampling, added noise, or both.
  VarNet-Seg achieved the highest Dice at every undersampling level. At eight-fold acceleration, Dice was 0.22 for VarNet-Seg, 0.14 for SENSE-Seg and 0.16 for VarNet-Seg (no DC). The paired difference from SENSE-Seg was 0.08 (95% CI, 0.04-0.11). With full sampling, VarNet-Seg and RSS-Seg performed similarly (0.40 vs 0.39). Patient-level AUROC decreased from 0.75 with full sampling to 0.63 at eight-fold acceleration; corresponding RSS-Seg AUROCs were 0.76 and 0.64.
  The proposed multicoil network improved prostate lesion segmentation under simulated undersampling compared to conventional models. Matched ablations supported contributions from both end-to-end training and data consistency. Prospective evaluation is needed to establish whether this approach can be used clinically in accelerated prostate DWI.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Video spatial reasoning requires multimodal large language models (MLLMs) to understand how objects are arranged and related across viewpoints. While these models can recognize objects in individual frames, integrating these observations to solve complex spatial tasks remains challenging. We examine route planning as a representative task and observe that existing models become substantially less accurate as the number of required turns increases, with several models achieving accuracy close to random guessing on routes requiring three or more turns. This finding suggests that recognizing landmarks alone is insufficient: models must also connect observations across viewpoints and track how landmarks are arranged in a shared space. Motivated by this observation, we introduce GeoLift, a training-free framework that uses feed-forward 3D reconstruction to map objects detected in individual frames into a shared metric 3D scene. GeoLift combines a frozen MLLM for 2D grounding with pretrained geometry models for scene reconstruction. It then uses shared geometric regions, called superpoints, to associate observations of the same object across views without a learned matcher. The reconstructed 3D map enables explicit computation of spatial relations and multistep routes, and provides geometric evidence for estimating distances and sizes. With all model weights frozen, GeoLift improves Qwen3-VL-4B's average score on VSI-Bench from 55.4% to 64.1%, including an increase in route-planning accuracy from 30.9% to 53.6%, and achieves 59.8% accuracy on MindCube. These results show that connecting frame-level object grounding through a shared 3D map can improve complex spatial reasoning without task-specific training.

</details>

Abstract (英文): In the era of large-scale pretrained models, zero-shot generalization has increasingly blurred the boundary between known and unknown concepts. However, current evaluations do not directly reveal which open-world concepts different vision-language models can recognize. To study this problem, we introduce OODConceptBench, a hierarchical benchmark for open-world concept recognition on image-concept pairs. The full benchmark set (L1) contains 30,752 images, and the harder subset (L2) contains 14,285 images. The benchmark covers 560 concept phrases across seven open-world categories. We evaluate 37 vision-language models across release year, model family, and variant. We obtain three main findings. First, target-concept recognition is lower on L2 than on L1 across all 37 evaluated models. Second, later-released models do not necessarily achieve stronger open-world concept recognition. Third, an increase in overall recognition does not imply consistent improvement across all benchmark categories. Together, these results show that OOD remains a challenge even in the era of large-scale vision-language pretraining.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Video world models are expected to generate videos that accurately follow specified camera trajectories while maintaining consistent 3D structures. However, existing models are predominantly trained on densely sampled videos with small inter-frame camera motions. When applied to sparse, large step trajectories, they often produce frozen frames, inaccurate camera motion, and severe geometric drift. We attribute this degradation to a mismatch between the gradual viewpoint transitions seen during training and the large viewpoint changes required at inference time. To address this problem, we propose Dense Trajectory Teacher Distillation (DTTD), a post-training framework that transfers a model's generation capability from dense trajectories to large camera motions. Given a sparse trajectory, DTTD interpolates additional camera poses to construct a dense trajectory covering the same path. The frozen base model then generates reliable teacher videos along this familiar dense trajectory, and pose aligned teacher observations are used to evaluate candidate videos generated under the original sparse trajectory. In this way, the model serves as its own generation teacher, requiring neither additional video data nor a stronger external video generator. We further combine dense teacher supervision with geometry aware rewards that assess cross frame consistency and camera trajectory following, and use the resulting preferences for offline preference optimization. To stably expand the model's motion range, we introduce a cumulative curriculum that progressively incorporates larger motion steps while retaining simpler trajectories to reduce capability forgetting.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Existing methods for CAD reconstruction from 3D scans achieve strong performance on standard benchmarks, yet their effectiveness often degrades in real-world scenarios where objects consist of multiple parts and scan artifacts such as noise. A key reason is that current CAD reconstruction datasets predominantly contain single-part models and clean geometric inputs, limiting the ability of learned models to generalize to realistic settings. In this work, we introduce CADonatello, a part-aware framework for reconstructing parametric CAD models from real-world 3D scans. Our approach first decomposes the input geometry into structurally meaningful components using a foundation-scale 3D part segmentation model, and then reconstructs each component independently through a cross-section-driven pipeline that performs sketch parameterization followed by an extrusion. To improve the robustness of sketch parameterization to scan-derived cross-sections, we further introduce CC3D Cross-Sections, a dataset of paired rasterized cross-section images from realistic scans and their corresponding parametric sketches derived from aligned CAD models. This dataset enables effective domain adaptation from clean CAD sketch images to scan-derived cross-section images. Extensive experiments demonstrate that the proposed approach improves generalization to noisy scans and enables reliable reconstruction of complex multi-part objects across multiple real-world CAD reverse engineering benchmarks.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Serial electron microscopy (EM) is essential for large-volume biological imaging and connectomics, yet its throughput remains a major bottleneck, with cubic-millimeter-scale brain imaging requiring months or years. Existing deep learning-based EM super-resolution methods are limited to small upscaling factors or rely on synthetic data, offering modest acceleration and limited practical impact. We introduce ZoomSEM, a deep learning-based super-resolution method to dramatically accelerate SEM imaging. Trained on real low- and high-resolution scanning electron microscopy (SEM) pairs, ZoomSEM enables reliable recovery of 4$\times$, 8$\times$, and up to 16$\times$ higher-resolution images from fast low-resolution scans while preserving fine neuronal structures critical for connectomic analysis, achieving up to 30-fold effective acceleration. Extensive experiments across datasets of diverse biological samples demonstrate robust generalization under realistic low-resolution acquisition. This advance enables high-resolution, large-volume serial EM imaging at unprecedented speed, poised to transform connectomics and related fields.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Megapixel visual synthesis with latent diffusion models requires operating far beyond the resolutions at which these models are typically trained. Cascade refinement is a leading strategy for this setting: it starts from a base image at a native resolution and progressively upsamples it to the target resolution through multiple refinement stages. Each stage typically performs a pixel-space resolution transition followed by latent-space denoising. While prior acceleration efforts mainly focus on reducing denoising cost, we identify a complementary and underexplored bottleneck: the repeated movement of representations through the VAE. We call these operations pixel-space transitions. In cascade pipelines, intermediate latents are often decoded to RGB, resized, and re-encoded before refinement, while final latents are decoded patch by patch with dense overlap to suppress boundary artifacts. We propose UltraFlash, a transition-efficient approach that accelerates these VAE-heavy operations. UltraFlash has two components. Latent Hyperloop replaces intermediate RGB round trips with a learned latent-space shortcut that approximates the decode-resize-encode transformation without repeated VAE decoding and encoding. Sparse Patch-based Decoding accelerates final reconstruction by addressing patch seams caused by inconsistent GroupNorm statistics and reusing cached statistics across patches, enabling sparse VAE decoding with much smaller overlap. Integrated into a state-of-the-art cascade baseline, UltraFlash reduces 4K generation latency by approximately 2x while improving image quality. These results show that optimizing pixel-space transitions can improve both efficiency and fidelity, offering an effective route toward fast, high-quality megapixel visual synthesis.

</details>

Abstract (英文): While interactive video world models require autoregressive generation, directly adapting pretrained bidirectional video diffusion models into autoregressive ones with teacher forcing can disrupt their visual priors and degrade autoregressive generation quality. In this paper, we introduce Mix Forcing, a causal pre-training paradigm for interactive video world models, implemented with the Mixture-of-Bidirectional-and-Autoregressive (MoBA) attention. Mix Forcing jointly trains a video diffusion model with both teacher forcing and bidirectional training, yielding a strong causal initialization for few-step post-training. In self-attention, Mix Forcing leverages teacher forcing to learn causal next-chunk prediction for autoregressive rollout, while using bidirectional attention to preserve pretrained visual priors. To support online text interaction, Mix Forcing further adopts a chunk-wise cross-attention design that conditions each video chunk on a shared environment prompt and its own local prompt, without exposing future instructions to the current chunk. Following causal pre-training on a 14B image-to-video model, we apply consistency and distribution matching distillation to obtain a four-step autoregressive generator. Experiments on our internal benchmark and public benchmarks, including WBench and SANA-WM-Bench, show that our model achieves state-of-the-art visual quality and timely event following with precise camera control. Notably, our model supports high-fidelity long-horizon generation without quality degradation even over one-hour rollouts. Project page is here: https://zelingao98.github.io/mixforcing

</details>

Abstract (英文): Before every neutral objective in professional League of Legends, each team decides which of its five players walk to the pit, which hold a lane, and when to commit. The leading analysis of such setups, TAGNet for Counter-Strike 2, clusters graph-network embeddings of player positions and reads cluster win rates as tactical quality. We treat a setup as an allocation on a walled map: formations are set partitions on the lattice $Π_5$, control is a geodesic Voronoi split of a navigation graph, and commitment is an allocation game solved as a quantal response equilibrium priced in win probability. On all 164 games of MSI and Worlds 2025 (2,994 team-setups, minimap tracks validated against Riot positions), trailing teams leave the pit empty far more often than leading teams, by +34.8 percentage points at the first baron and +30.4 percentage points at Atakhan. Our model, HASSE-QRE, finds six setup patterns that whole-model series-bootstrap refits reproduce (ARI 0.819, comparable to 0.813 for TAGNet's $K$-Means step alone) and indicates that teams price fights, travel and committed players, not the objective itself (weight 0.018; fixing it to one costs 59.9 log-likelihood points). The equilibrium reproduces a quarter of the commitment gap, almost all of it through state variables rather than its strategic channels. Weighting a team-level contrast by exposure moves it across $p=0.05$ (0.046 against 0.052). We release the tracks, setups and code.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Identity-document portraits are modified during document production by printing, engraving, and integration into the document template. These transformations may change image texture, contrast, colour, and other visual properties without necessarily changing the underlying biometric or presentation information. In this work, we introduce ONOT/TONO-Print, a small paired benchmark for evaluating face-image quality models under identity-document production shift. The benchmark is based on ONOT and TONO portraits and contains synthetic variants representing five common production techniques: toner, inkjet, dye sublimation, laser engraving, and thermal transfer. A smaller physical subset contains portraits printed and scanned using inkjet and toner devices. The benchmark is used to compare the behaviour of biometric and ICAO-related face-quality components before and after production. Results show that production can introduce substantial domain shift for some quality estimators, while other models remain comparatively stable.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Pavement crack segmentation under low light is degraded by reduced contrast, shadows, headlight glare, joints, stains, and wet seams. Existing crack segmentation methods mostly assume good illumination. Low-light enhancement methods improve visual quality, but they do not explicitly preserve crack topology. To address these limitations, we formulate low-light crack segmentation as a degradation-aware, prompt-grounded structural defect segmentation problem and propose DROA-CLIPSeg, a parameter-efficient framework that adapts a frozen CLIPSeg vision language backbone using two lightweight modules. QKV Low-Rank Adaptation is injected into the Q/K/V projections of the vision and text encoders to improve crack-specific image text alignment without backbone fine-tuning. The proposed Domain Residual Orthogonal Adapter (DROA) decomposes intermediate features into a dominant crack-domain branch, which captures recurring low-light crack patterns, and an orthogonal residual branch, which preserves faint, thin, and fragmented evidence usually suppressed by noise, shadow, or over-smoothing. We also introduce CrackLingo-9K and CrackLingo-12K, text augmented versions of Crack9k and Crack12k with crack-aware prompts. Training only approximately 1.93 M parameters, corresponding to 1.3% of the full model, DROA-CLIPSeg outperforms 9 zero-shot and 4 fine-tuned vision language baselines on both splits, generalises zero-shot to MVTec AD across textures and defect morphologies, and is supported by extensive ablations. Our source code is publicly available at https://github.com/VShuklacode/DROA-CLIPSeg

</details>

Abstract (英文): Pedestrian Attribute Recognition (PAR) predicts semantic attributes of individuals, such as gender, age, clothing and accessories, and its descriptions support attribute-based person retrieval and person re-identification. However, accurately recognizing fine-grained attributes across domains remains challenging due to occlusion, appearance variations, and shifts in image distributions. Ensembles of independently trained foundation encoders combine their logits with fixed fusion weights, so every attribute uses the same mix and the encoders are never trained together. We propose UniQuery, which instead jointly fine-tunes DINOv3 and CLIP for attribute-specific fusion. It combines two query heads, one for each encoder, with a joint query head whose attribute queries attend to tokens from both encoders, and the prediction is the average of the logits of the three query heads. We evaluate UniQuery on the benchmark of the UPAR Challenge 2027 at the WACV 2027 Real-World Surveillance workshop, where models are trained on PA100k, PETA and Market-1501 with a shared set of attributes. In leave-one-dataset-out experiments, UniQuery outperforms the independent ensemble in Challenge_Avg on both held-out datasets. Ablations show that learning fusion over independently trained encoders does not improve upon logit averaging in the mean over the two held-out datasets, while freezing the encoders lowers the Challenge_Avg of UniQuery. On the hidden test set of the benchmark, UniQuery achieves a Challenge_Avg of 0.8001.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Attribute-based person retrieval ranks surveillance images from a binary query of semantic attributes, but mean Average Attribute Degree of Match (mADM) drops on unseen cameras as predicted probabilities shift. Common pipelines rank gallery images by their L1 distance to the query, which penalizes a confident single-attribute mismatch no more than small uncertainties across many attributes, while post-hoc recalibration fitted on source validation data fails to transfer because the per-attribute maps fitted on source data do not match those of new cameras. We propose LENS (Likelihood Evaluation and Neighbor Smoothing), combining fine-tuned DINOv3 and CLIP models with a distilled student. LENS regularizes mutually exclusive attribute groups with group cross-entropy in training, scores gallery images by the log-likelihood of the exact query, and smooths gallery logits over nearest neighbors using DINOv3 appearance features from the same forward pass. On held-out datasets, all three changes raise mADM: the likelihood score improves all six retrieval metrics for the main ensemble and mADM for all 48 development models, and training-free kNN smoothing improves all 46 tested development models. On the hidden test set of the UPAR 2027 benchmark, LENS reaches 68.17 mADM.

</details>

Abstract (英文): GUI grounding requires locating small controls in cluttered screenshots and identifying which control a natural-language instruction refers to. Cropping and enlargement can expose local detail, but the resulting image may still leave ambiguity about the intended target. We propose PreView, an auxiliary preprocessor that improves the inputs to an existing grounding model without further training that model. PreView prepares two complementary types of input features: task-relevant views for localization and candidate-specific cues for target understanding. A trained lightweight auxiliary model predicts and refines a center, crops the screenshot into a local view, and enlarges that view for zoomed-in grounding. It also prepares a visual cue (i.e., a red-circle marker with a location hint) and a textual cue describing the candidate's identity or function. The frozen grounding model uses the prepared inputs to predict the final click. Across five benchmarks, PreView improves the average grounding accuracy of Qwen3-VL-2B, OS-Atlas-4B, and Qwen3-VL-4B without further training these grounding models. Extensive analyses examine the contributions of views and cues, center refinement, and visual input budgets.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Reconstructing a coherent 3D scene from uncurated image collections remains challenging due to the presence of irrelevant or non-overlapping views. Recent work has shown that feed-forward reconstruction models contain internal view-consistency signals that support distractor rejection. However, reference-conditioned filtering relies on a curated clean reference, which is not guaranteed in practical image collections. To address this limitation, we introduce FreeView, a training-free framework that jointly selects a filtering reference and rejects distractor views from an uncurated collection. FreeView leverages collection-level consensus to identify a representative view that serves as a reliable reference for filtering. It then adapts view rejection to each input collection by combining complementary visual and geometric evidence. This approach preserves useful views under substantial appearance and viewpoint variations while rejecting distractors. FreeView integrates with both VGGT and pi^3, removing the need for a manually curated reference or manual calibration of view rejection. Extensive experiments across multiple datasets demonstrate high clean-view retention and distractor rejection rates, leading to reliable 3D reconstruction from uncurated views.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Continuous physiological monitoring is essential for linking behavior, internal state, and neural activity in neuroscience and translational research. In common marmosets, a key nonhuman primate model for social neuroscience, respiratory rate provides a basic readout of arousal, stress, and physiological regulation. Contact sensors can provide accurate measurements, but they add preparation burden and may interfere with head-free behavioral protocols. Thermal video offers a noncontact alternative by capturing breathing-related temperature changes around the nose and mouth. However, in head-free marmosets, these cues are weak, localized, and easily corrupted by head motion, pose changes, and fur. Here we show that this setting exposes a broader challenge in video-based physiological sensing: visually reliable tracking does not necessarily imply physiologically valid measurement. Using synchronized thermal video and contact respiration recordings, we show that marmoset respiration is recoverable from thermal video but strongly ROI-sensitive. Under head-free motion, visually plausible nasal tracking can still produce traces inconsistent with contact respiration, demonstrating that tracking is not sensing. We therefore estimate respiratory rate from learned temporal signals extracted from thermal clips and introduce Selective Temporal Attenuation Regularization (STAR). STAR selectively attenuates high-motion thermal discontinuities through local temporal imputation during training, regularizing models toward respiration-consistent dynamics while leaving inference unchanged on raw video. Across multiple deep-learning backbones, STAR improves head-free estimation, reaching 2.23 bpm MAE compared with 20.76 bpm for tracked ROI analysis.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Visual token pruning substantially reduces the inference cost of multimodal large language models (MLLMs), yet existing methods are mostly training-free and plug-and-play. They treat the resulting performance drop as an inevitable cost of compression and pursue increasingly complex pruning rules. We rethink this view through two questions: how much of the loss can post-training rescue, and, building on this recovery, how can joint compression push pruning to its limit? We curate a 282K-sample post-training mixture covering general VQA, reasoning, OCR, and grounding, and post-train 36 configurations that drop or merge tokens under a unified protocol. Post-training rescues most of the loss, even for OCR and grounding, and its adaptation gain in closing the gap between the pruned and the dense model grows as the budget tightens. For joint compression, we find that the vision encoder, not the LLM, dominates prefill time in native-resolution MLLMs, so where tokens are removed matters more than how many: keeping 40% of tokens at the encoder runs faster than keeping 10% at the projector. Our best configuration prunes the encoder lightly and the projector aggressively, leaves the LLM intact, and keeps general understanding and reasoning at the dense level. It accelerates the LLM stage by 3.49x but the end-to-end time-to-first-token by only 1.54x, showing that LLM-stage speedups overstate end-to-end gains. We hope these findings offer insights for future research on joint visual token compression. Our data, protocol, and code will be released.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Whole Slide Image (WSI) analysis serves as the fundamental method for the clinical diagnosis of cancer in digital histopathology. Recent advances in Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have enabled state-of-the-art (SOTA) automated histopathology report generation. However, MLLMs reason purely through distributions encoded in their neural parameters, limiting the amount of knowledge and reasoning logic they can contain given the model scale. Their black-box reasoning poses another challenge to the human interpretation of intermediate reasoning. We address these issues by utilizing knowledge graphs to inject external structured knowledge for MLLMs to reason over, while making this reasoning component visible. Knowledge graphs are capable of partially externalizing the containment of medical knowledge, an area that remains underexplored in pathology report generation. We introduce PathKGen, a knowledge graph-assisted MLLM for histopathology report generation. PathKGen has two main branches: (1) a visual path that learns visual token inputs to the MLLM using pathology foundation models, and (2) a knowledge path that learns knowledge token inputs by extracting key morphological features of WSIs through querying a pathology-specific knowledge graph. A comprehensive evaluation on WSI-Bench dataset shows that PathKGen surpasses previous SOTA methods while revealing diagnostic reasoning as visually intuitive graphs. Due to its modular design, the proposed knowledge path is complementary to vision-aligned MLLMs and can be extended to other applications by curating domain-specific knowledge graphs to generate knowledge tokens.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Unrolled variational networks achieve state-of-the-art performance for accelerated MRI reconstruction, but standard end-to-end training becomes prohibitively memory intensive as the number and width of cascades increase. We propose a memory-efficient training framework that updates a randomly sampled cascade while retaining supervision from the final reconstruction loss. The error signal is propagated from the end of the chain to the sampled cascade using an activation-light backward pass that avoids storing full chain activations, thereby decoupling training memory from chain length. A time-conditioned network shared across cascades further keeps the parameter count independent of chain length. The backward pass is approximate only through frozen normalization statistics, and a trained exact gradient control shows the approximation leaves reconstruction quality unchanged. The framework remains trainable at chain lengths and cascade widths where standard end-to-end backpropagation exhausts GPU memory. On fastMRI single-coil knee and multi-coil brain reconstruction, our method consistently outperforms other local training approaches. On high-SNR knee slices, our method surpasses E2E-VarNet at $8\times$ and matches it at $4\times$, while on the complete brain validation set it surpasses E2E-VarNet at $8\times$ and achieves comparable performance at $4\times$, with fewer parameters, lower peak memory, and faster per-iteration training. Code is available at https://github.com/emehillaj/scalable-unrolled-mri.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Depth boundaries remain a major source of error in stereo matching. For IGEV on Scene Flow, geometric edge pixels account for only 6% of evaluated pixels but about half of the total absolute disparity error. Photometric edges can respond to texture changes without corresponding depth discontinuities, making them unreliable cues for boundary refinement. We propose E3Stereo, which learns geometric edges from disparity supervision and combines these boundary cues with the backbone disparity and image details to predict full-resolution disparity corrections. The same edge map also emphasizes boundary errors during training, while joint optimization allows the edge predictor to adapt to disparity refinement. On the Scene Flow dataset, E3Stereo reduces end-point error (EPE) at depth boundaries by 9.5%-14.3% across RAFT-Stereo, IGEV, and MonSter, while also reducing overall EPE. After fine-tuning, our IGEV-based model improves upon IGEV on all eleven reported metrics across ETH3D, KITTI 2012, and KITTI 2015. The source code will be made publicly available upon acceptance.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Driver emotion provides an important indication of how drivers perceive and evaluate ongoing traffic situations, making reliable emotion recognition an important capability for effective human-vehicle interaction. Yet, most existing driver emotion recognition approaches rely primarily on in-cabin images and overlook how surrounding traffic interactions shape driver emotional responses. To address this limitation, we propose a traffic-context-aware driver emotion recognition (TADER) framework that integrates in-cabin signals with out-of-cabin traffic scenarios, modeling driver emotions as arising from expectation deviation represented by the discrepancy between predicted and observed vehicle trajectories. The proposed framework comprises two key components: (1) a vision-based base recognizer that extracts spatiotemporal representations from in-cabin facial images to infer driver emotions, and (2) a traffic-context-aware fusion module that quantifies expectation deviation from external traffic scenarios and integrates it into the base recognizer via attention-based embedding fusion. To overcome the scarcity of paired in-cabin and out-of-cabin data, we introduce a transfer learning strategy that transfers a trajectory prediction model trained on large-scale naturalistic datasets to smaller paired datasets. Extensive experiments demonstrate that TADER consistently outperforms state-of-the-art methods across both naturalistic and simulated driving environments by incorporating traffic context information into emotion recognition, resulting in improvements of 5% in accuracy on the AIDE and manD datasets.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Daylight compliance assessment in residential buildings requires time-consuming raytracing simulations and specialist model preparation, limiting its integration into iterative architectural design. We present a machine learning-based methodology for predicting the spatial distribution of the Daylight Factor (DF) directly within Building Information Modelling (BIM) environments, delivering results in seconds without model export or manual curation. The approach addresses five gaps not jointly covered by any existing model: external obstruction encoding including balconies, own building height, variable material reflectances, non-orthogonal room geometries, and regulatory validation. Two models are developed: a default-materials model with fixed reflectances for preliminary design checks, and a custom-materials model with variable project-specific reflectances. Both are trained on about 20,000 synthetic Radiance-simulated rooms and validated on a held-out synthetic dataset and 102 rooms from 18 real building models. The default-materials model achieves $δ_1$ = 83.4% and MAE = 0.11% DF on synthetic data, and on real buildings predicts BBR (Swedish) compliance area within 4.85 pp MAE (bias +2.31 pp) and TEK17 (Norwegian) mean room DF within 0.304% DF MAE (bias +0.287% DF). The custom-materials variant shows 1.7-2.4$\times$ higher error across all metrics. Both models concentrate accuracy in the BBR compliance-critical zone (DF < 1%), achieving a sub-1% DF MAE of 0.105% DF and Compliance IoU of 0.883 on real buildings. All cases where compliance is overestimated are borderline, within 10.7 pp of the BBR threshold (median 3.2 pp) and 0.36% DF of the TEK17 threshold (median 0.10% DF). The default-materials model is recommended as a replacement for preliminary daylight simulations; the custom-materials variant requires further development before use in final compliance verification.

</details>

Abstract (英文): This work aims to assess the capabilities of the reinforcement learning algorithm, Proximal Policy Optimization (PPO), in developing a policy capable of controlling the speed and steering angle of a vehicle model to navigate within simulated road lines.
  The control system's input is a camera, and its outputs are the rotational speed of the rear wheels and the steering angle of the front wheels, controlling the vehicle's speed and direction, respectively. The algorithm is trained and tested in a simulated environment, where it receives image frames from the vehicle's camera, processes them through a feature extraction algorithm, and feeds them into the policy. For each frame, the policy outputs speed and direction commands for the vehicle. The policy is iteratively updated to enhance its performance.
  The reinforcement learning algorithm successfully generates a policy capable of navigating the vehicle in a simplified environment. Potential improvements are suggested to enhance the system's robustness in more complex scenarios.
  Creating a simulation environment suitable for testing an advanced reinforcement learning algorithm is a complex task. This project offers a framework for other developers to test different approaches and algorithms. The tested algorithm, PPO, uses proximity intervals - an innovative approach in reinforcement learning. In some scenarios, the algorithm achieved better performance than predecessor algorithms, which are typically harder to implement and require tuning more hyperparameters.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Road infrastructure perception is a fundamental component of autonomous driving, as it recovers the structured road elements that define roadway geometry, delimit operational space, and encode traffic regulations. Although individual tasks such as lane detection, drivable-space estimation, traffic-sign recognition, and online map construction have been extensively studied, the field remains fragmented across task definitions, sensing assumptions, output representations, datasets, and evaluation protocols. This survey provides a function-oriented synthesis of road infrastructure perception. We first clarify its scope and organize existing studies into three complementary categories: geometric-structural, functional-spatial, and regulatory-semantic infrastructure. Based on this taxonomy, we review sensing modalities, scene representations, representative methods, benchmarks, and evaluation protocols, emphasizing the field's evolution from image-space recognition toward metric, vectorized, topology-aware, occupancy-based, and rule-grounded scene modeling. We further examine how representation choices affect evaluation and downstream usability, revealing limitations of current benchmarks in relation reasoning, temporal consistency, robustness, and planning-oriented assessment. Finally, we discuss open challenges and future directions toward unified infrastructure representation, open-environment generalization, relation- and rule-aware reasoning, and downstream-oriented evaluation. By connecting tasks, representations, and evaluation under a common functional framework, this survey aims to establish road infrastructure perception as a coherent foundation for map-compatible and behavior-relevant autonomous-driving scene understanding.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Simulation is widely used in autonomous-driving validation, but matching task outputs between real and synthetic images does not guarantee that a system relies on the same visual evidence. This creates a validation gap for transfer, especially in safety-critical perception functions. We present Concept-Decisive Feature Fidelity (C-DFF), a concept-level mechanism-parity diagnostic for paired real-synthetic evaluation. Starting from decisive evidence extracted from a frozen system-under-test (SUT), C-DFF projects that evidence into a shared semantic concept space using off-the-shelf segmentation and compares real and synthetic concept-importance vectors. The result is not only a scalar mechanism-parity score, but also a concept-shift diagnostic that identifies which scene concepts become more or less decisive in simulation. We evaluate C-DFF on KITTI $\leftrightarrow$ Virtual KITTI 2 with 2126 matched pairs across three frozen SUTs (steering regression, YOLOP drivable-area, YOLOP lane-line). Across these tasks, C-DFF is SUT-discriminative and yields interpretable concept-level summaries that support validation triage and simulator debugging. When reported alongside task-specific output-value context metrics, C-DFF shows that output agreement does not by itself establish mechanism parity. These results position C-DFF as a practical semantic diagnostic layer for validation and verification workflows in simulation-based AV testing.

</details>

Abstract (英文): We present a principled, analytical framework for segmenting multiple independently moving rigid objects during single camera motion, built directly upon the OWL perception function. OWL encodes two fundamental visual motion cues, perceived looming $L$ and perceived rotation $ω$, into a single closed-form representation of relative 3D structure. A key consequence of this structure is that the unit direction of relative translation $\hat{t}$ is analytically recoverable per image point from $L$ and $ω$ alone. All points belonging to a non-rotating rigid body share the same $\hat{t}$, whereas points on different rigid bodies generally yield distinct values. This furnishes a natural segmentation criterion: clustering per-point $\hat{t}$ estimates partitions the visible scene into independently moving rigid bodies. Building on the 2D complex form of OWL, we derive the framework in 3D using quaternions, for a single moving camera. The approach is parallel, camera rotation-invariant, and requires no training data, no depth information, and no semantic priors.

</details>

Abstract (英文): An image is rarely described only once. Retrieval benchmarks and many collections attach several captions to each picture, yet dual encoders still judge every image-caption pair in isolation. We ask what these neighboring captions contribute when a frozen encoder consults them at inference time. Fusing image similarity with similarity to an image's own captions improves text-to-image R@1 by 3.4 to 16.8 points across nine frozen encoders when the captions come from the same annotators as the queries. The gain falls to 1.9 to 7.2 points when an off-the-shelf captioner writes them, and to at most one point when they come from a different annotation source; a paraphrase-trained text encoder strengthens the text side further. A natural refinement, scoring the part of the image that the captions leave unexplained, appears to add much more under a leave-one-out evaluation. We show that this advantage arises because the correct candidate's supports are built differently from those of the negative candidates, with set size and query removal each contributing about half, and that under a fixed support index the residual reduces to a reweighting of what the captions already provide. On compositional benchmarks, the captions help by matching candidates to human descriptions of the image, without improving image-text grounding. The code is available at: https://github.com/nhatkhangcs/clip_consensus

</details>

Abstract (英文): Feed-forward 3D foundation models have recently reshaped multi-view reconstruction, yet dynamic 4D understanding still often relies on large-scale motion supervision or hand-crafted, architecture-specific heuristics. We ask whether frozen 3D foundation model tokens already encode motion-relevant information that can be exposed by lightweight readout functions, rather than re-learning motion-specific features with high-capacity decoders. Through probing on three representative backbones, we find that static--dynamic separation is decodable from frozen token representations. Our rank-restricted and spectral analyses on $π^3$X reveal that the recovered motion cues are concentrated in a low-dimensional subspace. Building on this, we propose Probing-based Motion Distillation (PMD), which converts pairwise motion-state compatibility scores against a fixed anchor token into dense motion masks. Trained on only 160 YouTube-VOS videos using a single 16 GB GPU, PMD transfers zero-shot to DAVIS, SegTrackv2, and FBMS-59 without target-benchmark fine-tuning. With multi-view pretrained geometric backbones, PMD outperforms training-free heuristics and approaches substantially heavier supervised and self-supervised methods, suggesting that motion decoding from frozen 3D foundation models can be lightweight and data-efficient.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Open-ended image-editing requests, such as ``decorate this room,'' admit multiple valid realizations. Direct sampling may produce ungrounded variation, whereas explicit diversity prompting often produces superficial differences. We study how to ground these requests without prematurely eliminating their legitimate ambiguity. We introduce a two-stage framework that first identifies diverse, feasible edit scopes and then realizes them as executable edit instructions. Across 300 open-ended image-editing tasks, our approach achieves 24.6% higher mean semantic dispersion and 17.1% higher mean groundedness than direct diversity prompting, while producing more valid and distinct realizations. These results show that structuring feasible edit directions before realization supports more useful grounded exploration.

</details>

Abstract (英文): When an image and its caption disagree, can a user reliably control which one a vision-language model (VLM) follows? Accuracy alone cannot answer this question, since a model might give the correct answer from parametric knowledge without following the requested evidence. We therefore evaluate modality grounding control through conflict-preserving counterfactual interventions. Specifically, Flip measures whether an initially correct answer follows an edit to the requested modality, while Stay measures whether it remains stable when the competing modality is edited. To learn this control, we build on neologism learning by introducing new words for visual and textual grounding, training only their embeddings while keeping the pretrained model fixed. We also introduce Counterfactual Twin-Margin (CTM) supervision, which uses paired interventions to teach how answer probabilities should change when the requested evidence changes. Across three VLMs on three existing benchmarks and our multi-attribute Ground-CF benchmark, we find that learned modality words improve control over natural-language instructions. Surprisingly, a single learned embedding per modality provides strong lightweight visual control and shows little average degradation on held-out values. LoRA remains strongest overall, although its visual advantage narrows under held-out evaluation. Moreover, counterfactual supervision improves control in several settings where standard supervision is weaker. Finally, we test our method on real-world news video and find that visual control improves when the relevant evidence is made easier to access. Overall, our results show that reliable modality control requires testing not only whether an answer is correct, but whether it continues to follow edits to the modality of choice.

</details>

Abstract (英文): We introduce TrackTok, an efficient video tokenization framework that produces object-centric tokens, i.e., tokens that consistently encode the same semantic objects across time, even as it moves, deforms, or undergoes partial occlusion. Conventional patch-based or existing holistic tokenizers produce token identities often tied to fixed image locations. In contrast, our approach learns temporally persistent object-centric representations that bind visual features across frames into stable semantic units. This enables substantially more compact, continuous latent video representations, reducing the number of tokens required for downstream generative modeling with state-of-the-art flow models, while preserving the semantic structure needed for high-quality synthesis. Our tokenizer offers a favorable quality-efficiency trade-off. TrackTok achieves comparable class-conditional generative FVD on UCF-101 dataset at 8 times fewer latent tokens and better latency, and outperforms existing 1D tokenization baselines on frame prediction task on Kinetics-600 across low token budgets. These results suggest that object-aligned and temporally persistent tokenization is a promising direction for scalable video generation, where reducing latent token counts is critical for efficient modeling. Our code for this work is available at https://github.com/phamleyennhi/tracktok.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Vision-language-action (VLA) models enable robots to follow instructions in typed text, limiting broader deployment in natural speech. The traditional solution by prepending a speech recognition before VLA to convert speech into texts would incur additional latency and propagate errors. To address this, we present Speech-$π_{0.5}$, which maps raw speech directly to motor commands without explicit textual transcriptions. Specifically, built on $π_{0.5}$, we use a cross-modal projection to convert voice frames directly into audio tokens with significant response latency reduction. To further improve the accuracy, we adopt split LoRA adaptation with dedicated audio and task adapters, under a two-stage training. Speech-$π_{0.5}$ achieves $3\times$ lower response latency than ASR pipeline while maintaining competitive task success on LIBERO, and generalizes robustly to unseen accents where a single adapter degrades significantly.

Abstract (英文): Language-aware 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) has advanced language-driven 3D scene understanding by incorporating semantic and relational cues into reconstructed scenes. However, relation-aware grounding depends not only on representing such information, but also on whether the underlying scene-side evidence is reliable and sufficient. Multi-view semantic aggregation can incorporate weak observations, while object relations can remain under-observed along the available camera trajectory, leading to ambiguous evidence for target disambiguation. We present RelateGS, a structured language-aware Gaussian representation that selectively refines semantic and relational evidence for 3D visual grounding. RelateGS suppresses unreliable observations during semantic feature uplifting and consolidates multi-view object observations into a persistent 3D graph. The graph further identifies under-observed object relations and guides targeted re-querying of informative existing frames to complement their evidence. Experiments on Sr3D+ and Nr3D demonstrate competitive 3D grounding performance, improving the previous best by 7.9 and 1.7 percentage points at Acc@0.25, respectively.

Abstract (英文): Catastrophic forgetting in continual learning (CL) manifests not only as accuracy degradation but also as \emph{explanation drift}: models maintain predictive accuracy while silently shifting attention from diagnostic features to spurious correlations. This work identifies and resolves a previously overlooked structural flaw in explanation-aware CL: pixel-level saliency regularization derives supervision targets from the model's own frozen saliency maps, creating a \emph{self-referential drift loop} that actively reinforces spurious attention across tasks. We introduce \textbf{C$^3$L} (Concept-Consistent Continual Learning), which breaks this loop by anchoring regularization to class-consistent semantic concepts (e.g., ``curved beak'', ``striped pattern'') discovered offline, independent of model state, providing (i) target independence from model drift, and (ii) cross-instance semantic consistency across every instance of a class. We identify that our concept-based objective, combined with approximate LRP relevance conservation, induces a \emph{crowding-out effect}: since concept and background regions compete for the relevance of the same prediction, enforcing high relevance within concept regions implicitly suppresses spurious background correlations without explicit penalization. \method does not require human-annotated concept sets, as it employs a fully automated concept discovery and spatial grounding. Extensive evaluation across five benchmarks and over 20 baselines demonstrates state-of-the-art performance in accuracy, forgetting, explanation quality, spurious correlation mitigation, and out-of-distribution robustness.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Interactive world models hold immense potential for high-stakes applications in robotics, game engines, and immersive digital twins, yet existing benchmarks primarily focus on perceptual similarity rather than the physical-causal coherence essential for real-world utility. We introduce WMEval, a comprehensive benchmark that evaluates interactive world models against Unity-based physical simulations. By leveraging Unity as a high-fidelity ground-truth oracle, we shift evaluation beyond perceptual visual assessment toward the objective state-based verification required for downstream applications. Spanning 9 diverse scenes, WMEval assesses action controllability, scene and character consistency, prompt adherence, kinematics, fluid mechanics, physical causality, and lighting. By executing matched action sequences in both the simulator and the world model, WMEval enables evaluation at both the perceptual level and the physical-causal level, using simulator-derived states as reference. Experiments on state-of-the-art models reveal significant room for improvement in dimensions that remain hidden by standard perceptual metrics but are vital for real-world deployments. Our code is available at: https://github.com/hao-ai-lab/WMEval.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Out-of-distribution (OOD) detection identifies inputs outside the training distribution. Rank-based analyses such as RankFeat and RankWeight suggest that OOD overconfidence is closely related to dominant low-rank components in feature and weight spaces. Since weight averaging operates directly in parameter space and improves model generalization, we consider it for OOD detection from a dominant-component perspective. Trainable Weight Averaging (TWA) assigns a single coefficient to each checkpoint, limiting independent control of dominant weight components. We extend TWA with leading rank-one weight components extracted by Singular Value Decomposition as correction directions, whose contributions are learned through the in-distribution classification objective. On ResNet50, our method improves over fine-tuning and standard TWA across the evaluated datasets with MSP and RankWeight, both with and without Outlier Exposure. Mahalanobis-based evaluation yields mixed results, indicating that the benefit depends on the OOD scoring protocol. Target-layer analyses with MSP and Energy favor correction near the classifier.

</details>

Abstract (英文): A common strategy for data augmentation is to apply style transfer on training images to enrich appearance variations. Recent approaches have combined style augmentation with random erasing by selectively replacing image subregions with style-transferred patches. However, existing methods rely on uniformly random region selection or fixed geometric constraints, which may fail to cover informative texture patterns and limit augmentation diversity. To address these limitations, we propose a texture-aware subregion style augmentation framework that prioritizes regions with strong texture responses for style replacement. Furthermore, we extend this framework with a hybrid region strategy that combines a texture-driven region with an auxiliary random region, implicitly enabling more diverse style perturbations. Experiments demonstrate that our approach achieves higher classification accuracy and improves robustness compared with existing style-based augmentation methods.

</details>

Abstract (英文): World action models have demonstrated promising performance in robot policy learning, yet their high inference latency remains a critical bottleneck for real-world deployment. Recent approaches have sought to improve inference efficiency by reducing test-time future-frame imagination or accelerating action prediction. However, these methods often leverage representations from all video world model layers without explicitly considering their varying relevance to action prediction, leading to redundant computation and suboptimal representation utilization. In this work, we propose TurboWAM, a fast world action model that efficiently aggregates structured world representations conditioned on action predictions. Specifically, TurboWAM introduces a structured world representation aggregation module that adaptively fuses representations from different world model layers according to their relevance to the evolving action denoising state. Furthermore, TurboWAM employs a structured action supervision mechanism that adaptively modulates gradients from action supervision across different world model layers during backward propagation, facilitating world representation learning. During inference, by reducing the number of world model layers executed and caching relevant representations across action denoising steps, TurboWAM substantially reduces computational overhead. Extensive experiments on multiple robot manipulation benchmarks demonstrate that TurboWAM achieves low inference latency while maintaining competitive task success rates compared with state-of-the-art methods.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Automated classification of gastrointestinal (GI) findings in endoscopic images could support early diagnosis, but architectures are often chosen on accuracy alone. We present a controlled, reproducible benchmark of ResNet50 and ViT-B/16 for eight-class GI classification on the public Kvasir dataset (4,000 images), using a stratified split (400 test images) and stratified group 5-fold cross-validation over all images. In cross-validation, ResNet50 and ViT-B/16 reached 92.12% and 91.70% accuracy, and a two one-sided tests (TOST) procedure showed equivalence within a +/-3 percentage-point margin (difference +0.43 points, 90% CI -0.33 to +1.18; p < 0.001). The architectures differed mainly in cost: ResNet50 had 3.6 times fewer parameters and 4.3 times fewer operations, 2.5-fold lower single-image CPU latency (69 vs. 177 ms) and 3.8-fold higher GPU throughput. Cross-validated calibration was similar (expected calibration error 0.021 vs. 0.023). An edge-oriented transformer, MobileViT-S, was most accurate on the test split (95.5%) but lost 9.4 points when an acquisition artefact present in some classes (the ScopeGuide inset) was occluded, whereas ResNet50 was not significantly affected. On 500 unseen polyp images with expert masks, both models' Grad-CAM maps focused on the lesion (3.6 times chance). On a de-duplicated external subset of GastroVision (1,998 images), accuracy fell to 78-80% for all three models and differences between them remained small. Accuracy differences between architectures were thus small relative to the effects of shortcut learning and domain shift, whereas efficiency differences were large. An efficient CNN is a defensible default for resource-constrained screening, but no model is deployment-ready without local validation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Deep learning facilitates rapid single shot three dimensional (3D) reconstruction using fringe projection profilometry (FPP), but recovering accurate surface height from a single high frequency fringe image remains challenging because both long-range spatial consistency and fine geometric details must be preserved. Convolutional networks are limited in modeling long range dependencies, whereas window-based Transformers establish global interactions only progressively with increasing depth. To address these limitations, a hybrid Mamba Transformer network is proposed for fringe to height regression. The network adopts a Swin based U shaped architecture and introduces visual state space modeling at the intermediate resolution encoder stage. An axial and dilated vision state space (ADVSS) block is further developed to enhance prescan feature encoding through local, dilated, and axial depthwise convolutions while retaining four directional selective scanning. This design combines linear complexity long range modeling with direction aware preservation of local fringe structures. Experiments on a publicly available FPP dataset show that the proposed method outperforms hNet, SwinConvUNet, UHRNet, and HiDNet in mean absolute error(MAE), root mean square error (RMSE), absolute relative error, and structural similarity index measure (SSIM). Using only one deformed fringe image, the proposed method achieves an RMSE of 0.548 mm and an SSIM of 0.9985, reducing RMSE by 65.2% and 59.6% relative to UHRNet and HiDNet, respectively. These results demonstrate the effectiveness of combining selective state space modeling with hierarchical window based attention for accurate single-shot FPP reconstruction.

</details>

Abstract (英文): As Vision-Language Models (VLMs) increasingly enable robots to follow language instructions, understanding their navigation decisions becomes important for reliable deployment. Existing explainability methods overlook a key challenge in Vision-Language Navigation (VLN): evidence for the current action may be distributed across historical and current observations and associated with different instruction stages. We identify instruction-stage insensitivity, where existing methods produce similar explanations despite changes across different navigation stages. To address this issue, we propose a stage-aware attribution framework for VLN. We first construct action-conditioned temporal instruction--visual attributions across observations. These attributions are projected into instruction space to estimate stage associations, which guide the aggregation of relevant historical evidence for refining the current explanation. An instruction-guided spatial filter further suppresses isolated responses while preserving compact, instruction-relevant regions. Experiments across multiple VLN policies and benchmarks show improved attribution quality over existing methods. We further demonstrate that our method acts as a diagnostic tool for analyzing failure cases, and validate its applicability to real-world navigation on a Unitree G1 robot.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Camera motion recognition requires separating viewpoint changes caused by camera translation, rotation, or zoom from object motion and scene dynamics. Video-language models often rely on semantic cues and struggle with the geometric evidence needed for this task. Joint-Embedding Predictive Architectures such as V-JEPA offer strong spatio-temporal representations, but our experiments suggest that they underrepresent geometric cues in some regions. Under temporal-reversal supervision, V-JEPA improves overall but degrades on several motion families, whereas a weaker geometry-oriented VGGT predictor improves across all families. We refer to these geometrically underrepresented regions as geometric blind spots and propose Fovea-JEPA to identify and address them. Using V-JEPA as the base model and VGGT as a geometry expert, we construct intervention pseudo-labels from their prediction errors to weakly supervise a blind-spot predictor. The predictor produces token-wise response maps, which are aggregated into a clip-level intervention score to guide adaptive fusion of visual and geometric features. This selective intervention introduces complementary geometric information while preserving reliable visual predictions. Experiments on CameraMotionDataset, CameraBench, and ShotBench demonstrate the effectiveness of Fovea-JEPA, with gains of 9.67, 8.67, and 24.38 percentage points over previously reported state-of-the-art baselines, respectively. Code and models will be released upon acceptance.

</details>

Abstract (英文): LVLM benchmarks tell us whether a model answers correctly, but not which visual conventions it has retained, nor where those conventions live. Sketches expose this hidden structure. A sketch removes texture, lighting, and photographic context while preserving the strokes humans judged sufficient for recognition. If an LVLM can operate on that abstraction, the surviving signal is structural rather than merely photometric. We introduce a training-free sketch probe with three readouts: temporal part-construction order, spatial attention-based grounding, and the mechanistic layer/head locus of grounding. We evaluate nine LVLMs on part order and five on the attention readouts, over two part-annotated sketch datasets. Three findings emerge. First, LVLM part order tracks human convention: models reproduce canonical orderings where human drawings converge, and become correspondingly variable where human orderings vary. Controls that blank, withhold, and mismatch the sketch locate this convention in the language model. Second, part-level grounding survives the abstraction: attention heads selected from the frozen model with an existing recipe recover part and object regions on object and scene sketches, with no grounding supervision. Third, the grounding signal concentrates in a narrow mid-network band whose location follows the base language model rather than parameter count in the two families we compare, and remains stable across object and scene sketches. The result is a practical diagnostic for sketch-driven LVLMs: no fine-tuning, no auxiliary segmenter, and a layer band that doubles as a head-selection prior, matching or improving grounding mAcc@0.1 in every model and dataset we tested.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Few-step distillation for autoregressive (AR) video generation commonly first initializes an AR student and then refines it with distribution matching distillation (DMD). This refinement process faces two challenges. First, although initialization already yields a reasonably capable student, DMD obtains the refinement direction indirectly by estimating the full student score before forming the teacher-student correction. Second, detached-history training prevents the generator from assigning credit to a current chunk for its influence on subsequent chunks. We introduce Leta, a few-step distillation framework that addresses these challenges with ScoreDelta and FutureCredit. ScoreDelta directly estimates the teacher-student correction from denoising residuals, while FutureCredit learns an auxiliary cross-chunk update signal from vector-Jacobian products (VJPs) of next-chunk teacher residuals. Leta combines these signals into a joint teacher-guided update field in a single forward pass during generator updates. Teacher evaluations and credit VJPs are used only for estimator training. For ScoreDelta, we establish population equivalence to score-difference estimation and a conditional approximation advantage under limited capacity in a fixed-feature linear model. In our controlled experiments, Leta improves over DMD by 0.9 points on VBench and 0.66 on VideoAlign. This advantage persists at a higher generator update frequency (a generator-to-estimator update ratio of G:E=1:1), where Leta improves over DMD by 1.6 on VBench and 0.35 on VideoAlign. Project page: https://scdzx.github.io/Leta/.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Open-vocabulary 3D occupancy prediction is important for embodied agents operating in complex environments whose semantic categories extend beyond fixed taxonomies. While recent studies have explored open-vocabulary occupancy in outdoor driving scenarios, indoor environments remain challenging due to dense occlusions, complex geometry, and fine-grained long-tailed semantics. To reduce the need for expensive voxel-level semantic annotations, we study a geometry-only supervision setting requiring only binary occupancy labels. We build upon Language-Embedded Gaussians as a unified representation coupling 3D geometry with language-aligned semantics. For geometry learning, we introduce an opacity-aware Poisson-based Gaussian-to-Occupancy formulation that enables stable volumetric aggregation under binary occupancy supervision. For semantic learning, we render Gaussian features into images and align them with a frozen open-vocabulary model, together with Progressive Temperature Decay to reduce feature mixing during Gaussian splatting. Building on this framework, we further introduce Instance-Level Semantic Consistency to alleviate fragmented object predictions, and Covisibility-Aware Support-View Selection to provide informative and less redundant multi-view semantic supervision. We also extend our framework to outdoor driving scenarios to evaluate its generality across substantially different environments. Extensive experiments on indoor and outdoor benchmarks demonstrate that our method achieves strong open-vocabulary occupancy performance, validating the effectiveness and generality of our framework. Code will be available at https://github.com/JuIvyy/LegoOcc.

Abstract (英文): Indoor IoT cameras can support assisted living and activity-aware spaces, but exporting raw RGB frames raises privacy concerns by exposing facial, clothing, and household details that body-motion tasks often do not require. We present StickFigure, a pose-only IoT camera system that, during real-time monitoring, keeps raw frames local and ephemeral while exporting only compact skeleton streams sufficient for downstream human activity recognition (HAR). While pose estimation has matured on GPUs, extracting accurate pose streams on resource-constrained IoT cameras remains difficult. To overcome these challenges and enable on-device pose streams on (almost) any IoT camera, we present two core designs in StickFigure: an activation-aware pose backbone that allocates channel capacity after spatial downsampling and a lightweight hierarchical MLP refiner that selectively corrects elbow and wrist coordinates using intermediate visual features and kinematic context. We fully implement StickFigure and evaluate it on public benchmarks and real-world deployment. Compared with state-of-the-art MCU pose models, StickFigure achieves the highest pose accuracy and downstream HAR utility on both NTU RGB+D and Penn Action, while using 96.5% fewer parameters and 99.3% fewer MACs per frame than RTMPose-S-based baselines, achieving 0.107 s p95 latency on a representative STM32H753 MCU. We believe StickFigure establishes a new operating point for privacy-aware edge vision on massive IoT cameras.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Fine-tuning adapts pretrained models to new tasks, but identifying which weight changes support the resulting behavior remains difficult. Weight-space interpretability assigns semantic descriptions to these changes, called fine-tuning deltas. Such descriptions leave open how much task performance depends on the identified updates, particularly when other updates can support the same predictions. We address this gap by pairing reversion, which restores selected parameters to their pretrained values, with retention, which keeps only selected fine-tuning updates. We evaluate Contrastive Language-Image Pretraining (CLIP) models on synthetic financial charts, Flowers102, EuroSAT, and FGVCAircraft with ViT-L/14, and on Flowers102 with ViT-B/16, using a shared recipe that tunes the final six visual blocks. Our contributions are threefold. First, attention value-output (VO) updates alone recover 43-87% of the accuracy improvement, although reverting them leaves chart accuracy unchanged. Cross-entropy loss preserves this contrast on Charts and EuroSAT, while correct-class score margins reveal additional contributions. Second, VO weights recover more loss improvement than all ten equally sized, layer-matched random subsets in each setting. VO and multilayer perceptron (MLP) retentions jointly correct 25-81% of pretrained errors, demonstrating overlapping recovered behavior, which we call operational redundancy. Third, removing semantically selected directions reduces VO-retained recovery more than matched-random removal in all five settings. Together, these results distinguish semantic alignment, removal cost, and retained recovery as complementary evidence about the functional role of fine-tuning updates within the evaluated CLIP recipe.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Optical character recognition (OCR) systems depend strongly on the scale and diversity of their training data. One approach to document OCR first analyzes the page layout and extracts text-line images, which are then transcribed by a recognition model. Multilingual line-level recognition involving Persian, Arabic, and English presents additional challenges because different writing directions, fonts, and styles may coexist within a single line. This paper introduces MuSTEL, a large-scale synthetic multilingual OCR dataset containing more than 14.5 million annotated text-line images, organized into a training partition and predefined small and large test sets. Samples are generated from natural-language corpora, including multilingual content already present in the source texts. The generation framework incorporates capability-aware font selection, within-line font and style changes, mixed-direction text rendering, highlighting, and underlining. A validated repository of 2,009 font files from 711 families supports broad typographic coverage, while varied backgrounds, blur, geometric distortion, skew, stroke width, and image save quality provide diverse document-like appearances. Statistical analysis characterizes the dataset's language composition, transcription lengths, lexical and character-sequence diversity, image dimensions, typography, and rendering conditions. MuSTEL provides a resource for training and evaluating multilingual line-level OCR systems and investigating recognition robustness across languages, writing directions, and visual conditions.

</details>
