Abstract (英文): While Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) excel at zero-shot reasoning, their spatial capabilities are primarily evaluated through static, passive observation. In complex, human-centric scenes, occlusion often hides crucial geometric evidence, and passive benchmarks force models to guess rather than recognize their own visual uncertainty. To bridge this gap, we introduce SATE-Bench, a novel interactive benchmark comprising 316 real-world scenes and 2,525 multi-turn QA pairs to evaluate visual self-awareness and active exocentric view-exploration. It combines a Passive multi-view control with an Active exploration setting, where models start from a severely occluded view and must iteratively query adjacent viewpoints until they gather enough evidence to fully ``sate'' the question, scored by a step-penalized efficiency reward. Evaluating state-of-the-art MLLMs reveals that they terminate exploration prematurely while reporting high confidence, and struggle with fine-grained 3D geometric tasks; matched-view controls further attribute the Active--Passive gap primarily to insufficient evidence acquisition. Moreover, fine-tuning with Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) shows that rewarding view-seeking increases active exploration but lowers overall accuracy, as models over-explore and fail to fuse expanding multi-view contexts across turns. These findings indicate that scaling passive multi-view perception does not intrinsically yield active spatial reasoning, highlighting the need for models that know where to look, how to integrate what they see, and when to stop. Code and data are available at https://gtc-gh.github.io/SATE-Bench/

</details>

Abstract (英文): Crowd barriers are widely used to regulate pedestrian movement during large public events such as pilgrimages. However, fixed barrier layouts often create unnecessary stopping and local congestion even when they successfully control the overall crowd flow. Finding better barrier configurations is difficult because there is no dataset that provides the best barrier placement for a given crowd scene. We address this challenge by introducing a simulation-driven reinforcement learning framework that learns barrier configurations without requiring labelled training data. Starting from a segmented crowd scene, we construct a realistic simulation environment that reproduces the observed pedestrian distribution. This environment is then used to train a Soft Actor-Critic (SAC) agent that continuously adjusts barrier positions to improve pedestrian movement. Unlike supervised approaches the proposed framework allows the agent to explore barrier configurations that have never been observed in real data. We evaluate the learned policy under unseen numbers of pedestrians. The learned policy consistently outperforms the existing manually designed barrier configuration by reducing pedestrian stops while maintaining effective flow regulation. These results demonstrate that combining computer vision crowd simulation and reinforcement learning provides an effective framework for optimizing crowd control infrastructure.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Instruction-based image editors built on diffusion transformers run tens of denoising steps. Training-free acceleration either reuses features across steps (step caching) or stops computing the regions an edit leaves untouched (region freezing), and the two have been combined before. We ask how a high speed-up target should be split between them. On Qwen-Image-2.1, freezing does more than remove tokens: it lowers the cost of every step, so the step cache can reach the same overall speed-up with a conservative threshold that keeps its error small. A per-step cost model fitted on development runs predicts held-out latency with a median error of 4.0% and reproduces the speed ceilings of the region methods we re-implemented (2.73$\times$ predicted vs. 2.70$\times$ measured for RegionE). Driving frozen tokens to the model's one-step $x_0$ estimate beats returning them to the reference image on every development sample, and in 90% of measured cases the composed error stays below the sum of the two individual errors. The resulting method, FreezePlan, keeps five full steps and gives the rest of the budget to TaylorSeer. On a pre-registered test split it runs 5.05$\times$ faster end to end at 29.8 dB PSNR to the original output, with no significant drop in a VLM judge score; TaylorSeer at 5.02$\times$ reaches 23.5 dB. The curve extends to 5.99$\times$ at 28.0 dB. Swapping in the region selectors of prior methods under our schedule lowers median PSNR by only 0.5--0.6 dB, which places the gain in the schedule and budget split rather than in the selector.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Open-vocabulary instance segmentation (OVIS) provides a promising solution for marine perception by recognizing and segmenting categories beyond predefined label spaces. Existing OVIS methods usually rely on vision-language alignment, where visual region features are matched with textual category embeddings to recognize both seen and unseen classes. However, this alignment assumption becomes fragile in underwater environments. Color attenuation, scattering, turbidity, and low contrast distort visual appearance, causing region features to deviate from their corresponding semantic concepts. As a result, the similarity between degraded visual features and textual classifiers becomes unreliable, which particularly weakens the recognition of unseen underwater categories that cannot be corrected by category-specific supervision. To address these issues, we propose GeoPrompt, a geometry-conditioned prompt learning framework for underwater open-vocabulary instance segmentation. Our key insight is that geometric structure is more stable than visual appearance in degraded underwater scenes. Specifically, GeoPrompt introduces a geometry-guided visual adaptation module (GVAM) to extract depth-derived structural priors and inject them into multi-scale visual features. It further develops a geometry-conditioned prompt learning (GCPL) that generates geometry-aware textual classifiers from structural representations. These geometry-aware classifiers are integrated with fixed and image-conditioned textual classifiers to improve open-vocabulary semantic alignment. Experiments on the MARIS-OpenIS benchmark show that GeoPrompt achieves state-of-the-art performance and brings consistent improvements on underwater open-vocabulary instance segmentation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): On-policy distillation (OPD) enables flow-matching models to acquire complementary capabilities from specialized teachers through supervision on student-generated trajectories. However, different evaluation dimensions, such as aesthetics and OCR accuracy, and different tasks, such as image generation and editing, emphasize different aspects of the denoising process, suggesting that their effective supervision may occur at different stages. Existing methods typically select supervision states from predefined timestep distributions, which may overlook these phase-dependent differences and incur unnecessary computational overhead. This motivates two critical timing decisions: when to adaptively query teachers for effective supervision based on the evolving generative process, and when to skip the remaining rollout to avoid redundant computation.
  In this paper, we introduce PhaseOPD, a phase-aware query-and-skip framework that identifies generative phases online from revisions of predicted-clean latents, using existing student velocity predictions without additional model evaluations. For each task-assigned teacher, PhaseOPD selects a supervision state based on its phase preference and skips the unused rollout suffix once all query decisions are finalized, preserving the single-query velocity-distillation objective. On SD3.5-M, PhaseOPD executes only 5.56 of 16 rollout positions per local batch, reducing training time by 41.3% relative to DanceOPD while improving Aesthetic, GenEval, and OCR scores.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Modern surgical AI increasingly requires multimodal understanding rather than isolated perception, yet existing datasets remain largely limited to single-task supervision such as recognition or templated question answering. Current medical VQA resources often rely on synthetic templates, lack natural descriptive supervision, or provide sparse annotations that hinder reasoning about spatial relations and procedural context. We introduce MIS-VQA, a surgical vision-language dataset generated through an annotation-driven pipeline that converts heterogeneous surgical annotations into structured textual scene representations. Using EndoVis18 and CholecT50, we transform phase, instrument, interaction, triplet, and segmentation labels into unified contexts that guide large language models to generate clinically coherent question-answer pairs. MIS-VQA supports four response types-concise and detailed descriptions, perception-level grounding, and multi-step reasoning-enabling hierarchical evaluation from atomic visual grounding to compositional reasoning. To address annotation sparsity, we incorporate confidence-filtered segmentation and cross-annotation consistency checks, enabling spatially grounded supervision at scale. MIS-VQA establishes a unified benchmark for surgical vision-language understanding with spatial supervision and multi-tier reasoning evaluation. To demonstrate its utility, we introduce LLaVA-SurgXpert, a family of surgical vision-language models with visual encoders to analyze how visual representations influence multimodal reasoning. Experiments show consistent improvements over existing surgical VQA baselines while revealing capability gaps between perceptual grounding and higher-level reasoning hidden in prior benchmarks.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Cross-modality medical image translation is challenged by anatomically inconsistent synthesis that can compromise clinical diagnosis. We propose StaX-Net, a Stackelberg interleaved asymmetric network that incorporates a partially coupled encoder-discriminator structure, and a 2D feature aggregation and spatial transformation (FAST) layer that distills target-supervised priors under the learning using privileged information paradigm. The shared blocks between the generator and discriminator enhance architectural feature alignment. To stabilize the leader-follower training, a Stackelberg-inspired asymmetric optimization scheme employs explicit shared-parameter freezing and asymmetric learning rates. The FAST layer distills target-domain priors during training and applies a source-only transform at inference. This constrains the synthesis process through target-informed feature representations relative to standard generative architectures. The code is available on GitHub for public use.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Spatial localization of surgical instruments through bounding-box annotations is a foundational component of surgical scene understanding, supporting detection, tracking, interaction analysis, and grounded vision-language applications. Yet existing annotations often cover selected frames, video subsets, or dataset variants rather than complete procedures. Manual extension is time-consuming and expert-dependent. Additionally, weakly supervised and detector-based pseudo-labels require task-specific training and may be imprecise. In this study, we design a scalable MLLM-based annotation framework that converts surgical frames and existing instrument-presence labels into accurate bounding boxes, and systematically analyze its reliability, cost, and design sensitivity. We instantiate the framework using six MLLMs and a multi-call configuration on laparoscopic cholecystectomy in Cholec80. Using a carefully designed stratification scheme, two annotators independently label 1,000 frames, and an expert surgeon reviews one annotation set to establish the reference. Results show that instrument-set F1 ranges from 88.2 to 92.7,whereas mIoU ranges from 0.180 to 0.735, showing that comparable recognition can conceal large localization differences. A structured multi-stage annotation pipeline achieves 0.784 mIoU, compared with 0.735 for the best single-call baseline and 0.814 human-human agreement. Using the best accuracy pipeline, we create and publicly release a spatial extension of Cholec80 comprising 285,977 bounding boxes across all 184,498 annotated frames from the 80 videos. The annotations and human references are made available on GitHub

Abstract (英文): Aesthetic image cropping (AIC) aims to improve image composition and aesthetic quality by removing extraneous or distracting content. Over the past decade, AIC methods have evolved from specialized vision models to multimodal large language models(MLLMs). Reliable evaluation that reflects human preferences is essential for benchmarking AIC methods and guiding model optimization. However, the current AIC evaluation relies largely on geometry-based metrics such as intersection over union (IoU), as well as image aesthetic assessment(IAA) models. Although inconsistencies with human preferences have long been observed, the robustness and reliability of these evaluation criteria remain underexplored. To bridge this gap, we first construct CropBench, a large-scale benchmark comprising 106,995 human preference pairs for 1,019 images, with each candidate set containing the original image and candidate crops produced by 14 AIC methods. A systematic study on CropBench reveals that the existing criteria, ranging from IoU to off-the-shelf IAA assessment models, only exhibit limited alignment with human preferences. Building upon CropBench, we further propose CropReward, an MLLM-based reward model that evaluates a candidate crop by comparing with the original uncropped image and outputs a score indicating explicit aesthetic improvement or degradation relative to the original. In addition, we employ CropReward to construct CropReward-300k, the largest AIC dataset to date, and further develop CropAlign, a preference-aligned AIC model trained solely with CropReward supervision, without human-annotated crop boxes. Experimental results demonstrate that CropReward aligns with human preferences significantly better than existing criteria. CropAlign also surpasses the state-of-the-art AIC methods, demonstrating the advantage of CropReward in guiding AIC model optimization.

</details>

Abstract (英文): High-resolution 3D Gaussian Splatting training increases pixel and Gaussian-tile intersection counts, expanding rasterization caches that coexist with projection and gradient state at peak memory. A complete-view update aggregates gradients across the view; each region's forward and backward caches can be released after its contribution is collected. BoundedGS separates rasterization and projection backward passes and bounds the active local rasterization working set according to its measured load. Candidate-indexed projection and compact gradient storage address the view-level peaks exposed by this change, while CPU-assisted structure rebuilding and bounded image supply manage resources outside rendering. In a resolution sweep at a fixed count of about 6M Gaussians, the measured rendering working set stays within 1.073-1.077 GiB from 1K to 16K; at 11K, a warm pass takes 19.79 seconds versus 21.76 for full-frame execution. Across five native 5K outdoor scenes, full training reduces the mean rendering working set from 9.97 to 1.76 GiB (82.3%), retains similar reconstruction quality, and shortens training time from 36.7 to 33.9 minutes.
  Project page: https://xiaobin2001.github.io/BoundedGS-Web/

Abstract (英文): Scalable 3D Gaussian Splatting allows one scene representation to serve different Gaussian-count budgets, yet its quality-budget curve may lag behind models optimized separately for each budget. Reducing the number of Gaussians changes both the selected members and the reconstruction tasks carried by the survivors; parameters inherited from a high-budget model may fit a low-budget representation poorly. We propose ParetoGS, which describes member changes and parameter evolution with a small set of adapted budget anchors and decodes intermediate budgets without separate optimization at every requested count. ParetoGS alternates pruning and fixed-member adaptation to build nested anchors with persistent identities, and encodes their parameter changes relative to the full model in a compact residual atlas. At decoding, it interpolates the states of shared members and gradually introduces the rendering contributions of new members, connecting sparse anchors into a continuous-budget representation. Across 13 scenes, ParetoGS attains better average PSNR, SSIM, and LPIPS with fewer Gaussians than the compared prefix methods at 10% of each method's own full model. From a shared full model, it also outperforms FlexGS-imp at its trained 1%, 5%, and 10% budgets and NanoGS-imp at the 1% and 0.1% budgets. On Garden, its non-anchor quality approaches an empirical reference from independently optimized GNS models at higher retention ratios and exceeds it at some lower-budget points. Across 13 scenes, encoding reduces mean additional state from 390.0 to 85.7 MB with an average PSNR change of 0.01 dB.
  Project page: https://xiaobin2001.github.io/ParetoGS-Web/

Abstract (英文): 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) has emerged as a promising approach for fast, high-quality novel-view rendering. However, its large parameter size remains a bottleneck for storage and transmission. We propose RacerGS, a compact 3DGS compression framework that achieves scene reconstruction at significantly reduced storage sizes with preserved fidelity. To obtain compact and spatially coherent features, we design a hash projection model that projects the anchor hash encodings into compact context features. Additionally, we introduce a FiLM-modulated autoregressive GRU context model that utilizes causal dependencies among anchor feature groups and uses recurrent hidden states to refine entropy-model parameters, improving bitrate efficiency. Furthermore, we adaptively entropy-code quantized anchor-attribute residuals relative to their context-predicted means, leveraging spatial consistency to reduce symbol entropy. Overall, RacerGS achieves more than 150x compression over vanilla 3DGS and 28x over Scaffold-GS, while providing comparable rendering quality. Extensive experiments across five datasets demonstrate that RacerGS outperforms prior 3DGS compression methods in both storage reduction and rate-distortion performance.

</details>

Abstract (英文): We propose a novel method for robust point cloud registration with only one tunable parameter. Our method, dubbed TLP, uses truncated $L_p$ functions with different values of $p$ to assign priority scores to correspondences, select the hypothesis, and refine the final model. By leveraging the robustness of truncated functions, TLP effectively handles the high outlier rates commonly encountered in real-world 3D feature matching. We also exploit GPU parallelization to evaluate hypotheses efficiently, enabling registration from thousands of correspondences in under a second on average. With only one tunable parameter, our method offers greater practicality than multi-parameter methods that require dataset-specific tuning of several parameters. Evaluations on public benchmark datasets demonstrate that TLP achieves higher accuracy than state-of-the-art single-parameter methods. Furthermore, it outperforms two widely used multi-parameter methods on three of the four evaluated datasets while maintaining competitive runtime.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Training a spatial VLM to answer which side an object lies on does not require it to preserve the metric displacement that determines that answer, while supervision for a distance estimate may leave categorical relations largely unconstrained. When post-training emphasizes one readout, improvements can therefore concentrate on that interface while transfer to others remains weak or even degrades; we call this observed specialization interface lock-in. ChronoReplay addresses it by replaying heterogeneous spatial supervision as chronologically ordered episodes, retaining source-native targets, and balancing supervision across families. Under the same observation access, a single ChronoReplay checkpoint improves OVO-S-Bench, OST-Bench, and UCS-Bench together, including a 13.41-point gain on OST-Bench, and achieves the highest three-benchmark mean among the supervision settings compared here. Same-data controls further separate the ingredients of this transfer: answer serialization redistributes gains across OST and UCS, family-balanced learning improves over ordinary joint SFT and natural turn weighting, and chronological replay exceeds a shifted-order control by about 2.41 OST points across two paired seeds. Adaptive frame selection then changes evidence access while keeping the trained model fixed, providing an additional inference-time gain at lower visual cost. Streaming spatial performance depends on what distinctions supervision preserves, how heterogeneous histories are organized during learning, and what causal evidence inference makes available at test time.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Recent advances in image editing have enabled fine-grained and highly controllable visual modifications. However, achieving similar levels of controllability in video editing remains an open challenge, particularly for precise local edits, dynamic temporal effects, and consistent lighting across frames.In this paper, we propose EditDistill, a framework that distills image-level visual transformations from edits to guide video editing.Specifically, given a source video and an edit prompt, we first generate an edited version of the initial video frame using an off-the-shelf image editing model. We then distill the visual transformation of this original-edited image pair into a compact representation termed an edit descriptor.This descriptor is injected into a video diffusion model through our lightweight fusion module, which steers the denoising process toward the desired edit. To align the video trajectory with the demonstrated image edit while preserving motion and layout, we train with edit-descriptor alignment and DINO structural consistency losses, and derive an equivalent inference-time guidance formulation under flow matching.Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method achieves strong performance on standard benchmarks and produces high-quality video editing results across diverse scenarios. For more details and videos, please refer to the anonymous webpage: https://yu-shaonian.github.io/EditDistill-Web/.

Abstract (英文): Conveyor belts are vital arteries in continuous bulk material handling, particularly in heavy process industries such as cement manufacturing. Undetected structural anomalies (longitudinal tears, holes, degraded patch repairs, and impact punctures) cause catastrophic belt snapping, costly downtime, and severe safety hazards. While deep learning offers high accuracy, existing models rely on power-hungry workstation GPUs, hindering practical deployment at remote industrial edge points. In this paper, we propose, implement, and validate an end-to-end, low-power edge AI vision system for real-time conveyor belt defect detection deployed on a Raspberry Pi 5 accelerated by a Google Coral Edge TPU coprocessor. The system leverages an optimized YOLOv11 nano (YOLOv11n) architecture trained on a multi-source industrial corpus exceeding 3,000 augmented samples from an operational cement plant in West Java, open-source datasets, and automated captures. The model undergoes post-training full integer quantization (INT8) compiled for systolic tensor execution. Experimental results demonstrate robust detection capability, achieving precision of 0.742, recall of 0.730, and mAP@0.5 of 0.764 across four defect classes. Deployed on-site in an IP-rated enclosure interfaced with an RTSP PoE industrial camera, the system achieves stable throughput of 30-40 FPS at 320x320 resolution with an end-to-end latency of 70-90 ms. Operational profiling under harsh clinker dust confirms thermal equilibrium at 55 +/- 5 deg C on the host CPU and ~65 deg C on the Edge TPU with zero frame loss. This demonstrates that edge AI bridges the gap between high-accuracy computer vision and cost-effective predictive maintenance in continuous manufacturing.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Object re-identification requires embeddings that are robust to intra-class variation in viewpoint, illumination, and occlusion, yet preserve sharp separation between visually similar identities. The hardest errors arise precisely where confusable identities meet, a region that pairwise losses shape only implicitly. We present PBR-ReID, a prototype-based framework that makes this inter-class geometry an explicit learning target. Each identity is represented by a learnable prototype jointly optimized with a DiNOv3 backbone and the resulting prototype similarity matrix reveals, at every training step, which identities are most confusable. On this signal we build Boundary Repulsion MixUp (BRM), a geometry-aware regularizer that converts standard MixUp's weakness into a discriminative signal. Rather than interpolating samples across confusable identities, which blurs the boundaries that matter most, BRM constructs an explicit boundary proxy between each confusable pair and repels their features away from it, converting boundary ambiguity into a discriminative signal. We adopt a two-stage training protocol consisting of a prototype warmup phase followed by EMA-stabilized joint fine-tuning. Extensive experiments on person and vehicle ReID benchmarks demonstrate strong performance, with thorough ablations confirming each component's contribution. Code is available at https://github.com/psaltaath/pbr-reid.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Text-to-motion models generate for the skeletons they were trained on, while a new character arrives as a skeleton file and nothing else. We study text-to-motion for skeletons that no training clip has used, with no clips of the character, no template, no retargeting and no fine-tuning. We present TopX, a 73M-parameter flow-matching transformer that generates joint rotations and a root trajectory for any kinematic tree from its tree, rest pose and joint names. Motion is normalised by the rest pose and the size of the skeleton, and the rest pose enters as a world-frame descriptor that does not depend on how the rig's author oriented each joint, with rest conventions randomised in training. Attention is factorised over the joints and the frames of a motion and carries a spatial--temporal encoding designed for skeleton graphs of any topology: queries and keys are rotated across joints by a heat-kernel coordinate of the skeleton graph, which is defined for every tree and does not change with the order of its joints or the eigenbasis of its Laplacian, and across frames by a rotary encoding of frame order. To measure what this buys, we build SkelShift: four motion corpora under one codec, 143,334 captioned clips on skeletons of 4 to 142 joints, from which 4,232 clips on 2,043 skeletons are held out by clip, rig, tree class or species and organised along topology, joint count, body plan, species and rig source, and scored by motion quality: Fréchet distances on public kinetic features and on kinematic descriptors, foot skating and ground contact. Against UniMate trained on the same clips, on test rigs without a training clip, TopX lowers the descriptor FID from 5.73 to 4.58 when UniMate's guidance is tuned for the kinetic FID, and the kinetic FID from 1.48 to 0.55 when it is tuned for the descriptor FID, also on motions no training skeleton performs; fewer of its frames sink below the ground.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Language models switch tasks from demonstrations, but vision still often relies on separate systems for restoration, editing, and stylization. A before--after example offers a shared instruction only if the transferable change can be separated from the scene that demonstrates it. We introduce CrossPair, which adapts a pretrained multimodal image editor to transfer diverse transformations from one exemplar pair. Exemplar-before, exemplar-after, and query images take fixed roles in its native VLM and VAE pathways: the VLM extracts operation semantics, while the VAE retains fine appearance and structure. A training-only, change-aware DINOv3 objective aligns feature-change directions across content-distinct scenes, guiding the output to follow the demonstrated relation without an inference-time encoder. We construct Transform-360K by grouping filtered pairs from conditional representations, restoration, editing, virtual try-on, and stylization under shared transformations. On Relation252K-derived seen and held-out transformations, \method improves target agreement and edit-direction similarity over released systems. Ablations show that the semantic and visual exemplar routes are complementary and that DINO relation alignment further strengthens transfer; Transform-360K training also improves aggregate held-out-operation scores.

</details>

Abstract (英文): 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) has become a standard representation for real-time novel-view synthesis. Yet, its quality-efficiency trade-off still relies heavily on adaptive density control. Existing cloning, splitting, and pruning rules use proxy signals, such as position gradients, opacity, and image-space error. Although effective in practice, these signals do not directly answer the structural question behind density control: where should new Gaussians be allocated to reduce the multi-view reconstruction loss, and which existing primitives can be pruned with minimal penalty. To address this, we propose first-variation birth-death control for 3DGS, a principled approach that replaces heuristic structural decisions with a variational score derived from the reconstruction loss. At each structural update, we freeze the current Gaussian population, attach auxiliary mass coordinates to each primitive, and differentiate the multiview loss with respect to these coordinates. We demonstrate that this score is a coordinate of the first variation of the Splat Regression Model, giving a rigorous local descent surrogate for birth-death moves: high-score Gaussians are selected as birth sites, while low-score, low-opacity Gaussians are selected for removal. Across standard 3DGS benchmarks, integrating this score into 3DGS-MCMC yields state-of-the-art reconstruction quality and outperforms existing methods, delivering the most significant gains under tight Gaussian budgets. Ultimately, our work provides compelling evidence that gradient-based variational metrics offer a principled alternative to proxy heuristics in 3DGS.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Traditional autoencoders rely on static network architectures to learn latent representations. Recently we suggested a dynamic architecture inspired by Hebbian plasticity, enabling topological self-organization among convolutional feature maps (R. Koç et al., Proceedings of the SIU, 2026). Early implementations of this paradigm infer relationships among feature maps using global average pooling and model their interactions through scalar-valued connections. This approach falls short to fully exploit the underlying spatial information, embedded in input images. Furthermore, abrupt transitions between different loss functions and training dynamics can introduce representation shocks, leading to unstable feature learning. In this paper, we introduce a Spatially-Aware Self-Organizing Graph Autoencoder (S-LooM) to address these structural bottlenecks. By using spatial cross-correlation, and spatial kernels, S-LooM establishes dynamic connections among feature maps. We replace static scalar message passing of autoencoders with learnable spatial kernels and eliminate high-frequency deconvolutional artifacts observed in decoders using nearest-neighbor upsampling. Via a soft-blending training curriculum, the latent topology aligns smoothly during training. Our experiments show that S-LooM yields a more stable topology, compared to the state of the art dynamic architectures. Meaning, the graph structure that emerge has more resistance to feature entanglement and noise. It forms functional communities responsible for visual attributes, such as illumination, shape and texture even under high connection densities.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Feature analysis is a crucial step in medical image analysis, particularly in breast cancer classification. Traditional image analysis methods often struggle with optimal patterns along the diagonal axis, computational efficiency and feature buffer storage. Different classical operators require high-efficiency hardware for the complex operations, such as image convolution and gradient analysis, which are used for processing complex machine learning models for training and test operations. Here, in this research, we introduce a non-interpolation virtual trellis-based (NVHT) operator that utilizes a filter kernel over the same square image without transformation to a hexagonal trellis. This proposed method is very efficient in reducing computational and storage costs. The proposed operator is used for improved feature extraction efficiency and accuracy in disease classification. This method outperforms conventional approaches by leveraging a hexagonal pixel representation while maintaining high sensitivity in different directions with less storage.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Non-rigid structure from motion (NRSfM) aims to recover camera motion and time-varying 3D shape from tracked 2D image measurements. Recent high-performing methods show that shape-space factorization remains highly effective for this task. These methods often use costly solvers for two key steps. First, a semidefinite programming (SDP) cone solver to perform Euclidean correction. Next, an alternating direction method of multipliers (ADMM) solver to recover the low-rank shape. These steps often scale poorly to long sequences and dense point tracks. We propose a fast, scalable NRSfM solver that avoids these costly steps. For rotation estimation, we introduce a differentiable Euclidean correction that directly optimizes small factors for each sequence. A row-polar projection then maps each estimated camera block to the closest row-orthonormal matrix. For shape estimation, we introduce an unrolled spectral-proximal solver. It combines reprojection-gradient steps with weighted singular-value shrinkage in the reshuffled shape domain. This solver further uses FISTA-style restart and continuation. The spectral shrinkage operator itself is classical, but we embed it in a fixed differentiable solver, while preserving NRSfM's geometric constraints. The proposed approach is category-agnostic, requires no external training data, and maps naturally to modern accelerator-friendly linear algebra primitives. Across sparse, dense, noisy, missing-data, and long-sequence benchmarks, our solver achieves substantial speedups with competitive reconstruction accuracy, including 7x-1000x runtime improvements on dense sequences with 28,887 tracked points.

</details>

Abstract (英文): This study investigates whether predicted bounding boxes are reliable for determining where small-object segmentation should be performed without risking false negatives outside those boxes. The investigation leverages adenovirus particles as small objects, YOLO26x for detection, YO-LO26x-Sem for segmentation, and Mask R-CNN for both detection and segmentation. Segmenta-tion-model predictions were matched to ground-truth adenovirus masks or reference objects using their centroids, with ground-truth mask centroids preferred over ground-truth bounding-box centroids as references for matching. Matched predicted-mask centroids were considered as inside at least one detector-predicted bounding box or outside all such boxes, while ground-truth masks without matched predictions were recorded as having no matched segmentation. For the YOLO26 models, 34 reference objects (47.89%) had matched segmentation centroids inside at least one pre-dicted box, 8 (11.27%) had centroids outside all predicted boxes, and 29 (40.85%) had no matched predicted segmentation. For the Mask R-CNN model, the corresponding counts were 69 (97.18%), 0 (0.00%), and 2 (2.82%). These preliminary experiments demonstrated that restricting segmentation to detector-predicted bounding boxes risks excluding matched segmentation predictions outside those boxes, potentially introducing false negatives.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Federated continual learning (FCL) has attracted considerable interest by enabling local clients to continually acquire knowledge from new tasks. However, most FCL methods assume that the global server has prior knowledge of the arrival times and number of categories for new tasks, which is unrealistic in the real world. Furthermore, they neglect the importance of positive inter-task semantic relationships when learning new tasks and are significantly affected by irrelevant relationships from old tasks, thereby exacerbating catastrophic forgetting. To resolve the above challenges, we develop a novel Task Relevance-driven Prompt Expansion (TRPE) model. Specifically, to eliminate the reliance on prior knowledge of new tasks, we propose an out-of-distribution detector to identify the arrival of new tasks within local clients, along with a radius-adaptive DBSCAN to infer the quantity of categories in new tasks on the proxy server. Moreover, to explore positive inter-task relationships, we calculate the affinity between the original gradient for learning new task and its gradient orthogonal to the feature space of old tasks to evaluate semantic relevance between old and new tasks from a global perspective. Then, we use this global task relevance to determine the key-prompt pair for new tasks and identify highly related old tasks. After concatenating their prompts for training, we can leverage such positive inter-task relations to improve the continual learning of newly arriving tasks. To balance the learning of new tasks and the forgetting of old ones, a soft orthogonality constraint is applied to the local key and prompt gradients. Experiments spanning diverse datasets and backbones confirm the efficacy of our TRPE model.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Significant advancements in 3D asset creation have enabled high-fidelity 3D generation from images. However, most existing representations remain inherently static, lacking the ability to model physically plausible behaviors under dynamic interactions. We present \textbf{PhysCreator}, a unified framework that directly generates simulation-ready 3D representations with spatially varying physical properties from images. Unlike prior approaches that treat physical attributes as simple post-hoc predictions or rely on expensive per-instance optimization, we reformulate physical property estimation within a unified 3D representation learning framework. We introduce a voxel-aligned latent space by lifting image observations into a spatially structured 3D representation, in which geometry, appearance, and physical properties are jointly modeled and inferred in a feed-forward manner. To enable scalable learning without densely annotated physical ground truth, we leverage vision-language distillation to transfer material-aware semantic priors from powerful vision-language models into spatially grounded voxel-level supervision. Experiments demonstrate that our method achieves superior visual quality, physical plausibility, and generalization compared to prior approaches.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Vision--language models applied to medical question answering can reach correct diagnoses without using the diagnostic image, inferring instead from the clinical vignette. We ask whether this is visible in how \emph{firmly} a model holds its answer: does a model defend a diagnosis derived from a blank placeholder as tenaciously as one derived from a real medical image? Crossing an image-substitution protocol with two levels of adversarial pressure on 60 Italian State Examination items, we find no evidence that ungrounded verdicts are more fragile. In a paired item-level analysis, fabricated consensus (L4) overturns 94.4\% of verdicts with the real image and 98.1\% without ($Δ= +0.037$; one-sided 95\% upper bound $+0.105$); contentless doubt (L1) overturns 62.5\% and 51.8\% ($Δ= -0.107$, 95\% CI $[-0.209, -0.001]$), the point estimate running opposite to the direction a grounding detector requires. The two levels nonetheless produce opposite \emph{dynamics}: under L4 the model flips once, always to the exact option argued for (110/110 first-turn flips), and then freezes, averaging 1.9 verdict changes over ten turns; under L1 it never settles, averaging 7.4 changes. Instability under contentless doubt tracks intrinsic item ambiguity rather than evidence availability, and so does not serve as a grounding signal. Flips are almost entirely corrupting (99/100 across both levels). The model is not unaware of the missing evidence---it flags the blank image at baseline on 35 of 60 items and abstains on 4--6---yet those abstentions do not survive contact with pressure. None of the epistemic signals a user could plausibly consult distinguishes a grounded diagnosis from a confabulated one.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Visual-token compression is usually evaluated by clean accuracy and efficiency, but these averages do not reveal failures that compression introduces under an image shift where the uncompressed model stays correct. We introduce VTC-AUDIT, which estimates this incremental risk only on pairs where the full model is correct on both images and the compressed model is correct on the clean image. The four arms then cross two binary choices: which image selects the retained token positions, and which image supplies the visual features. We audit three open 8-9B vision-language models on four tasks and eight pre-specified shifts. We evaluate two training-free compressors---hard-pruning DivPrune and prune-and-merge ApET---at a matched 25% retained-position budget, producing 708,000 inference records. Reselecting positions on the shifted image, rather than reusing the clean-image positions, raises the failure rate in all six model-compressor settings. The increase is 3.04-9.60 percentage points for DivPrune and 2.05-4.20 percentage points for ApET, with every 95% interval above zero. Repeating the audit on a separately resampled second manifest reproduces all six increases. In a pooled monitor audit across the four benchmarks, support Jaccard instability fails the practical AUROC criterion of 0.65 in all six settings, whereas clean compressed-model uncertainty clears it. Thus, support turnover is a weak ranking signal under the audited benchmark mixture, while clean compressed-model uncertainty is substantially more informative. These results suggest that future visual-token compression evaluations should report conditional reliability alongside clean accuracy and efficiency.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Adapting general-purpose vision-language models (VLMs) to medical visual question answering (VQA) is valuable for helping clinicians read images and make diagnoses, but annotated data are scarce and provide only short answers, without reasoning annotations. Supervised fine-tuning (SFT) fits these labels but overfits to them and amplifies their biases, eroding out-of-domain (OOD) generalization. Reinforcement learning (RL) better preserves generalization but never uses the gold answer as a training target: GRPO-style algorithms learn from the contrast between correct and incorrect answers within a group of sampled responses, so all-correct or all-wrong groups give no correctness signal. Existing SFT-RL hybrids either apply SFT and RL in separate stages or switch between them per question, or need reasoning traces from experts or stronger models, which medical VQA data lack. We propose GRASP, which combines RL and SFT in every update using only short answers: RL lets the model explore its own reasoning, SFT teaches the gold answer after that reasoning to supply the direct supervision RL lacks, and their balance adapts to how well the model answers each question. Tuning only about 1% of the parameters with LoRA, GRASP outperforms all compared training objectives both in domain and on OOD open-ended questions across Qwen3-VL-8B, InternVL3-8B and Gemma 3-4B. On an OOD benchmark spanning eight imaging modalities, GRASP-trained InternVL3-8B and Qwen3-VL-8B beat all 10 compared general and medical VLMs in open-ended accuracy, including models trained on up to 2,600 times more data or with over 250 times more trainable parameters. We release MedTerse, a dataset of 12.9K short-answer questions, and our code at https://github.com/sea-comet/GRASP-MedVQA.

Abstract (英文): Contrastive vision-language models such as CLIP typically rely on independently parameterized transformer blocks across depth. We investigate the extent to which these blocks can instead be reused recurrently while preserving the transferable capabilities of CLIP. We study recursive transformer backbones in which a small set of $N$ transformer blocks is applied $M$ times per forward pass. By holding $N \cdot M$ fixed to the depth of a standard ViT-B, our models preserve computational depth while progressively increasing parameter sharing. We consider two recursive CLIP model families: rCLIP, where only the vision encoder is recursive, and r$^2$CLIP, where recurrence is also introduced in the text encoder. We study these models under both direct contrastive training and through knowledge distillation from a strong ViT-B/16 teacher pretrained on DFN-2B, using the substantially smaller CC3M and CC12M datasets. We evaluate the resulting models on zero-shot classification and cross-modal retrieval, and further study their adaptability through few-shot learning, visual grounding, and concept unlearning. Our results show that CLIP can tolerate substantial recurrent block reuse, although its effect depends on the encoder and downstream task. Recurrence also provides parameter efficiency by construction, since fewer distinct transformer blocks need to be stored and adapted. Our results establish recurrent block reuse as a practical design space for vision-language models and highlight its implications for knowledge transfer, downstream adaptation, and parameter efficiency.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Most action-conditioned video world models remain limited to a single player, while existing multiplayer models support only small, fixed groups of agents. We introduce FlockWorld, an artificial-life environment built on Reynolds's Boids algorithm for studying population-scale multi-agent video world models. Specifically, FlockWorld renders synchronized egocentric crops, assigns distinct colors to preserve camera-agent identity across views, exposes rule-derived accelerations as actions, and lets the number of camera agents be configured independently of the size of the surrounding population. We further introduce FlockDiT, an action-conditioned latent diffusion transformer trained with flow matching on FlockWorld data to jointly generate synchronized egocentric videos for multiple agents. Our results suggest that joint generation benefits agent detection at density-matched settings and that diffusion forcing improves rollout fidelity. Together, FlockWorld and FlockDiT provide a controlled testbed for multi-agent video generation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Erasing all furniture from a single room image to generate its empty counterpart is valuable for XR applications such as diminished reality and virtual staging. The challenge is twofold: inpainting the furniture regions with realistic content, and doing so without distorting the room's 3D geometry. Prompt-driven image editors, such as FLUX.2, handle realism impressively, but their structural faithfulness is inferior to explicit layout conditioned inpainting. To improve geometric consistency, we propose a training recipe for finetuning a LoRA on 2,500 furnished-empty room pairs. FLUX.2 with our LoRA adaptor significantly improves geometric consistency while retaining the high texture fidelity. This single-photo inpainter can be an effective building block for depth-aware and 3D-consistent XR pipelines.

Abstract (英文): Despite advances in automated liver lesion segmentation, a gap remains between image-derived predictions and clinically interpretable radiology reports. Bridging this gap requires quantitative measurements that are both reproducible and traceable. We present an evidence-grounded agentic AI framework that connects liver and lesion segmentation with deterministic quantification and structured reporting. TotalSegmentator-based nnU-Net models delineate the liver, Couinaud segments, and lesions, while computational tools extract volumes, dimensions, counts, and anatomical locations. A large language model (LLM) organizes these measurements into a predefined reporting schema, with numerical consistency checks against source measurements. On 51 LiTS cases, the segmentation stage achieved a liver Dice score of 0.947, lesion sensitivity of 75.6%, and positive predictive value of 68.9%. Exploratory screening of 25,494 MERLIN reports identified 10,208 (40.0%) with liver-related abnormalities. By connecting quantitative imaging evidence with agentic report generation, this work takes a step toward human-centered medical AI that prioritizes traceability and clinical relevance.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Objective. Deformable image registration remains challenging because corresponding anatomy may exhibit different intensity relationships. Hand-crafted modality-robust descriptors such as Modality-Independent Neighborhood Descriptor with Self-Similarity Context (MIND-SSC) and features from pretrained vision encoders provide distinct representations for correspondence estimation, but how they should be combined remains unclear. We investigate whether tighter integration improves registration over a sequential, scale-separated design. Approach. We introduce FlexiMIND, a training-free and label-free two-stage framework in which frozen FlexiCT-2D encoder features establish contextual correspondence and MIND-SSC refines the deformation within ConvexAdam. Five alternative feature-coupling strategies were evaluated under a protocol across CT-CT (DIR-Lab 4DCT 4DCT, 10 cases), MR-MR (ACDC, 50 patients), and MR-CT (Learn2Reg Abdomen MR-CT, 8 pairs) registration. Performance was assessed using target registration error (TRE), Dice similarity coefficient (Dice), SDlogJ, and local folding. Main results. FlexiMIND achieved a TRE of 1.27 mm on DIR-Lab 4DCT, a DSC of 0.807 on ACDC, and an organ-averaged DSC of 0.825 on Abdomen MR-CT. Across 15 coupling-strategy dataset comparisons, only four outperformed the reference method, with a maximum absolute improvement of 0.0043 in Dice, whereas degradation reached 20.6 % in TRE and 37.9 % in Dice. Changes in registration accuracy were closely associated with changes in deformation-field regularity, and strategies that better preserved regularity generally remained closest to the reference performance. Landmark-level analysis showed that the pretrained encoder stage primarily reduced large correspondence errors rather than uniformly improving local precision. Code is available upon acceptance at https://github.com/mosaf/FlexiMIND.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Vision-language anomaly detectors describe normal and abnormal appearance with text, and their gains are commonly credited to the semantic content of these descriptions. This attribution is plausible in the zero-shot setting, but few- and full-shot detectors interpose a trained visual adapter, and in that setting it has not been isolated. Changing a description changes both its meaning and its geometry, so accuracy comparisons cannot separate the two. We ask what a fixed pair of text embeddings, or anchors, contributes to such a detector: semantic content, effective scale, or joint anchor geometry. For a single anchor pair, the answer is effective scale alone. The score reads the anchors only through their difference, and a trained adapter can rotate to follow any replacement direction, leaving that direction, and with it the linguistic content, unidentifiable. We prove that replacement anchors with the same difference-vector length permit exactly the same probability maps and attainable losses. Across seven medical datasets, three vision backbones, both supervision regimes, and three adapter families, scale-matched random and orthogonal anchors track correct prompts; breaking the scale match opens a gap that restoring it closes without text. The same pattern holds in MVFA and when MadCLIP's learned prompts are replaced by a text-free learned scale. When one adapter serves many categories, the answer expands to their joint arrangement. Rotating all category axes together changes nothing, as predicted. For the tested CLIP prompts, these axes are nearly collinear, and meaning-free rotations that spread them apart improve performance on both industrial benchmarks. Even under frozen zero-shot transfer, a random anchor frame transfers as well as the prompt frame: coordinate consistency, not meaning. What these detectors read from their prompts is scale and arrangement, not semantic content.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Recently, point-supervised temporal action localization has attracted increasing attention due to its low annotation cost (only one frame per action instance) and competitive detection performance. However, since point annotations are less complete than proposal annotations, existing methods mainly rely on multiple instance learning for snippet-level classification, which leads to a performance gap compared to fully supervised models. To bridge this gap, we propose P2P-Former, a novel point-to-proposal Transformer-based framework that effectively connects point-level supervision with proposal-level learning for temporal action localization. In this work, we identify two key challenges in transitioning from point-level supervision to full supervision: (1) how to generate reliable proposals as pseudo ground-truth, and (2) how to effectively integrate point-level cues into the fully supervised paradigm. To address the first challenge, we propose a Point-aware Proposal Generation (PPG) strategy that refines the confidence of proposals produced by a base locator using a point-aware importance estimation function, and then performs optimal matching between point annotations and candidate proposals to select reliable proposals as pseudo ground-truth. To tackle the second challenge, we introduce a Point-guided Semantic Enhancement (PSE) module that aggregates prototypes from point-annotated features and injects them into snippet features through Transformer-based interactions. Moreover, we design a Point-to-Proposal Constraint Loss (PPCL) that enforces each predicted proposal to include the annotated point, thereby encouraging more accurate localization. Extensive experiments on the THUMOS14 and ActivityNet-1.3 datasets demonstrate that our method outperforms several existing state-of-the-art approaches.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Driving video captured through a windshield violates the world-space consistency assumed by 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS): the road scene exhibits parallax while cabin reflections remain attached to the camera, causing standard 3DGS to embed reflected content into the reconstructed scene. We introduce RIDE-Splat, a pipeline for reflection-free 3D reconstruction from posed driving video without clean images, reflection masks, or pretrained networks. RIDE-Splat assigns world-consistent transmission through the windshield to a 3DGS scene and the dominant camera-attached reflection to one shared, signed RGB image. It builds the clean reconstruction in two passes. First, it learns the 3D geometry and the signed reflection image, representing both added radiance and transmission attenuation. Then, it uses the reflection-removed training images to fit a clean 3DGS model. At inference, the reflection image is discarded, preserving the standard 3DGS rendering path. We also introduce the RIDE-Splat Dataset, the first 3D reconstruction benchmark for reflection removal from driving video -- with real driving scenes, physical windshield reflections in training views, and separately captured non-reflective evaluation images. Across the RIDE-Splat Dataset, XLD, and Wayve, RIDE-Splat outperforms 3DGS, DC-Gaussian, and RobustSplat on all nine aggregate metrics, improving PSNR by up to 3.59 dB and reducing LPIPS by up to 0.0876. We achieve the highest metrics in 38 out of 42 per-scene comparisons and removes windshield reflections that remain visible in the baselines while preserving the transmitted scene.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Background The combination of hematoma location, expansion and final volume influence neurological outcome. The aim of this study was to derive and validate a quantitative and interpretable region-based NCCT image feature model for predicting hematoma expansion. Methods The Ethnic/Racial Variations of Intracerebral Hemorrhage(ERICH) study was used for derivation and the Virtual International Trials in Stroke Archive(VISTA) was used for validation. NCCT images at presentation and 24+/-6hours were pre-processed, segmented and categorized into lobar, basal ganglia or thalamic regions. Hematoma expansion was defined by region and according to its effect on clinical outcome. Transport-based morphometry (TBM) produced a unique transport-based representation from each NCCT hematoma image. Linear classifiers applied to transport representations were used to derive a predictive model. The regional TBM model's performance was compared to alternate methods using AUROC analyses. Results The derivation dataset included 574 patients, comprising 162 lobar, 257 basal ganglia and 155 thalamic hematomas. The validation dataset included 196 patients, comprising 31 lobar, 119 basal ganglia and 46 thalamic hematomas. A combined regional model predicted hematoma expansion in the validation dataset with an AUROC of 0.787. The principal image features which varied by location were size, heterogeneity, eccentricity and density distribution towards the hematoma periphery. The regional TBM model outperformed alternative clinician based and machine-learning methods. Conclusions TBM discriminated and visualized regional features of hematoma expansion from presentation NCCT scans. This provides preliminary evidence for regional radiographic features of hematoma expansion. It has the potential to improve hematoma expansion prediction and understanding of hematoma expansion pathophysiology.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Vision-language models are shifting from offline passive understanding toward online proactive interaction. However, prevailing methods largely rely on explicit requests while miss the implicit user intent (e.g., "where should I click next?" while a user navigate a website), meanwhile fail to update the state simultaneously as user requests might update continuously. These limitations make them less suited to intent-aware interaction in practical. To address these challenges, we introduce StreamVibe-Bench, a new benchmark serving as testbed for user intent interaction, with 4.1K evaluation samples and 85K training samples spanning explicit, implicit, and dynamic intents. Building on this benchmark, we develop StreamVibe-VL, a streaming proactive model that infers implicit user intents from continuous observations and dynamically tailors its interactions as these intents evolve. We further introduce StreamVibe-Harness, which maintains user intents as persistent states with a fleixble lifecycles via a hierarchical memory to track the latest user intent and environmental state. Our experiments reveal three key findings: (i) StreamVibe-VL preserves strong general video understanding while improving response timing and answer quality by 10.7% over other baselines; (ii) existing proactive models remain limited in understanding user intent and deciding when to respond, especially under implicit and dynamic intents; and (iii) StreamVibe-Harness further improves response consistency and adaptation over sustained interaction, resulting in a 12.3% gain in proactive interaction.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Language-Only Vision-Language Model Selection (LOVM) aims to identify the Vision-Language Model (VLM) with the highest zero-shot accuracy on a target task using only textual information. We revisit the existing LOVM evaluation protocol and argue that ranking metrics, such as top-5 recall and Kendall's rank correlation, do not directly assess the performance of the selected model. We therefore adopt simple regret (SR), defined as the performance gap between the best and selected models, and introduce Outlier-Robust Normalized Regret (ORNR) to account for the varying performance ranges across tasks while reducing sensitivity to outliers. Building on this revised evaluation protocol, we propose Target-Conditioned Expected Source Regret (TC-ESR), a training-free method that selects the model with the lowest similarity-weighted mean regret across source tasks. Across 22 target tasks and 43 VLMs, TC-ESR achieves the lowest mean SR and ORNR among all compared methods. Separately, we identify two sources of target performance leakage in the prior ranking-based evaluation protocol and provide corrections that remove both.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Knowledge distillation can transfer large multimodal models to compact models for autonomous driving, but uniform token-level matching can dilute planning-relevant semantics and amplify teacher errors. We propose RSKD, a semantic-consistency-guided distillation framework that routes supervision according to both the task importance of an answer phrase and its semantic consistency with the teacher prediction. Task-critical semantic phrases are localized in the ground-truth answer, and a phrase-level consistency score controls the balance between Jensen--Shannon divergence (JSD) distillation and ground-truth supervision. Teacher information is emphasized only where it is semantically consistent, while semantically inconsistent phrases receive stronger ground-truth supervision. We evaluate RSKD on DrivingVQA, LingoQA, and MapLMv2. RSKD improves over the compared student-based baselines on DrivingVQA and LingoQA and remains competitive on MapLMv2.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Frequency-domain information is beneficial for semantic segmentation. However, existing frequency-based methods often suffer from insufficient frequency utilization, inefficient fusion, and high computational complexity. To address these issues, we propose FGD2HF, a dual-domain fusion network that leverages frequency features to guide spatial-domain fusion and enhancement. Specifically, we introduce a novel Hybrid-Attention Encoder (NAHA) that jointly models local and global relationships through neighborhood and hypergraph attention, enhancing the network's ability to extract effective features. We further introduce a Wavelet Transform and Enhancement (WTE) module to extract and refine low- and high-frequency cues from preceding-stage features, followed by Frequency-guided Dual-domain Fusion (FGDF) modules that use frequency cues to condition current-stage spatial grouping and feature propagation. This design incorporates both global and local frequency guidance into spatial feature modulation, enhancement and fusion with low computational cost. Extensive comparisons with numerous state-of-the-art semantic segmentation methods demonstrate that our method is highly competitive. Our full FGD2HF-B model achieves 48.7% mIoU on ADE20K (+0.3% over SegMAN-B with 18% fewer GFLOPs), 83.2% mIoU on Cityscapes (comparable to SegMAN-B with 21% fewer GFLOPs), and 1.1% higher mIoU than VWFormer-T on SBD with 21% fewer GFLOPs, while also achieving significant improvements in boundary segmentation accuracy.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Reconstructing 1-m imagery from Sentinel-2 combines two difficulties. The input and the reference are observed by different satellites, which differ in spatial resolution, spectral response, radiometry, acquisition time, and local geometry, so no synthetic degradation operator links them. The required magnification of ten in each spatial dimension also far exceeds the factors of two to four at which remote sensing super-resolution is usually developed. We study this real-world $\times 10$ problem using YREB-SR, which pairs 2022 Sentinel-2 RGB composites with 2022 Jilin-1 RGB references resampled to a 1-m grid over the Yangtze River Economic Belt. The benchmark contains 11,559 geographically matched scene pairs, with 6,409, 2,135, and 3,015 assigned to training, validation, and testing, respectively. We develop CrossVAR, a visual autoregressive model that predicts the 1-m latent as a nine-scale hierarchy conditioned on the Sentinel-2 input and estimates the remaining quantization residual with a diffusion refiner run without injected noise. Complete 1600 $\times$ 1600 scenes are reconstructed in overlapping windows that start on the Sentinel-2 pixel grid, as every training patch does, and every window is decoded at the size on which the decoder was trained before the windows are blended with Gaussian weights. On all 3,015 test scenes, CrossVAR achieves 27.60 dB PSNR, 0.7725 SSIM, 0.1451 LPIPS, and 7.15 FID, the best value of each metric among the compared methods. These results establish a strong autoregressive baseline for large-scale cross-sensor reconstruction. To facilitate reproducibility, the source code and implementation are publicly available at https://github.com/SHANOAL/RSSR.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Multi-modal video fusion aims to generate temporally coherent videos with salient targets and rich structural details by integrating complementary information from heterogeneous sensors. However, existing video fusion methods commonly rely on optical flow estimation and feature warping for inter-frame alignment and cross-frame information aggregation to maintain temporal consistency, thereby introducing considerable computational overhead and making the fusion performance vulnerable to flow estimation errors. To address these limitations, this paper proposes an efficient multi-modal video fusion network with dynamic region-guided temporal interaction. Specifically, the proposed spatio-temporal interaction module leverages the Test-Time Training paradigm to model temporal dependencies with linear complexity. Building on this design, the module restricts temporal modeling to key dynamic regions in the video sequence, as static background regions provide limited temporal cues. By excluding low-variation tokens from unnecessary temporal interaction, this selective strategy contributes to improved fusion quality and temporal consistency. In addition, leveraging the strong ability of pretrained MAE features to capture global structural dependencies, we introduce a feature-level temporal consistency constraint to suppress local flickering by aligning the inter-frame variation directions of the fused and source videos. We further propose a new MAE-based temporal metric that quantifies directional distance between their temporal variations within dynamic regions. Experimental results demonstrate that, compared with existing state-of-the-art methods, our method achieves superior fusion quality and temporal consistency on multiple benchmark datasets, while exhibiting high computational efficiency under edge-device deployment settings.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Diffeomorphisms provide a flexible, topology-preserving mathematical tool for modeling complex spatial or temporal deformations and are used in various scientific fields. However, making these deformations simultaneously highly expressive and computationally efficient remains challenging. Continuous Piecewise-Affine Based (CPAB) transformations (Freifeld et al., ICCV 2015; IEEE TPAMI 2017) offer an attractive solution by parameterizing a family of diffeomorphisms via continuous velocity fields that are piecewise affine with respect to a chosen tessellation of the domain. Importantly, $d=\dim(θ)$, the dimension of the parameter vector $θ$, depends on the fineness of the tessellation rather than on the data resolution. Despite this compact parameterization, CPAB optimization has long been hindered by the tight coupling between trajectory integration and gradient computation with respect to $θ$. We introduce FG-CPAB, a factorized-gradient formulation for scalable CPAB transformations. By separating integration from parameter-space projection, our method reduces gradient computation complexity from $\mathcal{O}(dTN)$ to $\mathcal{O}(TN+dC)$, where $T$ is the number of integration steps, $N$ is the number of transformed points, and $C$ is the number of tessellation cells. This reformulation yields speedups of up to four orders of magnitude (e.g., $\approx 10^4\times$ in 2D), substantial memory savings (e.g., $\approx 500\times$ reduction in peak GPU memory usage in 2D), and dramatically higher expressiveness. This makes fine-tessellation CPAB practical in 2D and 3D for the first time, enabling highly expressive parametric diffeomorphisms.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Recent generic reference-based image generation models natively support multiple conditions by concatenating all reference image and text tokens into a joint sequence processed by a single Diffusion Transformer (DiT). However, this versatile paradigm comes at an efficiency cost: accommodating multiple references leads to a token explosion, incurring massive $\mathcal{O}(N^2)$ computational complexity. Most acceleration methods operate inside the DiT, showing limited capability in reducing spatial redundancy and failing to alleviate peak VRAM. While some pre-model token reduction alternatives exist, they inevitably introduce external models. This raises a question: Is it possible to achieve acceleration and memory savings while preserving compatibility with in-model techniques and requiring no additional parameters? In this work, we realize this goal by proposing the early Token reduction via Downsampled self-Probing (ToDoPro). By executing a few denoising steps on spatially $2\times$ downsampled reference images, this cheap probing stage utilizes the DiT model itself to effectively and efficiently extract reference token importance. Using the probed importance, we apply a hierarchical reduction before the main denoising stage: we completely discard low-importance tokens, substitute mid-importance tokens with their downsampled counterparts to cheaply preserve global context, and keep high-importance tokens. Experiments across three base models and two benchmarks demonstrate that our early reduction accelerates inference significantly and mitigates peak GPU memory. Furthermore, we can improve generation quality by safely filtering out noisy, uninformative reference tokens, enabling the model to concentrate its limited attention capacity on genuinely useful tokens. The code will be released at https://github.com/scy639/ToDoPro

</details>

Abstract (英文): Deep neural networks are known to rely on non-robust features that align poorly with human perception. Prior work has largely framed these features as opportunistic shortcuts that help standard models on clean data. We show instead that non-robust cues can be antagonistic: they can act as Natural Jammers that actively mislead standard models on clean, unmodified images. Using disagreement between a standard model and robust models as an analysis lens, we identify a Jammed Set on which robust models outperform standard models. We provide causal evidence for Spectral-spatial Locking: jamming arises only when high-frequency components are precisely aligned with specific spatial structures. Disrupting this lock through spectral filtering or small geometric shifts can restore correct predictions. Crucially, we uncover a Substitution Paradox: while individual jammers are fragile to geometric shifts, the aggregate prevalence of jamming remains approximately stable and is redistributed rather than eliminated under such transformations. Our results reframe natural non-robustness as a failure field over the empirical model-data geometry: one that redistributes rather than disappears under small transformations.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Cities must coordinate domains like infrastructure, mobility, environmental management, emergency response, and public services as conditions evolve. An urban Spatio-Temporal Intelligence (STI) system helps the city monitor citywide states, analyze problems, plan and execute interventions, and adapt through feedback, making urban governance more timely, effective, and transparent. Recent urban twinning and agentic AI techniques provide foundations for such systems. From a twin-agent perspective, this survey takes a system-level view of how these disparate techniques can be coherently integrated into an urban STI system. Specifically, we review how the twin reconstructs and represents a persistent, information-enriched urban environment, and how the agent understands the twin via interfaces, reasons over it, acts reliably, and continuously evolves with the city. Built upon this synthesis, we summarize a two-loop blueprint, where an inner agent loop for grounding, reasoning, and verification, and an outer system loop linking data, twin, agentic action, feedback, and update. We then use this to structure an STI research agenda and show how a shared prototype supports diverse urban domains.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Monocular observations of a tumbling object can support a precise fitted trajectory without establishing that the recovered physical axis is correct. We study this distinction for elongated, approximately cylindrical targets observed by a calibrated stationary camera. ORBIT-SCOUT couples image-derived line and rim geometry to constant-cone and torque-free triaxial trajectory families, then returns a linked momentum-axis and mean-precession-rate estimate only when numerical, geometric, temporal and competing-hypothesis checks pass. We describe the measurement objective, selection policy and limitations, and situate them within spacecraft pose estimation, unknown-target reconstruction, rigid-body identification and selective prediction. An internally preregistered population of 96 new synthetic scenes was processed in two cold passes, retaining all 192 attempts. In the primary pass, 38 scenes produced jointly correct estimates, none produced an incorrect estimate, 57 were withheld and one execution was incomplete. Correct coverage on the 64 supported scenes was 25/64. Ninety-five repeated pairs were scientifically equivalent under a frozen comparison policy; the remaining pair was incomplete. The registered coverage, failure-free execution and all-case repeatability targets were not met. Historical audits also exposed an overly permissive axis metric and motion-dependent acquisition cadence. The contribution is a documented measurement-to-decision implementation and a failure-preserving evaluation case study, not a new rigid-body theory or evidence of state-of-the-art accuracy. Zero observed errors does not establish calibrated risk, and conditional temporal checks do not remove shared image-feature bias. Real-image transfer and external reproduction remain open.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Diffusion MRI (dMRI) provides information about white matter microstructure that can support brain age prediction. However, many existing prediction models use diffusion tensor imaging (DTI) scalar metrics that omit orientation information. Deep learning is increasingly used for brain age prediction, but conventional CNNs do not explicitly respect the geometric structure of orientation-dependent features. We present an interpretable brain age prediction framework that combines white matter fiber orientation distributions (WM-FODs) with an SE(3) (Special Euclidean group in 3 dimensions) equivariant CNN. The network operates directly on spherical harmonic (SH) coefficients of the FOD, preserving their transformation properties under rotation. Evaluation in 692 participants aged 36-90 years from the Human Connectome Project Aging dataset showed that the combined framework outperformed the evaluated DTI-based models and conventional CNNs. Averaging predictions from five cross-validation models yielded a mean absolute error of 3.54 years and Pearson's correlation of 0.958. Our results indicated that the performance advantage depended on combining WM-FODs with equivariant processing. Prediction accuracy was highest at a maximum SH order of Lmax = 2 and declined at higher orders. Tract-level occlusion, where each segmented tract was masked in turn, revealed age-dependent patterns of model sensitivity, with importance peaks in frontal projection pathways at 36-44 years, the fornix at 45-54 years, the callosal isthmus at 55-64 years, the middle cerebellar peduncle at 65-74 years, and the callosal genu at 75-90 years. These findings support rotation-equivariant processing of WM-FODs for accurate brain age prediction with anatomically interpretable model sensitivity.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Recent multi-view Transformers (e.g., DUSt3R and VGGT) have advanced 3D reconstruction by scaling training data and model capacity from two views to many views and long sequences, subsuming the classical SfM/SLAM pipeline of feature extraction, matching, and pose estimation in a single feed-forward pass. Yet this subsumption hides the interfaces between pipeline stages---correspondences, geometry, and pose are entangled in a single forward pass, leaving supervised fine-tuning as the major path to improvement and offering no mechanism to correct predictions from geometric evidence at test time. We propose PosePlaner to close this gap by reviving the classical coupling between correspondences and pose via flow matching, treating pose refinement as a conditional generation problem that takes coarse geometric matches and initial pose as input condition and fuses the resulting pose hypotheses into a single refined estimate, providing a continuous, data-driven analog of RANSAC voting. Our method is trained efficiently on two-view data, yet serves as a periodic refiner for streaming n-view pose estimators with no retraining. Empirically, it corrects failures of feed-forward predictors, substantially improves correspondence-based solvers, and reduces accumulated drift in long streaming sequences.

</details>
