Abstract (英文): Learning an unfamiliar visual category requires more than adapting a classifier: the model must connect its visual evidence to a useful semantic name. A pre-trained vision-language model can retain discriminative image features while providing a weak textual anchor for the target concept. We address this imbalance through context-grounded semantic anchoring. Our framework, SynerNet, learns concept-specific name embeddings, exposes them to compatible linguistic contexts, and conditions their representations on visual evidence. Difficulty-aware processing and adaptive objective weighting coordinate this adaptation while retaining the pre-trained backbone. On four VISTA-Beyond domains, SynerNet reaches a mean 16-shot accuracy of 74.35%, compared with 72.45% for few-shot name learning. Across 14 domains in the name-masked transfer setting, it improves mean accuracy from 45.17% to 46.74%. Ablations identify concept-specific name embeddings and cross-modal context integration as important components. These results support treating concept acquisition as the construction of visually grounded semantic anchors.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Point cloud models are increasingly deployed through score-only black-box interfaces. Under this access model, test-time rotations outside the training distribution can substantially degrade performance. Although existing mitigations such as equivariant retraining and gradient-based adaptation are effective, they are infeasible without access to model weights or gradients. To address this limitation, we propose CanDO, a test-time canonicalization method that recovers lost performance using only forward queries. Specifically, CanDO searches for an input transformation that maximizes model confidence and uses derivative-free optimization to perform this search without gradients. We further identify three conditions for successful recovery: a training augmentation gap, an accuracy-aligned confidence surrogate, and global search over a multi-modal objective. Across multiple architectures and benchmarks, CanDO recovers 76% of the oracle gap under azimuthal misorientation and 51% under arbitrary SO(3) rotations for object part segmentation. These results show that frozen score-only point cloud models can recover from rotation-induced distribution shifts through post-deployment canonicalization alone.

</details>

Abstract (英文): When zooming between dual cameras on smartphones, prominent discontinuities in both geometric perspective and color rendition inevitably degrade the visual experience of users. To address this issue, we introduce the dual-camera smooth zoom (DCSZ) task, aiming to synthesize continuous intermediate frames for fluid zooming. However, directly applying existing video frame interpolation (VFI) models to this task is severely hindered by substantial domain gaps and the practical intractability of capturing real-world continuous ground-truth. To this end, we propose a novel data synthesis framework to generate high-fidelity DCSZ training sequences, named ZoomGS v2. It explicitly decouples geometric and color transformations from 3D scene representation modeling, thereby enabling extensive data generation from easily accessible single-camera 3D scenes. Furthermore, we develop ZoomFI, a specialized VFI network tailored to the DCSZ task, which leverages complementary bidirectional warping for comprehensive dual-camera information fusion. Extensive experiments on test datasets captured using two mobile phones demonstrate that training with the constructed DCSZ data significantly improves the performance of various VFI models. Moreover, ZoomFI achieves state-of-the-art results in both quantitative metrics and visual quality. The datasets, code, and pretrained models are available at https://hitjunjief.github.io/DCSZ-v2

</details>

Abstract (英文): Change Detection & Captioning (CDC) aims to jointly localize changed regions and describe their semantic content from bi-temporal remote sensing images. While recent approaches have demonstrated the effectiveness of unified modeling, they primarily rely on shared representations or jointly optimized architectures, leaving task-specific communication under heterogeneous supervision less explored. However, detection and captioning differ fundamentally in their supervision granularity, leading to heterogeneous representations with uneven reliability across tasks and spatial locations. In this paper, we reformulate CDC as a selective information routing problem between heterogeneous task branches, where the key challenge is to determine when and where information should be transferred. To this end, we propose CoDeRNet, a unified framework that preserves task-specific representations while enabling bidirectional exchange of complementary information. CoDeRNet introduces a Confidence-Decomposed Routing (CoDeR) mechanism, which decomposes the routing condition into receiver-side need for support and sender-side reliability cues to guide selective transfer. Experiments on LEVIR-MCI and WHU-CDC show that CoDeRNet achieves strong overall CDC performance, particularly improving caption generation while preserving competitive change localization. Extensive analyses further show that the benefits arise not merely from joint learning or generic cross-task communication, but from explicitly modeling selective cross-task information routing. These results highlight the importance of conditional information transfer for unified CDC under heterogeneous supervision.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Document layout analysis (DLA) faces a long-standing dilemma: existing methods either capture rich local details at prohibitive computational cost (e.g., Transformer-based approaches with quadratic complexity) or achieve efficient global modeling but sacrifice fine-grained structural awareness (e.g., state-space models). This trade-off hinders deployment in resource-constrained scenarios. We present LayoutMamba, a hybrid framework that synergistically combines sparse-attention Transformers for localized feature extraction (O(n) complexity) and the recurrent state-space mechanism of Mamba for dilution-free long-range dependency modeling across the 2D document canvas. Our two-stage paradigm first uses a lightweight Transformer encoder to hierarchically distill salient local semantics; second, Mamba propagates global contextual dependencies without attention dilution, enabling precise, efficient, and scalable layout understanding. We further identify a critical yet overlooked issue in existing DLA benchmarks: the inherent low-overlap distribution bias of layout objects, which we mitigate through a novel Geometric Overlap Penalty Loss (GOPLoss), a dual-optimization objective that minimizes redundant bounding box overlaps and encourages geometrically consistent predictions. Extensive experiments on PubLayNet and D4LA show that LayoutMamba surpasses state-of-the-art AP on PubLayNet with 8x fewer parameters and maintains leading performance on D4LA, ranking top-3 among published methods, achieving an unprecedented balance between accuracy, efficiency, and scalability. Our work pioneers a cognition-inspired paradigm for document analysis, advancing practical deployment in edge computing and resource-limited environments.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Reusing pretrained medical-imaging models offers an alternative to training from scratch. A model trained for the same task may perform poorly at a new hospital because imaging protocols and preprocessing differ. Effective adaptation depends on the model architecture, domain shift, and available labels and compute. Adaptation can improve scores on a small labeled set yet reduce performance on unseen data. We present MedAdaptAgent, an automatic harness that turns a supplied model, target data, and a free-text request into a new-site adaptation workflow. Its LLM-driven Adaptation Planner uses model-site evidence to choose compatible experiments and revise subsequent adjustments. The harness requires evidence of improvement over the pretrained model, then delivers the selected model as a complete, runnable package. Evaluation spans 26 segmentation and classification transfers across architectures and compute settings, with segmentation annotation budgets ranging from zero-shot transfer to few-shot adaptation. With 20 local annotations, mean segmentation Dice rises from 0.483 to 0.774 across 10 transfers, a 60.1% relative gain, with no harmful replacements. Mean classification AUROC rises from 0.810 to 0.837 across 13 transfers, a 3.4% relative gain. Comparing adapted models with the pretrained model reduces harmful classification replacements by 30.8% versus selecting the highest-scoring model on the available labels, using identical candidates. Seven public-model deployments demonstrate adaptation and reproducible package delivery across contrast and modality shifts. A CT-to-MRI study extends the evaluation to four LLM planners, including two open-weight models. We release the code on GitHub.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Logical anomaly detection requires recognizing scene components and measuring whether their configuration satisfies task constraints. Correct identities can coexist with inaccurate geometry; clean mask fragments can yield misleading lengths. We propose Semantic-Geometric Decoupling and Evidence Binding (SGDB). A semantic decoder learns object identities from component-annotated normal sources and factual edits; a separate reader supplies final measurements. Partial one-to-one association binds geometric witnesses to identities while retaining unmatched objects in the count. A support-certified reader selects mask-derived axes using line quality and complete component ownership. Explicit task requirements and normal-data references define executable checks, with decision thresholds fixed by independent normal calibration. Execution-grounded explanation traces rule contributions to source objects and measurements, extracting compact sufficient alarm certificates. A frozen language model expresses selected outcomes as cited claims, with numeric evidence retained by the executor. Reports preserve observed deviations and unavailable evidence. SGDB achieves 98.19% AUROC and 94.52% fixed-threshold F1 on five MVTec LOCO logical categories, and 93.56% AUROC and 92.76% F1 across all 50 VID-AD tasks. Frozen-output controls evaluate geometric readout and explanatory sufficiency and compactness.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Training-free few-shot adaptation of vision-language models typically constructs a classifier from CLIP's final projected image representation, leaving intermediate representations unused. Combining them is not straightforward. Class scores from different representations differ in spread by orders of magnitude for the same query. Direct averaging allocates influence by numerical scale rather than predictive information. We propose Layer-Ensemble Gaussian Discriminant Analysis (LE-GDA), a method that fits regularized shared-covariance discriminants independently to intermediate and final representations extracted in one frozen encoder pass. LE-GDA standardizes each discriminant's class scores per query, selects a small representation multiset on labeled validation data, and combines it with the zero-shot classifier without gradient-based optimization. Across 11 datasets, four CLIP backbones, five shot counts, and three independently sampled K-shot training sets, LE-GDA achieves the highest mean accuracy of all evaluated methods on every backbone, raising the 16-shot mean from 78.01% to 79.54% over a matched GDA-CLIP (final-feature) baseline and improving that baseline for every backbone-shot combination. At one shot, LE-GDA retains a 1.20-point mean gain over the baseline even though covariance estimation is unavailable and per-query standardization is neutral. A control with richer per-class structure inside a single representation does not reproduce the gain, indicating that the benefit comes from combining representations. These results show that intermediate CLIP representations carry information beyond the final embedding and can improve training-free few-shot adaptation. Code and evaluation resources are available at https://github.com/pavodi-NM/le-gda.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Diffusion models enable multiple advertisers to compete for exposure within a shared generated image. Yet existing approaches are computationally costly when generating images under varying target-share configurations, and it remains unclear how the resulting outcomes should be evaluated and priced. To address these gaps, we propose DiffAuction, a framework integrating controllable generation, click-intent evaluation, and auction design. First, our training-free Residual Share Calibration (RSC) decomposes joint-attention updates into product-specific residuals and rescales them according to target shares. We further introduce Share-Conditioned Prompting (SCP) to adapt the viewpoint to each desired allocation. Second, we develop an evaluator that uses a VLM to extract visual evidence and an LLM to assess Click-Through Intent (CTI), a proxy for click propensity, along four dimensions: Product Prominence, Brand Recognizability, Scene Integration, and Contextual Value Communication. We validate its assessments against judgments from 10 trained annotators on 1000 images. Third, we formulate this advertising setting as an auction over jointly feasible generation configurations. Each configuration has a public expected CTI vector, while advertisers hold configuration-specific private values per unit of CTI that map click intent into commercial value. We adopt a learnable affine maximizer auction (AMA), which jointly selects a shared generation configuration and determines advertiser-specific payments to maximize expected platform revenue. The resulting allocation and payment rules guarantee dominant-strategy incentive compatibility (DSIC) and individual rationality (IR). Experiments show that our generation method strengthens the monotonic relationship between target shares and CTI relative to baselines, while the learned AMA achieves higher platform revenue in the resulting auction setting.

</details>

Abstract (英文): In surgical instrument segmentation, the increasing variety of instruments over time poses a significant challenge for existing neural networks, as they are unable to effectively learn such incremental tasks and suffer from catastrophic forgetting. When learning new data, the model experiences a sharp performance drop on previously learned data. Although several continual learning methods have been proposed for incremental understanding tasks in surgical scenarios, the issue of data imbalance often leads to a strong bias in the segmentation head, resulting in poor performance. Data imbalance can occur in two forms: (i) class imbalance between new and old data, and (ii) class imbalance within the same time point of data. Such imbalances often cause the dominant classes to take over the training process of continual semantic segmentation (CSS). To address this issue, we propose SurgCSS, a novel plug-and-play CSS framework for surgical instrument segmentation under data imbalance. Specifically, we generate realistic surgical backgrounds through inpainting and blend instrument foregrounds with the generated backgrounds in a class-aware manner to balance the data distribution in various scenarios. We further propose the Class Desensitization Loss by employing contrastive learning to correct edge biases caused by data imbalance. Moreover, we dynamically fuse the weight parameters of the old and new models to achieve a better trade-off between the biased and unbiased model weights. To investigate the data imbalance problem in surgical scenarios, we construct a new benchmark for surgical instrument CSS by integrating datasets. Extensive experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed framework, achieving significant performance improvement against existing baselines.

</details>

Abstract (英文): An action seen once and the same action seen three times receive the same action-recognition label, so that supervision does not require a model to count. Answers shared across histories that differ in event order, accumulation, or relations leave those distinctions unconstrained; we call this state under-specification. Visible-State Supervision (ViSS) addresses it by training on source-grounded probes of event identity, order, accumulation, relation change, and answer sufficiency; under the same recent-frame interface it improves OVO-Bench over a strict-matched generic temporal supervision baseline while remaining comparable on StreamingBench, with the largest gain in hallucination detection. Test-Time Causal View Search (TVS) then adapts evidence access while keeping the learned model fixed, expanding from recent frames to sparse event and compact history views on demand. The two interventions separate representation from view selection: supervision particularly benefits hallucination detection and causal reasoning, access particularly benefits event recall and forward reasoning. Together they reach the best reported accuracy among the published systems compared here on StreamingBench and OVO-Bench at a fraction of the processed frames of exhaustive selection, and strict replay shows that most of the remaining gap to a three-view oracle is a correct answer that was later replaced. Streaming accuracy depends on what supervision asks the model to keep, what evidence inference exposes, and whether the model keeps a correct answer once it has one.

</details>

Abstract (英文): RGB-X tracking complements RGB appearance with thermal, depth, or event observations, but centralized training requires collecting multimodal videos at a single location. Federated training keeps raw videos local, but sensor heterogeneity can create a mismatch between parameter-sharing domains and the clients contributing to them. This mismatch is particularly relevant when RGB-only clients coexist with sensor-equipped clients: their RGB observations remain useful, but they provide no auxiliary measurements for learning sensor-dependent interactions. We propose Fed-MAPTrack, a modality-aware federated prompt tuning framework for RGB-X tracking. The pretrained tracking backbone and prediction head are frozen, and clients optimize and exchange only prompt-related parameters. The server aggregates RGB-shared parameters globally and maintains separate thermal, event, and depth banks for auxiliary-specific, fusion, and normalization parameters. Selective participation retains RGB-only clients' contributions to the global RGB bank while excluding their fusion and normalization updates from modality-specific aggregation. Across three matched training repetitions under a fixed client partition, Fed-MAPTrack improves the mean FedAvg scores by 2.35 AUC points on LasHeR, 3.45 SR points on VisEvent, and 2.11 F-score points on DepthTrack. Among the nine evaluated methods, it achieves the highest mean thermal AUC and depth F-score, while its mean event SR is 0.07 points below that of Independent FL. Controlled ablations show that extending the bucketing scope without a compatible participation rule can reduce performance. These results highlight the need to jointly define the sharing domain and eligible contributors for each parameter group.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Dynamic Gaussian reconstruction requires compact scene features to support coordinated changes in geometry and appearance. A factorized grid provides efficient local queries, but accurate interpolation alone does not ensure that the resulting dynamic attributes remain compatible across space and time. We introduce DimFlow, a framework that learns these features through bidirectional interaction between a multi-resolution grid and a coordinate-based expert branch. Feature-level adversarial alignment connects their distributions, adaptive fusion transfers complementary information, and activation-masked residual feedback refines the grid during training. Four attribute experts organize deformation, rotation, opacity, and temporal information before Gaussian rendering. In the aggregate comparison across D-NeRF, HyperNeRF, and DAVIS, DimFlow achieves 39.73 dB PSNR, improving on 4D-GS by 1.92 dB while matching its reported 85 FPS rendering speed. The model occupies 18.9 MB, compared with 18.3 MB for 4D-GS. Component and attribute-expert ablations reveal complementary contributions from feature alignment, adaptive exchange, and temporal processing.

</details>

Abstract (英文): GUI agents built on vision-language models interact with continuously evolving interfaces, yet conventional inference repeatedly recomputes representations of largely overlapping observations and histories. This mismatch makes multi-step interaction costly, while changes in context, interface content, and action arguments prevent straightforward cache reuse. We introduce GUIFlow, a framework for full-pipeline incremental inference in multi-image GUI interaction. Our central insight is that advancing a decision need not require rebuilding all supporting representations: each interaction step can instead selectively update computational states maintained across the trajectory. To support new decisions as the context evolves, GUIFlow adapts how past computation is reused: historical KV states are position-aligned and injected into the current context; change signals guide selective updates of current-observation states in the vision encoder and language model; and earlier action responses serve as speculative drafts verified by the target model. Together, these mechanisms enable cross-step reuse throughout visual encoding, prefill, and decoding without additional training, a separate draft model, or changes to the model-visible inputs and action interface. Experiments demonstrate up to 3.39x geometric-mean speedup across offline multi-image benchmarks and up to 3.49x in online, closed-loop interaction on OSWorld, while maintaining task performance broadly comparable to the dense baseline.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Camera-based bird's-eye view (BEV) perception methods such as Fast-BEV have demonstrated promising results with fast inference speed and competitive accuracy. However, their robustness to real-world corruptions remains insufficiently explored, posing risks for autonomous driving deployment where environmental variations are inevitable. In this paper, we propose Sparse Recovery Bird's-Eye View Model Soup (SR-BEVS), a sparse recovery learning approach that simultaneously improves clean accuracy and corruption robustness without requiring additional data or architectural modifications. Our method identifies pruning candidates based on layer-wise weight magnitudes, applies random pruning to create diverse sparse models, and retrains each model with a randomly sampled learning rate. By averaging these diverse recovered models into a single dense model, we achieve enhanced robustness against various corruptions while introducing zero inference overhead. We further provide a mode connectivity analysis demonstrating that our pruning-based diversification preserves the connected low-loss basin necessary for stable weight averaging. Experiments on the nuScenes dataset demonstrate up to 33.8% improvement in corruption robustness and a 1.8% gain in clean accuracy over the Fast-BEV baseline, with consistent improvements across sensor noise, weather distortions, and illumination changes. Our simple yet effective approach may be applicable to other BEV models.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Road anomalies such as collisions, violations, and sudden hazards demand more from video understanding than detection: a practical system must verify what happened, describe the scene, and explain the cause. We find these competences pull in opposite directions for vision-language models: closed-form questions reward precise, evidence-grounded reasoning, while open-ended answers are scored by similarity to reference text, where matching the annotation style matters as much as reasoning correctly. We present Reason-or-Recite (RoR), a two-specialist framework for the Traffic Anomaly Reasoning track of the 2026 AI City Challenge. RoR (1) rewrites the official chain-of-thought annotations into a structured, temporally grounded schema and LoRA-tunes a 9B vision-language model on the enhanced traces; (2) fine-tunes a 4-bit-quantized 27B model on answers alone, absorbing the annotation style; and (3) selects between the two candidates per question with a training-free LLM judge. RoR ranks first on the in-domain leaderboard with mean score 0.6788.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Long-video generators increasingly retrieve past visual observations to prevent identity drift. The intuition is compelling: more access to history should make future video more consistent. But an image can be faithful to the past and wrong for the present. Does visual memory always improve the generated story? We investigate this question through VMem-Bench, a two-track diagnostic study. Track-A measures which references are selected from 92 real films (16,548 segments), before generation and without generator confounds. Track-B compares four retrieval policies with no-memory and gold-informed last-seen controls on 30 causally generated stories (2,824 segments) using a shared generator. Retrieval increases entity recall from 0.559 to 0.628-0.693 and improves similarity to past appearances, but decreases precision from 0.977 to 0.915-0.938 and forbidden-entity avoidance from 0.968 to 0.845-0.898. It can also reinforce obsolete appearances after the story changes them. Conversely, high long-gap recall can arise without memory when the prompt itself supplies the appearance; an appearance-stripping analysis exposes this confound. These results distinguish historical fidelity from current-story correctness and show why retrieval relevance or final-video consistency alone does not establish memory utility.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Sparse-view 4D reconstruction is highly under-constrained when only a few cameras are available. Generative priors can recover missing appearances, but video models are computationally impractical for iterative reconstruction, whereas single-view models produce temporally inconsistent pseudo-observations. Moreover, directly absorbing uncertain generative outputs into Gaussian optimization often creates redundant primitives and floating artifacts. To address these challenges, we propose DUET4D, a self-corrective coupled guidance framework for sparse-view 4D Gaussian splatting. The core of our approach is a self-corrective framework where the 4D representation guides generative prior for spatio-temporal consistent supervisions. We first construct sequential alignments from current Gaussian evidence to steer the latent restoration. Then we use Sequential Fidelity Resampling to lift generative supervisions into Gaussian space to selectively distinguish repeatedly supported primitives. The corrected representation then provides more reliable Gaussian evidence for the next restoration cycle. Extensive experiments demonstrate that DUET4D significantly improves rendering quality and rendering efficiency.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Intelligent operating rooms require accurate awareness of surgical workflow for better intra-operative decisions, teamwork, and surgical documentation. Therefore, accurately recognizing surgical phase during surgery is crucial. However, previous artificial intelligence-based methods provide fluctuating predictions for the transitions, restricting their clinical use. To address this challenge, we propose a clinically meaningful framework that integrates a Swin Transformer and Temporal Transformer Encoder (TTE) for effective surgical phase recognition and a parallel YOLOv11s network for real-time surgical instrument localization. To avoid phase flickering and to produce stable workflow predictions, a causal temporal smoothing is applied. The proposed framework is tested on the Cholec80 dataset for the simultaneous recognition of the surgical phases and instruments. The overall phase recognition accuracy of this model was 92%, and the macro average F1-score was 0.86. This performance surpasses CNN based methods such as EndoNet (81.7%) and SV-RCNet (85.3%), as well as temporal convolutional and transformer-based approaches including TeCNO (88.6%), Trans-SVNet (90.9%), and LoViT (91.5%). Besides, the module of instrument detection achieved mAP@0.5 = 0.863. The reliability of the results is confirmed, obtaining a high recognition rate of the workflows, and a high accuracy of the localization of the instruments. The proposed framework can serve as a foundation for intelligent surgical assistance, enriching situation awareness, and potential robotic surgery applications. Code will be made publicly available at: https://github.com/ac005sheekar/LapChol-Surgery-Swin-Cholec80

Abstract (英文): Text-steerable visual encoders condition a frozen Vision Transformer on a natural-language prompt and point at the matching image region, producing prompt-conditioned heatmaps and features. We audit the released SteerViT model and find that its steering interface turns language-driven pointing into demographic profiling. On counterfactual face mosaics and real multi-person photographs, "the person" selects a man more often than the base rate, crime words select Black people more often than occupation words do, "the man" among four women selects the Black woman 75.6% of the time on SocialCounterfactuals, and "the monkey" selects Black people. None of them is in the backbone's unsteered saliency, and the crime association is not in the frozen text encoder; the race effects grow with steering strength and enter through the last two cross-attention layers: giving the prompt to those layers alone reproduces them. Four other grounding models, CLIPSeg, Grounding-DINO, SAM 3, and Qwen2.5-VL, show no crime routing and weaker race routing. The model cannot refuse. Its patch softmax must place its mass somewhere, and its heatmap separates a groundable request ("the police officer") from one that is absent ("the pizza") or that would require a stereotyped demographic inference ("the criminal") only at chance (AUROC 0.52). We introduce SafeSteer, which adds a learned abstention output and counterfactual-invariance training on the same 23M steering parameters. SafeSteer separates should-point from should-abstain prompts at AUROC 0.997, generalizes to held-out sensitive and absent words (0.998; at a 0.5 threshold it refuses 99.8% of trained and 62% of held-out sensitive words) with no over-refusal of visible people on the benchmark, and removes most race routing while keeping localization and retrieval within 1 point of the released model. Code, benchmarks, and the curated mosaic sets will be released.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Monocular depth estimation has advanced rapidly with the development of foundation models that demonstrate strong zero-shot generalisation across diverse visual domains. However, the applicability of these models to visual perception for lower-limb prosthetics remains largely unexplored. Reliable depth perception is particularly important for prosthesis users, who often experience significant challenges when navigating stairs, uneven terrain, and other complex environments. To address this gap, we introduce the Lower-Limb Prosthesis (LP) dataset, a benchmark designed to evaluate depth perception from an emulated knee-level prosthetic viewpoint. Using this dataset, we evaluate four state-of-the-art monocular depth estimation foundation models. Our results provide the first systematic benchmark of monocular depth estimation performance from a prosthetic viewpoint, while highlighting both the challenges and the potential of deploying foundation models in prosthetic perception systems. The code and dataset are available at https://github.com/Ishivijay/lp_dataset.

Abstract (英文): Small uncrewed underwater vehicles are proliferating and threaten maritime security, yet evade sonar and magnetic sensors built for larger submarines. Infrared thermal wakes, generated by hydrodynamic displacement that modulates the cold-skin layer, offer a non-acoustic, non-magnetic alternative even without cooling-water discharge. Existing studies, however, rely on idealized simulations that fail to capture real-world backgrounds, offering limited guidance for target-background discrimination. We establish AVP-IR-Wake, the first large-scale, real-world, physically diverse benchmark of 161,485 frames collected over three years. We show that thermal wakes are extended structures of discrete vortices, violating the compactness assumption of conventional targets. We reveal local indistinguishability: individual wake vortices are statistically identical to wind-induced background vortices in temporal, frequency, and sparse domains, sharing low-frequency information and long-range correlations. This challenges the assumptions of compact, transient, high-frequency targets in conventional detection. Yet we establish global morphological separability: although locally inseparable, the wake as a whole is organized by vehicle trajectory, whereas background clutter is organized by wind-driven thermal fronts. This explains why representative detectors fail and shows that reliable detection must shift from local feature extraction to global trajectory-constrained physical modeling. Because the cold-skin layer varies globally and seasonally, forecasting wake observability will require integrating physical oceanography and atmospheric science, linking underwater surveillance to the changing ocean surface.

</details>

Abstract (英文): World models are emerging as a fundamental component of policy learning for real-world agents, enabling agents to predict future world states and plan actions within a learned representation of the dynamic environment. Existing approaches, however, often model appearance, metric geometry represented by projected LiDAR depth, and ego motion either partially or independently, overlooking the fact that these modalities are different projections of a shared underlying world state. This separation can lead to information loss and cross-modal inconsistency, ultimately limiting downstream performance. Motivated by this unified view of the world, we propose DriveUniTok, a unified multimodal tokenizer for driving world-action models (WAMs) that encodes these modalities in a single decodable continuous latent. We freeze DriveUniTok and adapt a pretrained diffusion model, DriveUniWAM, to predict future unified latents; the tokenizer then decodes them into future appearance, projected LiDAR depth, and ego trajectories. Experiments on NAVSIM demonstrate that our unified design achieves better future prediction and planning performance at lower downstream computational cost than separate-stream alternatives. With reinforcement learning post-training, DriveUniWAM achieves a PDMS of 91.54, reaching state-of-the-art performance among the evaluated WAM-based planners.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Vision-language models (VLMs) ground a referring expression in one of two ways. They decode the box as coordinate tokens, which is precise but takes one sequential step per token, or they regress it from a hidden state with a small head, which takes one step but loses accuracy at high IoU. The two fail differently: a head usually finds the right object but places its edges loosely, while coordinate tokens place edges precisely. We therefore use the regression head as a speculative drafter for the model's own coordinate tokens. Locus writes the head's box as coordinate tokens and checks them with the same backbone by Jacobi iteration over a shared image prefix. The fixed point is the model's greedy coordinate-token answer, reached in about 5.60 sequential passes on average instead of 18.46 decoding steps; head and verifier are one set of weights, trained jointly. On the 8 RefCOCO, RefCOCO+ and RefCOCOg splits, verification raises Locus-2B's Acc@0.9 from 54.87 for its own head to 59.13, and the one-pass head is ahead at Acc@0.5 of every published model with at most 3B parameters except Florence-2-L. Against Jag, the strongest open regression-head model, at its own size and training budget, Locus ties at Acc@0.5 and, with typical acceptance, gains +4.52 points at Acc@0.9, with paired significance tests. Exact answers come 1.5x faster than vLLM greedy-decoding the same coordinate tokens with the same weights; a pure regression head remains faster still.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Tube-wise video completion reconstructs temporal trajectories at spatial locations unobserved in every frame. We propose a two-stage framework combining regularized masked Kronecker product decomposition (KPD) with cross-fitted self-supervised residual learning. First, we estimate a compact structural backbone directly from observed entries using cyclic component-wise least-squares updates, spatial and temporal factor regularization, and regularizer-optimal scale balancing. The exact updates yield a nonincreasing objective. Second, we address the mismatch between in-sample fitting errors and the prediction errors encountered at missing tubes. We partition observed tubes into folds and construct residual targets using backbone models fitted without the corresponding target tubes. A lightweight 3-D convolutional neural network then predicts corrections from the backbone, visible residual context, observation mask, and local detail features. Exact data consistency preserves observed measurements. Experiments include ablations, sensitivity analysis, grayscale and RGB video completion, and structured missing patterns. Our method achieves the highest mean PSNR and SSIM at the higher tested sampling rates on the grayscale benchmark. On eight selected DAVIS RGB sequences, it achieves the highest mean missing-region PSNR and full-frame SSIM and the lowest mean missing-tube temporal-gradient MAE at every evaluated sampling rate.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Vision-language navigation (VLN) requires an embodied agent to follow language instructions through 3D environments. Maintaining persistent episodic memory remains a challenge for VLM-based streaming agents in long-horizon navigation. We present MEMVLN, a streaming episodic memory framework that maintains a bounded bank of compressed perceptual features annotated with timesteps and action-derived relative poses, and produces compact memory tokens via cross-attention. We pair the proposed memory with a frozen pretrained geometry encoder. To learn the memory module, we design a masking-based trajectory summarization task with multi-granularity sub-trajectory decomposition, encouraging route reconstruction exclusively from episodic memory. Under navigation training restricted to the official R2R/RxR trajectories, MEMVLN achieves state-of-the-art performance on most reported metrics on standard VLN-CE benchmarks. Long-horizon analyses further characterize when episodic memory is useful. For physical evaluation, we collect 30 instruction-conditioned episodes in one physical scene, use 24 as demonstrations for post-training, and hold out six for testing. On the held-out episodes, MEMVLN improves success over the VLM baseline from 17% to 40%, and post-training further raises it to 47%.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Blendshape generation for 3D models has been extensively researched, but very few works address generating blendshapes for models with multiple disconnected parts. We present a case study of generating fixed facial expression blendshapes for commercial anime-style 3D character models with up to 11 parts per face. To make sure that our system can handle models in a way that is agnostic to topology, we train neural networks to generate deformation fields around the input meshes. We employ at least one network to independently process each part, and the relative positions of their outputs are coordinated by having each network also process the geometry of an anchor part. We use two mechanisms to improve accuracy: having the networks predict whether the output displacement is zero or not, and classifying blendshapes into different size groups and dedicating a network to each group. We also introduce a method to improve blendshape quality by enhancing bilateral symmetry. Our method is simpler than recent neural-network-based works and solves a real problem of the user community of these models.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Understanding and operating with scientific charts is a standard approach in data analysis, and while humans are good at interpreting such charts, open vision-language models (VLMs) are known to perform poorly when it comes to question answering based on such data types. Motivated by an initial observation that many of their errors are due to inexact answering, we raise the following natural question: how much accuracy can we recover without changing the model at all? In this paper, we study this question by searching over inference programs and the code around a "frozen" version of the model. Using ideas from autonomous evolution (famously used in AlphaEvolve), we first start from a baseline inference program, and in each round we show an LLM judge the code together with a few failures on training questions, and ask for an improving evolutionary move. As usual in autonomous evolution approaches, the candidate programs are kept in a small pool ranked by training accuracy, and the next edit is performed on top-scorers from this pool. We run this approach on three VLMs and four popular benchmarks on scientific charts, and we measure the fraction of descriptive questions whose answer exactly matches the ground truth. The accuracy improves in all experimental settings. On average, it rises from 16.6% with the starting program to 41.3% with the evolved one, and the largest single increase is 55.2 percentage points. As a side benefit, our evolved program is 3.8x faster than the starting program, because short answers are easier to grade and faster to generate. Our results demonstrate that a large part of the observed errors arise due to the inference pipeline, and that optimizing it can improve the "frozen" VLM without the need to retrain it.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Unlocking medieval archives at scale is bottlenecked by the cost of line-level diplomatic transcriptions and manual abbreviation expansion. Focusing on the registers of Pope Honorius III (1216-1227) over 5,000 letters surviving in full only as manuscript images - we show that existing, line-break-free expert transcriptions (edition drafts) suffice to train end-to-end hand-written text recognition (HTR) that jointly transcribes and expands heavily abbreviated Latin. Using forced alignment on 860 unaligned edition entries across 394 pages of Reg. Vat. 9, we automatically bootstrap 13,122 training lines without manual alignment of segmented lines. While the generic CATMuS baseline struggles on this domain (18.9% CER) and a modular space-free HTR + word-segmentation pipeline (cf. Camps et al. 2021) plateaus at 15.6% WER, our from-scratch hybrid Conformer - combining CTC with bidirectional subword attention decoders, iterative self-realignment and n-best LM rescoring - achieves 4.7% CER and 8.7% WER (8.4% WER with ROVER system combination), nearly halving errors on clause-final words (22.7% to 12.2% WER). These figures are measured on the test lines that align with the transcription (86% of all test lines for the best hybrid). Furthermore, we address a tension between paleographic fidelity and editorial normalization: training on targets that preserve scribal name initials reduces the WER against the manuscript text on the 31 affected test lines from 13.3% to 9.2%, at a small cost against the expanded transcription (WER +0.3 points, CER +0.4). Finally, extensive ablations show that none of an auxiliary diplomatic CTC head, a copy mechanism, a permutation decoder or masked-image pretraining improves on direct end-to-end supervision, while strict padding-mask propagation and per-line beam termination make inference batch-invariant.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Test-Time Prompt Tuning (TPT) adapts large pretrained models to unseen target distribution shifts by updating only lightweight prompt tokens. Existing TPT methods are effective, but they mainly optimize each prompt update locally. Across test-time steps, prompts updated at the current step are reused for the corresponding predictions and updates, so noisy or biased unlabeled objectives can accumulate and destabilize the prompt-update trajectory. We identify two failure modes: magnitude expansion, where updates move the prompt too far, and directional drift, where consecutive updates point in inconsistent directions. To analyze and control these failures, we propose Test-Time Prompt-Agnostic Decomposition ($\mathtt{TPD}$), which characterizes observed prompt-update trajectories from a dynamical-systems view and decomposes base prompt updates into magnitude and direction. $\mathtt{TPD}$ computes a spectral radius to measure update expansion and decomposes update directions into persistent, oscillatory, and residual components. Building on this decomposition, Adaptive Koopman Control ($\mathtt{AKC}$) regulates update magnitude by shrinking updates under expansive recent dynamics, while Hankel Update Router ($\mathtt{HUR}$) refines update direction by preserving persistent components and suppressing oscillatory and residual components. Together, $\mathtt{AKC}$ and $\mathtt{HUR}$ produce a stabilized prompt update. As a prompt-agnostic framework, $\mathtt{TPD}$ can be plugged into visual and text TPT methods without modifying the backbone, prompt architecture, or adaptation loss. Experiments on 15 datasets show that $\mathtt{TPD}$ consistently improves 10 baselines, with accuracy gains of $3$--$8\%$ for visual prompts and over $2\%$ for text prompts.

</details>

Abstract (英文): For safety of the intended functionality (SOTIF) analysis according to ISO 21448, a machine learning object detector for light detection and ranging (LiDAR) point clouds can meet a performance target, a limit on the mean per-frame share of wrong detections among accepted detections, by rejecting detections above an uncertainty threshold. A threshold selected on data of one condition carries a statistical guarantee only for data distributed like its selection data, whereas SOTIF analysis covers every triggering condition. This paper presents an uncertainty evaluation in which the threshold is transferred into a triggering condition, a test decides at a stated error probability whether the target is applicable there, not applicable or not determined, and a lower bound counts the independent units an applicable outcome requires. For PointPillars ensembles on KITTI and nuScenes, counted in recorded sequences, as the dependence between frames requires, neither dataset held enough independent units for any target limit, and no transfer had a not applicable outcome. Counted in frames, the same data gave not applicable outcomes at limits fixed in advance after transfers into far distance, into rain and between two separately trained detectors, thresholds that met the target limit after a transfer rejected most correct detections, and in rain the area under the receiver operating characteristic curve did not decrease. A performance target met under one condition therefore counts as evidence under a triggering condition only after a test there, reported with its share of accepted correct detections and its number of independent recorded sequences.

</details>

Abstract (英文): A cooperative 3D detector can miss a vehicle even when the LiDAR points on it remain unchanged. We study this failure under adverse weather as Weather-Induced Context Interference. To our knowledge, this is the first controlled study of how weather-related exterior changes remove cooperative benefits while preserving all points inside annotated objects. Controlled experiments on simulated and real roadside inputs show that exterior loss changes object representations before fusion and removes previously useful assistance. On real inputs, restoring object-neighborhood features recovers 30 of 31 induced failures; three equal-area exterior controls recover none. The failure condition also appears with a second fusion model. These findings motivate Adverse-Weather Cooperative Repair (AWCR), with separate implementations for the available supervision. Point Classifier filters added weather returns in simulation. On real inputs, Local Correction refines candidates and Quality Selection checks them against current measurements, without naturally paired clear/adverse-weather recordings. Candidate tracing shows that correction supplies accurate geometry for some targets that original candidates cannot localize at the evaluation threshold. On natural adverse-weather CoInfra development data, AWCR improves car bird's-eye-view (BEV) AP@0.7 by 4.66 points with V2X-ViT and 5.56 with AttFuse. On simulated V2X-R fog, it improves vehicle BEV AP@0.7 by 4.47 points at a fixed 4,096-candidate budget.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Positron Emission Tomography (PET) provides important metabolic information for tumor detection and staging but remains significantly less accessible than Computed Tomography (CT) due to its high cost, infrastructure requirements, and reliance on injected radioactive tracers. CT-to-PET image translation using generative models aims to address this disparity, yet state-of-the-art diffusion-based methods are limited by the scarcity of paired training data. To overcome this, we propose a domain-adapted latent diffusion framework that transfers natural-image priors using a relatively small dataset of 21,930 paired CT/PET slices, incorporating a signed power transform to resolve extreme intensity sparsity in PET data. Compared to CPDM, a state-ofthe-art baseline for CT-to-PET image translation, our approach yields superior performance in pixel-wise reconstruction and localized tumor fidelity, demonstrating effective transfer of natural-image priors to the medical domain. We further provide a systematic analysis of evaluation metric reliability for sparse PET data, showing that standard full-image metrics can be misleading indicators of translated image quality, and propose background-filtered and mask-restricted metrics as more clinically meaningful alternatives.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Understanding the envelope of shape variation encompassing both sexual dimorphism and growth-related changes in living tissue is a non-trivial task. In this work, we use statistical shape models (SSMs) and analyze demographic-stratified shape parameters to quantify sexual dimorphism and sex-specific shape changes. To this end, we developed a two-stage detection-segmentation framework that accurately segmented $n = 27664$ unique bone masks from a radiographic dataset of $n = 1456$ subjects with diverse ages and sexes (HD95 = $1.512 \pm 0.005$, ASSD $0.449 \pm 001$, Dice = $0.987 \pm 0.00004$, IoU = $0.975 \pm 0.0001$). Following SSM fitting, our follow-up analyses of stratified shape parameters highlighted the morphology of sexual dimorphisms for each bone and demonstrated sex-specific differences in shape development. Our results were consistent with existing literature and provided an unambiguous, data-driven methodology for characterizing tissue shape and its population-scale variation. This approach provides a potential methodological foundation for developing population-based models of anatomical variation and could be extended to investigate tissue growth and shape changes over time. Future work should evaluate the applicability of this methodology on alternative anatomical structures and imaging modalities, with the goal of validating these growth trajectories on longitudinal data.

Abstract (英文): Recent advances in generative video models have improved visual realism, making detection increasingly dependent on subtle temporal inconsistencies. However, these signals are weak and spatially localized, while most of the video content is redundant. This creates a fundamental mismatch: existing detectors rely on heavy backbones to model full videos, which leads to high computational cost and limited scalability. In this work, we rethink AI-generated video detection from a representation perspective. Instead of modeling entire videos, we restructure them into compact temporal slice representations that isolate informative temporal evolution. We introduce a lightweight framework using frequency-aware temporal slice consistency learning, where informative spatial rows and columns are aggregated over time to form slice-domain inputs. This reparameterization suppresses redundant appearance while preserving discriminative temporal dynamics, enabling efficient and targeted detection. By constructing slices from both high- and low-frequency regions, our method exposes complementary temporal artifacts, including unstable details and abnormal motion smoothness. We instantiate this idea in a Temporal Slice Consistency Network, which integrates task-driven slice localization, hierarchical positional encoding, and a lightweight Transformer to model cross-slice and cross-frequency dependencies. We further introduce AI-Artist, a new benchmark with artist-curated videos from recent high-fidelity generators, including Seedance and Kling. Experiments on GenVideo and AI-Artist show that our method achieves strong performance while requiring over 400$\times$ fewer model-forward FLOPs and 3.6$\times$ faster model inference than the strong video-based baseline ReStraV (2.9$\times$ faster end to end, including preprocessing), enabling scalable and practical deployment.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Recent studies on generalizable human Gaussian splatting have achieved the notable progress in synthesizing novel views of unseen human subjects from multi-view images. Despite this progress, existing methods still struggle to represent fine-grained details of human subjects, which inherently involve high geometric and textural complexity. This is because previous methods do not impose a constraint on how densely Gaussians are placed, which often leads to blurry rendering results in complex regions. To address this problem, we propose a complexity-guided regularization scheme. The key idea is to leverage a complexity prior computed via the local entropy in geometric and textural characteristics of a clothed human. This effectively prevents Gaussians from being coarsely placed in complex regions, which facilitates the faithful reconstruction of intricate structures. Furthermore, we sample local patches predominantly from complex regions according to this complexity prior, which allows the model to be sufficiently supervised by local details. Experimental results on benchmark datasets demonstrate that our method delivers state-of-the-art performance while maintaining the real-time inference speed in generalizable human Gaussian splatting. The code and model are publicly available at https://github.com/DCVL-3D/CGR-GS_release.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Neural synchrony is hypothesized to help the brain organize visual scenes into structured multi-object representations. In machine learning, synchrony-based models analogously learn object-centric representations by storing binding in the phase of complex-valued features. Rotating Features (RF) instantiate this idea with vector-valued activations, encoding object presence in magnitudes and affiliation in orientations. We propose Orthogonal Rotating Features (OrthoRF), which enforces orthogonality in RF's orientation space via an inner-product loss and architectural modifications. This yields sharper phase alignment and more reliable grouping. In evaluations of unsupervised object discovery, including settings with overlapping objects, noise, and out-of-distribution tests, OrthoRF matches or outperforms current models while producing more interpretable representations, and it eliminates the post-hoc clustering required by many synchrony-based approaches. Unlike current models, OrthoRF also recovers occluded object parts, indicating stronger grouping under occlusion. Overall, orthogonality emerges as a simple, effective inductive bias for synchrony-based object-centric learning.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Existing multi-agent collaboration (MAC) methods have primarily explored a group thinking collaboration topology, where agents independently generate solutions and aggregate their outputs. In this work, we investigate an alternative relay thinking topology, where reasoning ownership is dynamically transferred across agents, allowing subsequent agents to inherit and refine an evolving reasoning trajectory. We introduce Multi-Agent Confidence-guided Relay (MACR), a framework that enables dynamical relay collaboration among agents. The key challenge is determining when reasoning should be relayed and which agent should continue the process. method addresses this challenge through confidence-guided relay triggering and a bidding mechanism that selects the next agent based on its confidence. Experiments on four benchmarks show that method outperforms the strongest single-model strategies in accuracy, e.g., +3.3% on MathVista and +3.9% on MMStar, and remains on par with multi-agent strategies while consuming 6.0x less token overhead on HallusionBench and MMStar.Further analysis demonstrates that relay collaboration enables heterogeneous agents to progressively integrate complementary insights, combining heterogeneous capabilities across reasoning stages. Our source code is available at https://github.com/67L1/MACR.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Medical image segmentation (MIS) remains challenging due to composite degradations and multi-scale geometric variations, which jointly impair lesion-background separability, structural consistency, and optimization stability. Existing single-stream frameworks entangle heterogeneous cues within a single representation pathway, while hybrid-stream approaches often rely on generic or static fusion, limiting effective coordination across domains and failing to explicitly address optimization instability. To overcome these limitations, we propose a new optimization-aware dual-domain framework for MIS (O2D-MedNet). First, a degradation-aware dualdomain interaction (DDI) module integrates Fourier - and Wavelet-domain features to suppress degradation-sensitive responses while preserving structural and fine-detail information. Second, a cross-backbone contour-guided fusion (CGF) module enables selective interaction between CNN-based local detail modeling and visual state-space-based global structure modeling, improving boundary accuracy and representation consistency. Third, a gradient-context optimization (GCO) strategy stabilizes training by adaptively adjusting learning dynamics based on loss trends and gradient statistics, mitigating instability caused by class imbalance and heterogeneous sample difficulty. Extensive experiments on six benchmarks demonstrate consistent improvements over state-of-the-art MIS methods across multiple imaging modalities. Further analyses confirm enhanced sensitivity, structural consistency, and optimization stability, validating the effectiveness of the proposed O2D-MedNet for robust MIS under heterogeneous conditions.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Effective video agents must learn not only how to answer, but also where to look next. Transferring this capability to smaller models is challenging because each observation serves two purposes: supporting an answer and guiding the search for further evidence. Textual descriptions may capture what is currently visible while omitting cues needed for subsequent acquisition. We introduce Latent Agentic Reasoning for Video Analysis (LARVA), a framework that distills multi-round video agents through jointly learned latent reasoning and visual observations. Rather than communicating through captions, an observer passes compact continuous representations to a reasoner that selects the next acquisition action or produces the final answer. Crucially, action and answer supervision propagate through this interface, training the observer to communicate information useful for downstream decisions. Observation reconstruction and reasoning-distribution alignment provide auxiliary supervision from textual teacher trajectories. Across five video-understanding benchmarks, LARVA achieves strong performance with 4B and 7B backbones. On VideoHolmes, Video-MME, and LVBench, the 7B model outperforms a textual CoT student trained on the same teacher trajectories by 5.1, 2.5, and 3.7 percentage points, respectively. These results highlight the value of jointly learning how agents represent evidence and act on it when distilling video-understanding capabilities.

</details>

Abstract (英文): India's historical public archives present severe computer vision and document image analysis challenges: decades of parliamentary records survive only as degraded, multi-column scanned pages featuring mixed scripts, complex layouts, and dense visual noise where OCR failure is the primary obstacle to information retrieval. We present an end-to-end framework that couples vision-based layout segmentation and multi-script OCR with an agentic retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) pipeline. Our approach specifically addresses how visual degradation and layout fragmentation propagate into text extraction errors, using an iterative agentic loop to navigate around OCR noise and layout ambiguities before generating answers. Evaluated on the 200,000-page Punjab Vidhan Sabha archive, our system demonstrates robust handling of historical document degradation, outperforming standard text-only baselines.

Abstract (英文): Emotion recognition in artworks remains challenging because affective meaning is often subjective, implicit, and shaped by relationships between visual form and semantic associations. While vision-language models provide strong multimodal representations, direct image-to-emotion prediction may overlook explicit relations among formal, semantic, and affective cues that support emotional interpretation. This paper proposes Visual-Semantic Affect Graphs (VSAG), a graph-based framework that represents artworks through interpretable feature nodes, including color, brushstroke, composition, light-shadow, line quality, and extracted visual entities. The graph learns feature-emotion associations for dominant emotion prediction, with valence-arousal structure modeled through an optional hierarchical auxiliary affect module. We evaluate VSAG and VSAG-TopK against direct VLM inference using Qwen3-VL and InternVL3.5 on EmoArt-5k. Results show that direct VLMs provide useful but modest baselines, while graph-based models improve dominant emotion prediction. In particular, the hierarchical valence-arousal variant of VSAG achieves the best overall performance, improving accuracy, macro-F1, and weighted-F1 over both direct VLM inference and the emotion-only VSAG baseline. Per-class results indicate that graph-based models and VLMs capture partly complementary signals, while valence-arousal structure is most useful when integrated as a coarse-to-fine affect hierarchy rather than as a simple post-hoc correction. These findings suggest that explicit visual-semantic structure can support both prediction and interpretation in artwork emotion recognition.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Optical coherence tomography (OCT) is a non-invasive imaging modality used for visualization of retinal tissue. However, OCT volumes are affected by speckle noise, which degrades image contrast and obscures fine structural details. Furthermore, modern clinical practice requires large field-of-view, high-resolution OCT volumes, whose storage and transmission are demanding for archiving and teleophthalmology applications. To address aforementioned challenges, we propose a low-rank multi-scale entanglement renormalization ansatz (MERA) tensor network approximation of 3D OCT image volume, where the image volume is represented as contractions of a top core factor encoding global features and multiple interacting low-rank orthogonal or semi-orthogonal factors modeling inter- and intra-B scan correlations. This representation jointly enables compression, denoising, and feature extraction. Unlike deep learning approaches, the proposed approach is training-free and processes each OCT volume independently. We evaluate the proposed framework on OCT image volumes acquired by two different scanners and demonstrate improved visual quality and reduced retinal layer segmentation errors in comparison with several competing methods. We also extract MERA features from two clinical OCT volume datasets. They are subsequently classified using a Grassmann-manifold nearest-subspace classifier. With the subspace dimension as its only hyperparameter, the classifier is theoretically shown to be robust in small-sample clinical scenario. We demonstrate competitive accuracy of classification of retinal diseases in these small-sample settings, where deep learning methods are often limited by the scarcity of labeled data. The proposed structure-aware model-driven representation learning for high-dimensional data under limited supervision provides good generalization performance while maintaining high interpretability.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Monocular occupancy prediction in indoor scenes aims to recover a scene's 3D geometry and semantics from a single RGB image. Existing methods are restricted to a closed set of predefined categories, limiting their ability to generalize to unseen categories. This limitation is particularly critical in indoor scenes, where objects exhibit highly diverse and fine-grained semantic categories. We study open-vocabulary monocular occupancy prediction for indoor scenes and present OVM-Occ, a Gaussian-based occupancy framework with an instance-aware vision-language teacher. Specifically, we adopt Language-Embedded Gaussians as the intermediate scene representation, propose a geometry-aware deformable attention module to refine Gaussian semantic features, and construct instance-level vision-language embeddings to provide reliable semantic supervision. We further introduce geometry-aware decoupled semantic distillation to mitigate the interference between geometry learning and semantic learning. To evaluate open-vocabulary occupancy prediction performance, we introduce OpenOcc-ScanNet and OpenOcc-Replica, two extended benchmarks with more diverse and fine-grained semantic categories. Experiments show that our method achieves state-of-the-art performance in open-vocabulary monocular occupancy prediction for indoor scenes, outperforming the previous state-of-the-art approach by 12.2 mIoU and 0.6 IoU on the Occ-ScanNet dataset, with consistent gains under new open-vocabulary evaluation protocols.

</details>

Abstract (英文): UK Biobank is a British clinical study which, in addition to large amounts of medical data collected, plans to include detailed Magnetic Resonance Imaging (MRI) examina- tions from 100,000 participants. The image data can be analyzed for example by auto- mated analysis with neural networks for image segmentation or classification to expand the collection of image-derived phenotypes for medical research. In this work, we pro- pose, present and evaluate a methodology that uses image-based regression to estimate numerical properties from the collected neck-to-knee water-fat MRI scans. It applies a ResNet50-style network to 2D multi-channel collages that represent targeted and study- specific anatomical sub-regions from the MRI scan. The targeted sub-region sampling is achieved by propagating manually defined subregions from a reference scan to the other >30,000 analyzed scans using image registration. The resulting collages can be compact while retaining high-resolution, for example by providing multiple slices of kidney tissue for a kidney volume regression network. This approach also enables group-wise saliency analysis that highlight image and anatomical regions used by the network for the pre- dictions. The method was applied for the regression endpoints of kidney volume, heart volume, and age. Predictive performance was found between R2 = 0.731-0.984 and the group-wise saliency maps were found to highlight medically relevant regions. We con- clude that the method can be used to standardize and restrict the information presented to a deep regression analysis pipeline, increasing information density and opening up a wide range of future medical inference studies.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Voronoi tessellations have recently emerged as an adaptive, space-partitioning discretization for shape and appearance reconstruction from multi-view images. However, the irregular cells they produce make the repeated reconstruction of the tessellation during optimization costly. We therefore explore adaptive centroidal Voronoi tessellations (CVTs), which extend standard CVTs with non-uniform cell sizes while preserving the regular, well-shaped cells that make them efficient to compute. In CVTSplat, cell size is modulated by the evolving signed distance field, concentrating sites near the zero level set while keeping cells nearly centroidal. This regularity lets us specialize the whole pipeline, with GPU algorithms for nearest-neighbor queries and approximate Voronoi construction, and a rasterizer tailored to small, nearly isotropic cells. Our Voronoi construction is 5 to 10 times faster than general-purpose alternatives in this regime. On DTU and BlendedMVS, CVTSplat reaches geometric accuracy competitive with the best Gaussian-splatting surface methods while training in about 2.5 minutes on a single GPU.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Retinal vessel segmentation is widely used in retinal image analysis, yet model selection based only on aggregate overlap scores can overlook structural fidelity, cross-dataset robustness, and application-specific error costs. We evaluated five executable binary vessel-segmentation pipelines (FSG-Net, SA-UNetv2, DCP-1024, GAVE, and FR-UNet) on 244 images from six public datasets using pixel-level agreement, cross-dataset stability, thin-vessel retention, structural metrics, error composition, and available computational evidence. Results were aggregated with equal weighting across datasets. No pipeline was optimal in every setting. FSG-Net achieved the highest equal-dataset mean Macro-F1 (0.77), IoU (0.63), clDice (0.83), and Skeleton F1 (0.78), whereas SA-UNetv2 achieved the highest mean sensitivity (0.85) and Thin-Vessel Sensitivity (0.63). Differences across datasets were often larger than those among the leading pipelines, and errors shared by all five methods were concentrated near annotated vessel boundaries and faint peripheral vessels. In an ancillary analysis on 45 HRF images, segmentation masks were converted into 11 ring-wise vascular features for leave-one-out classification. Controlled perturbations showed that local mask errors can be amplified in downstream features, while targeted stabilization reduced sensitivity for five features. The results support task-conditioned, benchmark-relative pipeline selection rather than a single universal ranking and highlight the need to validate the selected pipeline on the intended target domain.

</details>

Abstract (英文): 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) has become a promising representation for real-time novel view synthesis and 3D scene reconstruction. For practical deployment, recent studies have explored pose-free feed-forward 3DGS, which reconstructs Gaussian representations directly from unposed input images. However, these methods remain sensitive to residual pose errors, which perturb canonical-space Gaussian transformation, introducing noisy supervision for geometry and attribute learning. To address this problem, we present SG-Splat, a pose-free feed-forward 3DGS framework that improves robustness to pose uncertainty and stabilizes the learning of Gaussian primitives. SG-Splat decouples geometry estimation and Gaussian attribute prediction into two dedicated branches, reducing error coupling between pose-sensitive geometry and Gaussian attributes. We further introduce a coarse-to-fine refinement pipeline, consisting of Flow-Guided Pose Refinement (FGPR) and Residual-Guided Geometry Correction (RGC), to improve geometric alignment before Gaussian attribute reconstruction. Specifically, FGPR refines camera poses with flow-based correlation features and differentiable bundle adjustment. RGC then corrects initial point maps using depth residuals derived from FGPR, producing more reliable Gaussian centers. To stabilize training, we introduce a dual-path strategy that mixes ground-truth and predicted poses for canonical transformation. It provides clean supervision for Gaussian attributes while improving geometry robustness to residual pose errors. Extensive experiments on DL3DV and RealEstate10K show that SG-Splat achieves state-of-the-art performance in both novel view synthesis and pose estimation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Tensor recovery spans heterogeneous data structures and observation processes, calling for a representation that can adapt across modalities and tasks. We propose DiffTR, an unsupervised framework that unifies tensor completion, denoising, and hyperspectral super-resolution through coordinate-conditioned diffusion. Shared factors indexed by tensor coordinates condition a compact score network, and score matching jointly learns the factors and an implicit tensor representation from the available observations. Scalar and vector-valued outputs accommodate individual entries and entire tubes, enabling a common formulation for tabular tensors, images, and videos. The framework separates representation learning from task-specific observation modeling: reverse diffusion recovers missing values; estimation via Tweedie's formula, soft thresholding, and optional spatial consistency support mixed-noise denoising; and measurement-guided sampling transfers a prior learned from low-resolution hyperspectral observations to high-resolution reconstruction, followed by post-sampling refinement with data consistency and spectral subspace regularization. This separation makes the representation compatible with different corruption models and permits task-specific constraints to be incorporated within the same recovery framework. Experiments across multiple tensor modalities and recovery tasks demonstrate competitive reconstruction quality and the flexibility of a unified conditional generative representation. The code is available at https://github.com/CZY-Code/DiffTR

</details>

Abstract (英文): Single-Domain Generalized Object Detection (S-DGOD) aims to generalize from a single source domain to unseen domains under distribution shifts. Existing efforts mainly focus on simulating unseen domains or suppressing domain-specific factors. However, they largely overlook the intrinsic generalization capability embedded in Vision Foundation Models (VFMs). In this work, we show that unconstrained fine-tuning disrupts the geometric structure of pre-trained representations, which encodes domain-invariant semantics. Motivated by this, we propose Geometric-Invariance Fine-Tuning (GIFT), a geometric-aware fine-tuning method for VFMs. Specifically, we introduce a series of learnable Householder reflections to the pre-trained weights to encourage geometry-consistent adaptation under domain shift. In addition, we propose a lightweight channel-wise scaling to facilitate effective task-specific adaptation. Extensive experiments demonstrate that GIFT consistently outperforms state-of-the-art (SOTA) approaches on two S-DGOD benchmarks. Notably, by introducing fewer than 1% additional trainable parameters into the frozen backbone, the proposed method achieves improvements of +7.9% and +2.9% in mPC over the previous SOTA on the Cityscapes-C and Diverse Weather Dataset, respectively. This finding highlights that preserving representation geometry is crucial for robust cross-domain generalization, establishing GIFT as a principled and efficient solution to domain shift.

</details>

Abstract (英文): In the past few years, Vision Transformer (ViT) has achieved excellent performance in the field of computer vision. However, there still some problems remain. For instance, ViTs heavily rely on large-scale training data since they simply contain a large number of trainable parameters, which slows down their learning efficiency and limits their applicability in resource-constrained scenarios. In this paper, we propose a novel deep learning model, namely All-Convolutional Self-Attention Network (ACSAN), which applies convolution operations to replace fully-connected matrices multiplications in the ViT framework. To implement ACSAN, we firstly design Dynamic Convolutional Self-Attention (DCSA) as the basic module, which employs multi-scale dilated convolutions to generate Query, Key, and Value. We defined spatial self-attention and channel self-attention to generate attention features respectively. The two attention features are finally merged as the output of the DCSA module. Based on DCSA, we then construct the basic ACSAN block. Comparing with ViT, we use DCSA to replace the regular self-attention operations, and use convolution layers to replace MLPs. To evaluate the performance of the proposed method, we train ACSAN on three widely-used image classification databases, i.e. CIFAR-10, CIFAR-100 and ImageNet-1k, from scratch. Experimental results show that ACSAN achieves excellent classification accuracy comparing with standard ViT with comparable or fewer parameters, as well as other state-of-the-art methods. Ablation studies verify that each component contributes positively to the final performance.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Recovering an object's 3D structure and camera motion from a few uncalibrated views is a fundamental problem in computer vision. Classical structure from motion methods recover 3D geometry consistent with the input images but cannot recover unseen regions; native generation methods produce plausible 3D objects which are related but not necessarily faithful to the input images. Methods simply concatenating reconstruction and generation allow error to cascade across the two stages, instead of producing a 3D object consistent with both the distribution and the input. We propose GenSfM, which integrates image-based 3D reconstruction with a native 3D diffusion model. In particular, we use a multi-modal diffusion transformer to jointly denoise the shape latent and per-view pose latents under shared self-attention, grounding generated geometry in the observed views while using object-level priors to infer unobserved regions. A compact 3D-native UV-volume tokenization keeps the joint sequence small as views are added, and a pose-aware stage refines geometry and texture by sampling per-voxel image features at the recovered camera poses. On Toys4k and GSO, for $4$ uncalibrated views, GenSfM improves novel-view PSNR by $3.2$-$4.5$ dB and reduces median Chamfer distance by $2.8$-$3.8\times$ over a strong native-3D generation baseline, while recovering input-view poses over $96\%$ Acc@$5^\circ$ without any external pose-estimation backbone, post-hoc optimization, or known intrinsics. Project page: https://dotb1ue.github.io/GenSfm/.

</details>
