Abstract (英文): Soil bidirectional reflectance distribution function (BRDF) is strongly influenced by soil physicochemical properties, yet most hyperspectral retrieval studies rely on single-view reflectance, leaving the value of multi-angle information insufficiently explored. In this study, bidirectional reflectance factors (BRFs) were measured at 48 viewing angles for 156 soil samples collected from a region dominated by Ali-Perudic Argosols. An optimal multi-angle retrieval framework was developed for different soil properties by systematically comparing spectral preprocessing methods, feature extraction, and modeling algorithms. The effects of viewing angle on retrieval accuracy and feature wavelength distribution were further analyzed, and the applicability of simulated multi-angle BRFs generated using SOILSPECT model was further assessed. Results show that the optimal framework for single-view reflectance combines Savitzky–Golay (SG) smoothing, a hybrid random frog–competitive adaptive reweighted sampling (RFROG–CARS) feature selection strategy, and nonlinear support vector machine (SVM) regression. Retrieval accuracy varied substantially with viewing geometry, and no universal optimal viewing angle was identified across soil properties. For multi-angle BRF data, a two-dimensional convolutional neural network (2D-CNN) feature extraction approach outperformed the RFROG–CARS wavelength selection method. Specifically, R 2 (RMSE) improved from 0.64 (8.8 g/kg) to 0.73 (7.7 g/kg) for SOM content, from 0.53 (6.9 %) to 0.61 (6.3 %) for sand content, and from 0.64 (4.7 %) to 0.69 (4.4 %) for silt content, while remaining comparable for cation exchange capacity (0.77/2.4 cmol/kg to 0.76/2.4 cmol/kg) and clay content (0.80/4.3 % to 0.79/4.3 %). Although the retrieval accuracy based on multi-angle BRFs remained slightly lower than that obtained from the optimal viewing geometry, it consistently outperformed most individual viewing angles. The selected multi-angle hyperspectral features were distributed across nearly all viewing angles, indicating complementary spectral–geometric information. Although retrieval accuracy based on simulated BRFs was lower than that based on measured BRFs, the results were broadly comparable. These findings support the use of multi-angle information in soil spectroscopy and remote sensing applications.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate estimation of leaf nitrogen content (LNC) and grain protein content (GPC) in winter wheat is crucial for making fertilization recommendations and assessing grain quality. Previous methods primarily relied on chlorophyll (Chl)-sensitive vegetation indices from the visible and near-infrared domain, while shortwave-infrared spectral features linked to dry matter and nitrogen remain underutilized due to strong leaf water masking. Although existing water removal methods can mitigate the absorption effects of leaf water, the reliability of the resulting simulated dry-canopy reflectance spectra (SDR) is limited by inaccurate leaf mass per area (LMA) retrieval. This study aimed to develop a semi-empirical water removal approach by integrating PRior knowledge and INitialization of leaf water conTent and LMA (PRINT) to simulate SDR, with the objective of improving the estimation performance of LNC and GPC. To achieve this goal, a Random Forest-based hybrid retrieval model was developed to increase LMA retrieval accuracy. Meanwhile, a visible and shortwave-based nitrogen index (VSNI) by combining the visible (proxy of Chl) and shortwave-infrared features was proposed for improving estimation performance of LNC and GPC. Moreover, experimental datasets covering multiple growth stages and geographic sites were collected to improve sample representativeness. These datasets were utilized to improve the retrieval of LMA and evaluate the performance of LNC and GPC estimations. The results demonstrated that the PRINT significantly improved the estimation accuracy of LMA over two standard physically-based inversion models (RMSE: 6.23 g/m 2 vs 8.25 g/m 2 and 45.73 g/m 2 ). Compared with existing VI-based models, the VSNI-based models yielded substantial improvements in LNC (R 2 : 0.62 ∼ 0.65, RMSE = 0.42 ∼ 0.54%) across sites, and enhanced GPC estimation accuracy up to R 2 = 0.66 (RMSE = 0.70%) at the peak filling stage across two years at one site. The proposed methodology advances the spectroscopic understanding of shortwave-infrared responses to nitrogen-related agronomic biochemicals, and holds significant potential for future applications in large-scale growth monitoring of wheat systems using satellite imagery.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The southeastern Tibetan Plateau (SETP), a critical component of the “Asian Water Tower”, hosts a large concentration of maritime temperate glaciers that are highly sensitive to climate change. However, persistent cloud cover, seasonal snow, and supraglacial debris complicate consistent long-term glacier mapping, while the use of static glacier boundaries may introduce temporal mismatches in altimetry-based glacier mass-change estimates. In this study, we developed a multi-source data-fusion framework on Google Earth Engine (GEE) to reconstruct glacier-area changes in the SETP from 2000 to 2025 at five-year intervals and 30 m spatial resolution. Random Forest classifiers were used to integrate Landsat and Sentinel-2 optical imagery, Sentinel-1 synthetic-aperture-radar data, and NASADEM-derived topographic information, supporting glacier delineation under complex surface and atmospheric conditions. The reconstructed glacier area decreased from 8,083 ± 664 km2 in 2000 to 6,228 ± 572 km2 in 2025, corresponding to a cumulative loss of 22.9 ± 3.6% and a mean loss rate of 80.51 ± 10.51 km2 yr−1. The mean area-loss rates were 51.31 ± 6.51 km2 yr−1 during 2000–2010 and 92.99 ± 15.55 km2 yr−1 during 2010–2025, indicating a higher average loss rate in the later period. By combining satellite-altimetry-derived elevation changes from ICESat, CryoSat-2, and ICESat-2 with the reconstructed time-varying glacier areas, we estimated a mean glacier mass-change rate of −6.51 ± 1.22 Gt yr−1 during 2003–2022. Compared with using a fixed 2003 glacier area, the time-varying-area calculation reduced the estimated mass-loss magnitude by 0.63 Gt yr−1, equivalent to 8.8% of the fixed-area estimate. These results indicate that incorporating time-varying glacier areas can reduce temporal mismatch in altimetry-based mass-change estimation and improve the temporal consistency of regional glacier mass-change assessments.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Mangroves are among the most important blue carbon ecosystems, contributing substantially to global carbon sequestration and biodiversity conservation. Canopy carbon (CCC), nitrogen (CNC), and potassium (CKC) contents provide critical indicators of nutrient cycling, ecosystem functioning, and ecological health. However, the structural complexity and strong spatial heterogeneity of mangroves present major obstacles to understanding the spectral response mechanisms and achieving reliable large-scale retrieval of these biochemical parameters. To address these limitations, we developed an interpretable retrieval framework that integrates causal analysis with physical constraints for reliable estimation of mangrove biochemical parameters. First, we proposed a Multi-Channel Chatterjee-Enhanced Correlation Spectrum Analysis (MC-ξ-CS) method that integrates causal discovery to systematically identify spectral bands causally associated with CCC, CNC, and CKC. For large-scale retrieval of mangrove canopy parameters, the identified causal relationships were transferred to satellite observations through a Global Angle-Divergence Spectral Transfer (GADT) framework, yielding robust estimation (R2 = 0.511–0.669). Subsequently, a Dual-Mechanism Guided Physics-Informed Neural Network (DM-PINN) was developed to synergistically integrate causal analysis and physical constraints, achieving robust retrieval performance (R2 = 0.604–0.83) for three representative mangrove species and enhancing the physical interpretability of the results. To the best of our knowledge, this study provides the first comprehensive large-scale retrieval of key mangrove biochemical parameters along the southeastern coast of China, revealing CCC values of 409.18–566.89 g/kg (C/N = 9.77–14.21), CNC values of 14.38–74.9 g/kg (N/K = 1.99–3.86), and CKC values of 4.78–19.67 g/kg (C/K = 28.22–37.67). Distinct inter-provincial spatial patterns were observed: CNC and CKC exhibited similar gradients, increasing from landward to seaward regions, whereas CCC displayed an opposite spatial distribution pattern with lower values in coastal regions. Moreover, CCC was strongly positively correlated with CNC, while CKC showed negative correlations with both parameters. Collectively, this study established a novel theoretical and methodological framework for elucidating the spectral response mechanisms of mangrove biochemical parameters, offering critical scientific support for large-scale mangrove degradation monitoring, ecological restoration assessment, and biodiversity conservation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Long-term deformation of high earth-rockfill dams reflects post-construction consolidation, creep, reservoir loading, and cyclic operation. Separating these superimposed components is essential for gaining process-level insights into spatial patterns and differential responses. This study investigates the Pubugou Dam, a 186 m high earth-rockfill dam in Southwest China, using ascending and descending Sentinel-1 observations from 2017–2023. The two viewing geometries were jointly inverted to recover vertical and east-west displacements. A wavelet-guided decomposition, in which dominant periods identified by the continuous wavelet transform constrain the window length and component grouping in singular spectrum analysis, was proposed to separate the displacement records into long-term trend, annual cyclic, and residual components. Synthetic experiments yielded root mean square errors of 0.69–2.16 mm, indicating effective decomposition performance. Cross wavelet transform and wavelet transform coherence quantified localized coherence and phase lags between the cyclic components and reservoir water level. The central earth-rockfill body exhibits the largest magnitudes of vertical and east-west deformation rates, whereas the spillway and abutments remained comparatively stable. The trend components and settlement indices are consistent with relatively slow long-term post-construction settlement during the observation period. Annual cyclic deformation is more pronounced in the east-west direction and exhibits spatially variable lags relative to reservoir level fluctuations. Settlement inclination and apparent horizontal strain remain low under the adopted criteria, suggesting limited crest-cracking susceptibility during the observation period. This work demonstrates how wavelet-guided decomposition of two-dimensional InSAR time series provides spatially explicit insights into long-term settlement and cyclic deformation of high earth-rockfill dams.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Offshore wind is an important renewable infrastructure supporting the global energy transition, making accurate offshore wind turbine (OWT) mapping essential for understanding infrastructure expansion and informing marine spatial management. Existing long-term approaches often extend single-period detections over time based on structural-stability assumptions, but accumulated identification errors and regional imaging differences limit consistent object-level representation across years and regions. To address this challenge, this study proposes a coordinated three-stage object-level SAR mapping framework, in which a stable and balanced sample representation strategy under unified annual temporal windows provides a consistent data basis across regions and years. On this basis, the lightweight YOLO-SAR model integrates frequency-aware feature modeling and hierarchical structural representation to improve OWT detection under heterogeneous conditions. Compared with YOLO11n, YOLO-SAR reduces the parameter count from 2.58 M to 2.30 M while improving mAP@0.5:0.95 from 0.3956 to 0.4553. Spatial reconstruction then transforms detection outputs into consistent annual object-level OWT maps, forming a continuous spatial database covering 2015–2025. Multi-region validation and comparisons with public datasets further support the reliability of these spatial records. These records reveal multiscale patterns of offshore wind spatial evolution: shifts in regional contributions reshape global growth, regional expansion pathways and internal spatial restructuring exhibit stage-dependent differences, and site-boundary expansion and turbine-spacing adjustments determine local spatial organization. These findings demonstrate that global offshore wind development is not merely a process of quantitative growth, but a multiscale spatial evolution driven by regional shifts, site expansion, and internal spatial reorganization. The proposed framework provides a consistent data foundation for global offshore wind evolution analysis and can be extended to other marine energy infrastructures.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Remote sensing spatiotemporal fusion aims to generate image sequences with both high spatial and temporal resolution. However, many deep learning approaches treat fusion as a black-box regression problem, overlooking differences in sensor imaging physics—such as the point spread function (PSF) and pixel-area integration—which often leads to boundary blurring, spectral mixing, or even physically implausible high-frequency details over heterogeneous landscapes. In addition, these methods commonly rely on paired coarse-/fine-resolution reference images at the same time, which limits data availability. To address these issues, we propose SPP-STF, a superpixel-based fusion network trained with a physics-guided degradation-consistency regularizer that requires only a reference fine-resolution image and a target coarse-resolution observation, while deriving an edge intensity map as a structural prior. The model adopts a U-Net-style, three-branch encoder–decoder to fuse texture, temporal variation, and edge cues at multiple scales. An EdgeResBlock uses high-frequency gating to sharpen boundaries and reduce cross-class leakage, and a superpixel cross-attention Transformer learns object-level correspondences to alleviate alignment errors caused by mixed pixels. During training, we introduce a learnable anisotropic Gaussian PSF-based degradation-consistency term: the predicted fine-resolution output is blurred and downsampled via area integration to match the observed coarse image, and this term is optimized jointly with the reconstruction loss. Quantitatively, SPP-STF achieves the best RMSE, SAM, PSNR, and UIQI on CIA (0.0254, 0.1522, 31.9330 dB, and 0.8451), the best results across all six metrics on LGC (including RMSE 0.0188 and PSNR 34.6307 dB), and the best RMSE, SSIM, SAM, PSNR, and ERGAS on Tianjin (0.0410, 0.7640, 0.2459, 27.8666 dB, and 30.4838). Ablation studies confirm the complementary benefits of the superpixel and edge modules. The PSF term is interpreted here as a training-time regularizer that provides consistent and physically more reasonable improvements across the three datasets. Structured pruning further indicates that the model can retain spatial structure under substantial compression, while spectral fidelity becomes notably more sensitive beyond roughly 70% pruning.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Slow-moving landslides are complex systems characterized by marked spatial and temporal variability in deformation, which may be difficult to capture using landslide-wide displacement measures. This study investigates the Gimigliano landslide (Calabria, southern Italy), a large, long-lived and urbanized slope instability, by integrating multi-temporal Sentinel-1 SBAS-InSAR observations, data-driven kinematic segmentation, geomorphological analysis and rainfall–deformation relationships. InSAR time series were clustered to identify spatially coherent kinematic domains, which were subsequently compared with independently mapped geomorphological sectors. Continuous and event-based analyses were then used to investigate the temporal relationship between rainfall and landslide deformation. Four kinematic domains were identified, with the highest domain-averaged deformation rates reaching approximately −12.8 mm/yr in the vertical component and −4.8 mm/yr in the east–west component. The identified domains show a clear spatial correspondence with the geomorphological elements of the landslide. Rainfall–deformation relationships showed the highest-magnitude correlations for 60–120 days rainfall accumulation windows, with maximum correlations occurring at lags of approximately 30–35 days for the vertical component and 60–75 days for the east–west component. Event-based analysis further highlighted variable response magnitudes among domains and delayed responses within a few weeks. The results indicate that deformation is associated with antecedent hydrological conditions while retaining substantial spatial heterogeneity, highlighting the value of integrating InSAR, geomorphological and rainfall information for the interpretation of slow-moving landslide dynamics.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Satellite precipitation products alleviate sparse gauge coverage, but their coarse resolution limits hydrometeorological applications. This study develops and evaluates an integrated downscaling–calibration framework for the Yellow River Basin, China, using Geographical-XGBoost (G-XGBoost) for downscaling and Geographical Difference Analysis (GDA) for calibration. Geographically weighted regression, Random Forest, and XGBoost were used for comparison. Fraction disaggregation was compared with an optimal-lag Normalized Difference Vegetation Index (NDVI) method for monthly disaggregation. Using Global Precipitation Measurement (GPM) data and four explanatory variables, including NDVI, Land Surface Temperature (LST), Digital Elevation Model (DEM), and slope, the spatial resolution was improved from 0.1° to 1 km. The downscaled products covered both annual and monthly temporal resolutions. Results show that: (1) G-XGBoost achieved relatively strong overall performance among the four downscaling models before GDA correction across the dry year 2015, wet year 2018, and near-average year 2019, while after GDA correction, all products improved and G-XGBoost remained among the best-performing methods across the three years; and (2) at the monthly scale, fraction disaggregation showed modest numerical improvements in pooled accuracy across the three-year comparison, although its relative performance varied among years and metrics. The framework provides a practical approach for generating high-spatial-resolution precipitation estimates in the Yellow River Basin.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Kelp forests are vital marine habitats with significant ecological, cultural, and economic importance. These ecosystems, found along coastlines, are susceptible to regional and global stressors including coastal development and climate change. This paper presents Satellite-based Kelp Mapping (SKeMa), a novel framework for automatically mapping canopy-forming kelp forests along the coast of British Columbia using Sentinel-2 satellite scenes. SKeMa employs a deep learning semantic segmentation model, offering an efficient alternative to traditional, labor-intensive, and time-consuming kelp-mapping methods. Here, we initially evaluated SKeMa against two conventional, non-deep-learning baselines, NDVI thresholding and Random Forest, and several alternative deep learning architectures. For the best-performing model, an ablation study on input channels identifies a suitable input configuration, and a cross-validation study with independent test sets yields a mean Intersection over Union (IoU) of 0.6024 and F1-score of 0.7491. Average precision and recall are 0.7261 and 0.7951, respectively. Overall, the highest validation metrics were for images with medium to large kelp beds and the lowest for images with small kelp beds, likely because Sentinel-2’s spatial resolution limits mapping of small kelp beds. These results are achieved under a highly imbalanced class distribution, with labeled kelp pixels constituting, on average, less than 0.1% of all pixels in a Sentinel-2 scene. The results demonstrate the model’s ability to detect kelp canopies across diverse coastal regions, particularly for medium to large kelp beds.

Abstract (英文): Remote sensing captions typically interleave domain-specific terminology, object attributes, spatial relations, and scene context within a single sentence, while the corresponding imagery contains targets spanning markedly different spatial extents—from individual vehicles to entire airport layouts. A single global text vector and fixed-scale visual pooling therefore tend to discard the local and hierarchical evidence needed for reliable image–text alignment. Moreover, standard contrastive objectives treat all unmatched pairs as equally unrelated, ignoring the fact that semantically adjacent scenes (e.g., airports and industrial zones) share substantial visual content. To address these issues, we develop a CLIP-style dual encoder with three targeted additions: a text encoder that preserves word-level, phrase-level, and sentence-level representations before fusing them through cross-granularity attention; a visual encoder that associates four hierarchical feature levels with scale-aware receptive-field configurations and incorporates SAM-guided local views during training; and a semantic membership-aware contrastive loss that applies detached, bounded discount weights to semantically close negatives without inverting their penalty sign. On the cleaned RSICD benchmark, the model achieves 87.5% mAP for in-domain prompt-based scene recognition and 34.37% mean recall for scene-constrained retrieval, outperforming the quoted RemoteCLIP and fully fine-tuned CLIP results in the scene-constrained retrieval comparison. Ablation confirms that each of the three components contributes meaningfully to the final performance. The deployable dual encoder contains approximately 59.7 million trainable parameters, and SAM is not required at inference.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Lakes are crucial markers of climate fluctuations, yet identifying water bodies in mountainous regions remains a persistent challenge for satellite remote sensing due to terrain-induced obstacles and dynamic surface conditions of ice and snow. We present a novel approach based solely on Sentinel-1 Synthetic Aperture Radar (SAR) data that allows for the detection of lakes as well as the tracking of their surface area and conditions in mountainous terrain. Our methodology is built around a new index called OASIS, which uses pairs of SAR images to detect and monitor lake surface conditions. We show that this index provides critical insights into lake surface conditions, including the onset and duration of ice cover and the observation of frozen lakes concealed beneath snow. We evaluate this capacity using in situ lake temperature measurements at Arpont Lake. At this lake, the estimated open-water onset typically occurs in mid- to late July, with an ice-free season lasting roughly three to three and a half months. The years 2017 and especially 2022 stand out, showing an earlier break-up (2 July and 24 June, respectively) and longer open-water periods (114 and 132 days; revisit 6 days in 2017–2021 and 12 days in 2016 and 2022–2024). When combined with a segmentation technique, the index allows for accurate quantification and tracking of temporal changes in lake surface area, highlighting lakes that experienced severe drought from 2016 to 2024 in the French Alps. We successfully validated our lake surface area estimates using state-of-the-art lake databases derived from optical sensors.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Mountain glaciers are sensitive indicators of climate change and provide essential freshwater resources for downstream ecosystems and human societies. Although glacier area changes have been extensively investigated in the Greater Caucasus, no long-term assessments of regional glacier ice volume reconstruction have been made. This study presents the first comprehensive reconstruction of glacier ice volume changes across the entire Greater Caucasus over the past 65 years, integrating glacier inventories at five observational periods (1960, 1986, 2000, 2014, and 2025) with the automated PalaeoIce reconstruction framework. Total glacier ice volume declined from 88.08 ± 4.5 km3 in 1960 to 42.66 ± 1.1 km3 in 2025, corresponding to an overall loss of more than half of the Caucasus glacier volume (51.6%) since 1960. Ice volume loss accelerated markedly after 2000, with the most rapid decline (27% or −2.45 yr−1) occurring between 2014 and 2025. Spatial patterns in ice volume change show pronounced regional variability. Relative ice loss over the past 65 years was greatest among glaciers in Azerbaijan, which experienced a 98.4% reduction in ice volume. In comparison, glaciers in Georgia and Russia lost 52.6% and 50.9% of their ice volume, respectively, over the same period. Sectoral analysis further reveals contrasting spatial responses across the Greater Caucasus, with the eastern sector experiencing the greatest proportional ice loss of 71.6% over the 65-year study period, followed by the western sector with 60.4% and the central sector with 47.3%. Despite the continuous regional decline in total ice volume, mean reconstructed glacier thickness increased from ~28.2 m in 1960 to ~33.5 m in 2000 before declining to ~17.4 m by 2025 for the entire Greater Caucasus, indicating substantial changes in the regional glacier-size distribution and geometry through time. These findings provide region-wide quantification of long-term glacier ice volume and thickness evolution in the Greater Caucasus and establish an important baseline for assessing glacier response to ongoing climate change, future water availability, natural hazards, and regional cryospheric evolution.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Outbreaks of the Siberian silk moth (Dendrolimus sibiricus) pose an increasingly serious threat to larch forests in Mongolia, particularly as rising air temperatures and increasing drought frequency create favorable conditions for pest proliferation, degrading forest ecosystem stability. This study presents a UAV-based deep learning (DL) framework that integrates multispectral (MS) and LiDAR sensor data to detect individual trees and classify their health condition in a Dendrolimus sibiricus-affected larch forest. Field surveys were conducted over a 59-hectare study area using UAV flights during the pre-pesticide application period. Vegetation indices (NDVI, GNDVI, NDRE and CIRE) combined with tree structural point cloud data were employed to train a Mask R-CNN DL model to classify tree health into 3 classes: (i) healthy, (ii) moderate and (iii) severely damaged. The model was evaluated on 10 held-out test plots, containing 573 trees. Detection precision, recall and F1-score were 0.679, 0.684 and 0.682, while Total (detection plus correct health class) precision, recall and F1-score were 0.627, 0.632 and 0.630, respectively. Compared with spectral-only input (RGB plus vegetation indices), the full 10-channel input improved the Detection F1 from 0.675 to 0.682 and the Total F1 from 0.619 to 0.630. The findings contribute to forest pest early warning systems, hotspot identification and forest health monitoring in Mongolia.

Abstract (英文): Urban environments consist of highly heterogeneous surface materials whose physical and radiative properties influence surface energy exchange, heat storage, and urban microclimate conditions. While urban materials have been extensively investigated in the visible, near-infrared, and shortwave-infrared regions, their characterisation in the long-wave infrared (LWIR) domain remains comparatively limited, particularly using airborne hyperspectral imaging. This study assesses the potential of airborne hyperspectral LWIR emissivity data for urban surface-material characterisation and discrimination. Hyperspectral data were acquired over Dessau-Roßlau, Germany, using the Telops Hyper-Cam Airborne Mini. The radiance data were geometrically corrected, mosaicked, atmospherically compensated, and processed using temperature-emissivity separation to derive a 66-band emissivity mosaic. After MNF-based denoising, 11 material endmembers were extracted from a representative urban-industrial subset, including asphalt, concrete, cobblestone, bitumen roofing, metal surfaces, solar panels, storage silos, tree cover, and sparse grass. Selected airborne endmembers showed generally consistent spectral shapes when compared with independent ECOSTRESS and LUMA laboratory reference spectra. Linear and radial basis function Support Vector Machine classifications were then applied and evaluated using spatially independent validation regions. The linear SVM achieved the highest overall accuracy of 84.95%, balanced accuracy of 85.30%, macro-averaged F1 score of 82.34%, and a kappa coefficient of 0.83, while the RBF-SVM reached 81.19%, 83.06%, 77.68%, and 0.79, respectively. Solar panels, concrete, cobblestone, metallic materials, tree cover, and sparse grass showed high class-specific performance, whereas aged asphalt, bitumen roofing, and storage silos remained more difficult to distinguish. Overall, the results demonstrate the strong potential of airborne hyperspectral LWIR emissivity imaging for urban material discrimination under real-world conditions and support the development of dedicated LWIR spectral libraries for urban environments.

</details>

Total SMW area increased from 53,843.67 ha in 1990 to 71,637.39 ha in 2020, while patch number increased from 104,011 to 125,971; patches smaller than 3 hm2 remained dominant. High-density areas were concentrated in the Jianghuai Plain, Yangtze River plain, Chaohu Lake region, and agricultural drainage networks, with a persistent northeast–southwest distribution orientation. Land-use transitions were dominated by exchanges among cropland, water bodies, and SMWs. At constant 2020 grain prices, total ESV increased from approximately CNY 3.668 billion in 1990 to CNY 6.840 billion in 2020, with hydrological regulation remaining among the largest service components. Geodetector results showed temporally varying single-factor spatial associations, while interaction patterns suggested potential joint spatial associations among natural and anthropogenic factors. These results support spatially targeted SMW conservation and management in Anhui Province.

Abstract (英文): In building change detection from high-resolution remote sensing imagery, genuine building changes typically appear as local alterations in roof contours and spatial layouts, whereas differences in solar elevation, atmospheric conditions, and sensor calibration introduce cross-temporal appearance drift in unchanged regions. When the two types of difference are jointly encoded in a single feature space, appearance drift tends to dominate the network response. We therefore propose SARE-CD, a dense, pixel-wise, soft-gated heterogeneous mixture-of-experts (MoE) framework that combines scene-level bias correction with functionally distinct expert modeling. At the feature front end, SARE-CD generates affine parameters from the channel statistics of the bitemporal change representation and applies them in residual form to suppress systematic radiometric drift. The Structure, Appearance, and Adaptive Experts then model geometric boundary changes, local appearance perturbations, and composite differences, respectively, and a pixel-wise router assigns expert weights at each location, with routing-entropy and weak load-balancing regularization used to stabilize the division of labor among the experts. On LEVIR-CD+, S2Looking, and SYSU-CD, SARE-CD achieves F1 scores of 91.58%, 65.78%, and 84.46%, all of which are the highest among the compared methods. In addition, controlled perturbation experiments show that F1 retention remains high under hue shifts, soft shadows, and small misregistration, whereas extreme additive brightness offsets lead to a notable F1 decline on S2Looking.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Appearance differences between acquisition dates can increase change responses at unchanged locations. Standard supervision on augmented pairs does not explicitly isolate this additional response. We define an input-level counterfactual response as the logit difference produced by the same fixed EMA teacher at the same pixel when the original time-2 image is replaced by a specified appearance intervention. This paired model response holds the scene geometry, label, teacher parameters, and operator realization fixed; it is not a causal reconstruction of the physical acquisition process. We propose counterfactual false-positive mining with reference prediction preservation (CFPM-RP). An exponential-moving-average (EMA) teacher compares an original pair with a pair undergoing a controlled appearance transformation. Positive logit increments within stable unchanged regions define candidate locations. A smooth one-sided loss penalizes the perturbed student response relative to the original teacher prediction, with auxiliary support weighting, segmentation supervision, and changed-region constraints. The auxiliary paths are used only during training, retaining the deployed detector architecture. Experiments cover LEVIR-CD, WHU-CD, and SYSU-CD, eight appearance perturbations, an equal-budget continuation, eight additional comparison methods, and a second backbone. CFPM-RP attains perturbation-averaged F1 values of 91.444%, 71.181%, and 77.842% on the three datasets, respectively. Relative to Generic-FT, false-positive counts decrease by 10.37%, 11.15%, and 11.27%. The results show that CFPM-RP reduces appearance-induced false positives while retaining competitive change-detection performance.

</details>

Abstract (英文): With the rapid expansion of UAV applications, UAVs have posed growing challenges to public safety and airspace management. Pan–tilt–zoom (PTZ) cameras are widely used for monitoring, but conventional rule-based control lacks adaptability in dynamic environments, and existing deep reinforcement learning (DRL) methods mostly rely on discrete action spaces with fixed time steps, preventing fine-grained camera adjustment. Meanwhile, infrared UAVs are often small, distant, and low-resolution, leading to low detection confidence and difficult classification. To address these issues, this paper proposes a hybrid-action-space DRL framework for PTZ camera control. PTZ control is modeled as a discrete-continuous hierarchical subspace: the agent selects a discrete action type and predicts its continuous execution duration for adaptive precise control. A target stable tracking reward function integrating object recognition accuracy, position, and size guides the agent to improve detection confidence and complete recognition efficiently. Furthermore, a lightweight multi-scale feature fusion recognition network combining CNN and Transformer is designed for accurate state representation, and two infrared UAV datasets are constructed to support training and evaluation. Experiments in a Unity3D-based simulation environment show that the proposed framework significantly improves detection confidence and recognition accuracy, maintains high tracking stability across multiple scenarios, and generalizes well with different object detectors, outperforming rule-based and single-action-space baselines.

This study assimilates clear-sky and all-sky Channel 10 brightness temperatures (BTs) from GOES-R Advanced Baseline Imager (ABI). The implementation is achieved in a convection-permitting model using a 5 min cycling ensemble Kalman filter (EnKF) framework. A quality control procedure is developed to enable the effective assimilation of clear-sky ABI observations. Results show that clear-sky assimilation partially removes spurious background clouds and adjusts temperature and moisture in the near-storm environment. All-sky assimilation substantially improves the analyzed cloud field, produces stronger in-storm thermodynamic and dynamical increments, as well as directly constrains hydrometeor variables. In addition, assimilating all-sky observations reduces errors of analyzed surface temperature and humidity by over 25%. Forecast verification further indicates that all-sky assimilation provides more persistent forecast improvements in terms of BT and reflectivity quantities than clear-sky assimilation does. Both clear-sky and all-sky assimilations improve the spatial coverage and intensity of accumulated precipitation, but assimilating all-sky observations produces substantially larger FSS gains (0.163–0.380 versus 0.02–0.13).

Abstract (英文): GNSS radio occultation (RO) is widely used to detect metallic-ion sporadic-E layers on a global scale, but in auroral environments, particle precipitation-driven ionization can produce auroral-E structures with similar RO signatures. This overlap complicates reliable identification of sporadic-E and may bias high-latitude occurrence rate estimates. In this study, we quantify auroral contamination in RO-based sporadic-E detection and evaluate the performance of both threshold-based and multivariate classifier approaches. A dataset of 106 conjunction events was constructed using RO observations from the Spire and COSMIC-1 constellations, together with co-located ionosonde and magnetometer measurements at two high-latitude sites and an ionosonde at a mid-latitude site. Events were labeled as sporadic-E, auroral-E, or non-event from manual ionogram inspection. Two commonly used RO detection methods were applied: an excess phase perturbation Δϕ approach and an amplitude-based S4 approach. Results show that true sporadic-E events are primarily identified when both amplitude- and phase-based thresholds are satisfied. In contrast, more than 80% of auroral-E events satisfy at least one sporadic-E detection criterion, with false triggering of auroral-E and non-events dominated by the phase-based method. A Random Forest classifier was developed as a proof-of-concept model showing success at classifying sporadic-E and non-events, although ambiguity between auroral-E and sporadic-E persists likely due to overlapping phase-perturbation distributions. A Multiple Phase Screen Model is implemented to provide physical insight into the RO observations, where the vertically thicker auroral-E layers are shown to produce significant phase perturbations but negligible amplitude scintillation. These results demonstrate that RO-based detection methods identify E-region structure rather than formation mechanism, implying that high-latitude sporadic-E occurrence rates derived from phased-based RO observations are likely overestimated without additional context.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Semantic segmentation of multispectral satellite imagery is central to large-scale tropical deforestation monitoring, yet cross-region deployment suffers from severe accuracy degradation due to radiometric, atmospheric, and geographic covariate shifts. We introduce dares (Domain Adaptation via Rényi Entropy for Semantic Segmentation), an unsupervised domain adaptation framework that transfers deforestation-mapping models across biomes without target annotations for training. Its core mechanism performs class-conditional Rényi-2 (α=2) alignment on the deepest feature grid together with decoupled confidence-weighted Rényi-2 entropy minimization on target predictions, stabilized against a degenerate collapse as detailed in Methods. Evaluated on an ecologically critical transfer task from the Brazilian Amazon to the Colombian deforestation arc using Sentinel-2 MSI data, dares attains 64.65% target-test mIoU (78.43% DICE) under moderate radiometric attenuation, numerically outperforming Source-Only (44.24%), ADVENT (50.24%), and CBST (48.92%) while maintaining 76.75% source-domain discriminability. Across a Low→Medium→High stress trajectory, dares follows a non-monotonic path from 75.42%→64.65%→69.54%, a 5.9-point Low-to-High change with a Medium dip and a High rebound. It is not the best at Low, where ADVENT (85.78%) and Source-Only (78.93%) exceed its 75.42%, while its advantage opens from Medium (+14.4 points over ADVENT, +20.4 over Source-Only) at the cost of the lowest source-test mIoU (76.75%). Component analysis indicates that decoupled entropy minimization drives target adaptation (−17.2 points if removed), whereas alignment, anti-collapse floors, and repulsion act as source-stabilizing regularizers rather than target maximizers.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate mapping of medicinal plants is essential for resource inventory and sustainable management. While Unmanned Aerial Vehicle (UAV) hyperspectral sensing provides a powerful monitoring tool, the high-dimensional spectral ambiguities and the black-box nature of conventional global classifiers severely restrict mapping accuracy and interpretability. To overcome this, we developed an interpretable, class-specific framework for fine-scale crop identification. A multi-feature pool was distilled through a hierarchical Successive Projections Algorithm (SPA)–Competitive Adaptive Reweighted Sampling (CARS)–Genetic Algorithm (GA)–Recursive Feature Elimination (RFE) strategy, isolating class-specific diagnostic fingerprints that transition mapping from black-box modeling to an explainable mechanism. The tailored feature subsets were supplied to parallel Random Forest binary classifiers to generate class-specific probability outputs, which were subsequently fed into a Logistic Regression decision-level fusion layer that calibrated prediction probabilities and resolved inter-class conflicts to yield the definitive classification results. Applied in Yunfu, Guangdong, the framework mapped target species (Murraya exotica, Zanthoxylum nitidum, and Melicope pteleifolia) with an overall accuracy of 97.35% (Kappa = 0.97), outperforming conventional benchmark models such as standard multi-class Random Forest, Extreme Gradient Boosting, and spectral angle mapper. These findings demonstrate that coupling class-specific feature distillation with probability-calibrated decision fusion provides a robust, interpretable paradigm for precision agricultural mapping in heterogeneous environments.

Abstract (英文): The spatial distribution of river ice types during the freeze-up period provides important information on channel freezing and ice-cover development and serves as a basis for ice-condition monitoring and ice-jam flood risk assessment. However, river ice types differ considerably in spatial scale, spectral response, and surface structure, making their fine-scale identification under complex ice conditions challenging. In this study, nine representative frozen reaches along the Inner Mongolia section of the Yellow River were investigated using UAV multispectral orthomosaics with a spatial resolution of approximately 0.15 m. Four representative river-surface classes—open water, transparent ice, smooth ice, and rough ice—were mapped. Their responses in the visible, red-edge, and near-infrared bands were analyzed together with spectral indices and gray-level cooccurrence matrix (GLCM) texture features to characterize interclass spectral and textural differences. A deep-learning mapping model integrating multispectral and textural information was subsequently developed and evaluated on spatially independent reaches. Permutation importance was also used to quantify the contribution of each remote-sensing feature. The results showed that the four surface classes exhibited distinct spectral and textural characteristics. Transparent ice and open water showed limited spectral differences in several bands, whereas texture features captured differences in their local spatial structures and improved the model’s ability to distinguish spectrally similar classes. The model combining spectral indices and GLCM texture features accurately delineated the spatial distributions of the river ice types. In the spatially independent test reaches, it achieved an overall accuracy of 95.80%, a Macro-F1 score of 94.53%, and a mean intersection over union (mIoU) of 90.14%. The model achieved an mIoU of 84.55% on an additional independent test reach with complex ice conditions, which was excluded from training and parameter optimization. Feature-contribution analysis identified spectral indices and texture features as important sources of discriminatory information. NDUWI and NDFSI, together with texture measures calculated from the red and near-infrared bands, ranked among the most informative inputs. This result suggests that the four river-surface classes were distinguished not only by their spectral differences but also by variations in local surface patterns. The UAV imagery therefore supported detailed mapping of heterogeneous frozen reaches and provided spatially explicit information relevant to ice-condition monitoring, analysis of river-ice development, and assessment of potential ice-jam flooding.

Abstract (英文): Large-scale afforestation has been carried out across the Loess Plateau since 1999, yet its impact on terrestrial water storage (TWS) remains uncertain. Here, we integrated GRACE-derived TWS data, leaf area index (LAI), and multiple ancillary datasets, and applied a multivariate regression framework incorporating precipitation, LAI, and temperature to quantify their conditional associations with annual TWS change (TWSC) in the substantially greening region of the Loess Plateau. The signals in the Loess Plateau observed by GRACE were separated into coal mining, soil erosion, leakage of water storage changes from North China, and local TWS using multiple datasets and their variation characteristics were analyzed. In the substantially greening area, annual TWSC was negatively associated with LAI after accounting for precipitation and temperature. Multiplying the fitted LAI coefficient by the observed LAI trend yielded an estimated trend of −2.1 ± 0.5 mm/yr in the LAI-associated component of annual TWSC during the study period. Observed runoff increased by only ~0.6 mm/yr, compared with ~1.7 mm/yr expected from the historical precipitation–runoff relationship, indicating a runoff reduction of ~1.1 ± 0.8 mm/yr relative to that expected from precipitation alone. This departure cannot be uniquely attributed to vegetation because concurrent changes in human activities were not explicitly resolved. Analyses using multiple evapotranspiration products further associated increasing LAI with an ET trend component of approximately 2.0–7.5 mm/yr across the analyzed products. Vegetation greening was associated with regional TWS changes, with enhanced evapotranspiration emerging as an important hydrological pathway. These findings underscore the need to account for non-target mass signals and vegetation–water interactions when evaluating water-cycle responses to ecological restoration.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Landslide susceptibility assessment in cold, high-altitude regions is complicated by interactions among topographic, lithological, hydrological, and anthropogenic factors. Machine learning methods are widely used, but model selection, spatially independent evaluation, and interpretation of covariate-adjusted associations remain challenging. This study developed an integrated framework for landslide susceptibility assessment in Tongren City, on the northeastern margin of the Qinghai–Tibet Plateau. The framework combined H2O AutoML, nested spatial cross-validation, Shapley additive explanations (SHAP), partial dependence plots (PDPs), and double machine learning (DML). Each of the 20 sampling realizations included 217 landslide points and 217 non-landslide points, with 13 conditioning factors retained after collinearity screening. DML used partially linear regression with spatial cross-fitting across 200 repetitions to estimate covariate-adjusted associations. Stacked Ensemble achieved the highest mean area under the receiver operating characteristic curve (AUC) of 0.953 ± 0.009 across the 20 realizations. Spatial out-of-fold evaluation showed that the 20% of the study area with the highest susceptibility contained 93.27% ± 1.51% of held-out landslides (mean ± standard deviation). Elevation, distance to roads, lithology, distance to faults, and land use/land cover together accounted for 76.69% of the total mean absolute SHAP values. PDPs revealed nonlinear relationships between conditioning factors and model-predicted susceptibility. Elevation, interpreted as a proxy for valley position, and distance to roads were the only factors with statistically significant negative covariate-adjusted associations after multiple-testing correction. The random common cause (RCC) test indicated that DML estimates remained stable under the random-covariate perturbations examined. The framework distinguishes predictive contributions from covariate-adjusted associations and supports landslide monitoring and spatial planning in cold, high-altitude plateau transition zones.

Abstract (英文): Polarimetric synthetic aperture radar (PolSAR) image classification requires the joint use of polarimetric scattering information and spatial context. However, aggregating or propagating information across locations with different scattering characteristics may mix features from dissimilar regions, and recurrent state propagation in Mamba can extend this influence along scan paths. Class-agnostic regions generated by the Segment Anything Model (SAM) from Pauli pseudo-RGB images may also span multiple land-cover classes, which may reduce the reliability of direct regional consistency constraints. This study proposes SSHMamba, a SAM-assisted scattering-guided hybrid selective Mamba network. It constructs a seven-channel scattering prior from the Pauli components, span, and H/A/α parameters. The local branch combines center-outward spiral rearrangement, one-dimensional convolution, and neighborhood weighting based on spatial distance and scattering similarity. In the long-range branch, the scattering prior conditions the state-space parameters, while scattering similarity and a learnable gate regulate the contribution of preceding states. Multidirectional outputs are aggregated using pixel-wise direction weights, and local and long-range features are fused using scattering-prior-driven channel-wise coefficients. During training, SAM candidate regions are screened using scattering homogeneity, prediction reliability, and region area before a soft regional consistency constraint is applied; SAM is not used during inference. Across three independent training runs under the same fixed data split, SSHMamba achieves 99.12 ± 0.04% OA, 99.13 ± 0.04% AA, and 0.9904 ± 0.0005 Kappa on Flevoland, and 99.42 ± 0.02% OA, 97.52 ± 0.03% AA, and 0.9909 ± 0.0004 Kappa on San Francisco. In the fixed reference runs, ECP-Mamba yields a slightly higher OA on Flevoland (99.30% versus 99.16%), whereas SSHMamba yields a higher OA on San Francisco (99.44% versus 99.20%). On Flevoland, removing the complete scattering-guided selective state propagation mechanism reduces OA from 99.16% to 96.48%. The SAM ablation shows that direct consistency over raw SAM regions yields 97.12% OA, candidate-region reliability screening increases it to 98.72%, and the full reliability-controlled SAM-assisted strategy reaches 99.16%. On the DLR E-SAR Oberpfaffenhofen dataset, SSHMamba achieves 99.29 ± 0.07% OA, 99.15 ± 0.04% AA, and 0.9889 ± 0.0006 Kappa across three runs, providing additional cross-sensor and cross-scene evidence.

</details>

Abstract (英文): SAR tomography (TomoSAR) is capable of separating scatterers at different elevations within the same range–azimuth resolution cell. Differential TomoSAR (D-TomoSAR) further incorporates temporal information, enabling joint estimation of scatterer elevation and deformation velocity. Most existing sparse inversion methods rely on single-polarization observations, and the joint use of observations across polarization channels remains limited. Separating adjacent and weak scatterers in complex urban layover scenarios remains challenging. Sparse Bayesian learning (SBL) offers strong capabilities for sparse support recovery and super-resolution parameter estimation. When SBL is applied over a global fine grid in the two-dimensional elevation–deformation parameter space, the high correlation among neighboring dictionary atoms may hinder the separation of adjacent scatterers and affect the stability of sparse support recovery. To address these issues, this paper proposes a two-step sparse Bayesian learning (TSSBL) D-TomoSAR method with a dual-polarization shared-support constraint. First, joint Orthogonal Matching Pursuit (OMP) search is performed on a coarse grid using HH/VV observations to achieve global coarse localization of potential scatterers. Subsequently, a local fine grid is constructed around the candidate positions, and SBL is performed under an HH/VV shared-support constraint to achieve refined estimation of scatterer elevation and linear deformation velocity. Finally, the number of effective scatterers is determined through a statistical test based on residual reduction. Simulation and real-data experiments demonstrate favorable scatterer separation and joint elevation–deformation estimation performance. Using 22 PAZ HH/VV images, the proposed Dual-Pol TSSBL method achieves a building-height root-mean-square error (RMSE) of 2.72 m for five buildings with reference heights. The results indicate that combining dual-polarization shared support with coarse-to-fine SBL inversion can effectively improve joint elevation–deformation estimation for urban buildings in D-TomoSAR under limited observations, with potential for future application to large-scale urban building deformation monitoring using long SAR time series.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Rapid cataloging of non-cooperative targets in newly formed space debris clouds requires robust radar-based multi-target tracking to form valid observation arcs and reliable association of arcs acquired during temporally separated observation windows. Dense target distributions and ambiguous measurement-to-track and arc-to-arc relationships often result in missed measurements and false associations. To address the challenge, this study proposes an iterative joint probabilistic data association (JPDA) procedure to perform radar tracking of multiple targets in a single observation window, in which a data mining operation is implemented after each JPDA iteration to maximize observation utilization rate. In the two-arc association stage, candidate arc pairs are first constructed using two-arc association algorithm based on the Lambert boundary-value problem, and are then further screened according to the residuals of two-arc orbit determination. This strategy suppresses false associations while maintaining high true positive associations. The algorithms were experimented with simulated radar observations of 100 targets in the Iridium 33-Cosmos 2251 debris cloud in the time period 2 h–4 h after the collision. Results show that the iterative JPDA tracking increased the number of successfully tracked targets in the first observation window to 96 from 88 using the single-run JPDA tracking, corresponding to an improvement of approximately 9%. The first-round data-mining operation increased the minimum observation utilization rate from 54.58% to 71.67%, demonstrating its effectiveness in recovering unused observations in the JPDA tracking. For the two-arc association, the Lambert-problem-based method suffered a false association rate of 95.17%, although it achieved an association recall rate of 96.88%. In contrast, the improved two-arc association method reduced the false association rate to 5.26% and maintained a high association recall rate of 93.75%. These results demonstrate that the proposed framework supports rapid and reliable tracking, association, orbit determination, and cataloging of non-cooperative space targets.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Daily precipitation estimates over the Haihe River Basin show strong terrain-related error contrasts. We developed a regional two-stage post-processing fusion workflow for five gridded precipitation products. Physically constrained XGBoost-Tweedie regression uses non-negativity and an archive-derived MaxPrec reference to generate a bias-corrected candidate, ENS_Reg. Dynamic Classification Probability Weighting (DCPW) then uses classifier softmax outputs to weight ENS_Reg and the five original products, producing ENS_RegCla. Models were developed using 2000–2018 data and evaluated on 357,134 station-day records from 2019 to 2023. With the original 1961–2022 MaxPrec reference, ENS_RegCla achieved MAE = 1.064 mm/day, RMSE = 4.807 mm/day, R2 = 0.666, and KGE = 0.746. MAE was 12.6–15.3% lower than for the evaluated equal-weight, inverse-error, and neighborhood error-variance weighting baselines. At a 0.1 mm/day threshold, CSI = 0.653 and HSS = 0.720 exceeded the two specified ablations. Grouped RMSE favored the ablations, whereas grouped MAE differences were not robust. Restricting MaxPrec to 1961–2018 retained better MAE, RMSE, and KGE than all five original products. However, CSI/HSS fell to 0.633/0.698, approximately matching GSMaP-Gauge in CSI but falling below its HSS. The event-skill advantage over the ablation without MaxPrec nearly disappeared, indicating strong dependence on the reference period. Full-sample evaluation included both training and temporal-evaluation periods and yielded pooled MAE = 0.677 mm/day and KGE = 0.783. Of the 2,004,598 full-sample records, 1,647,464 (82.2%) were from model development. GTCH provided an assumption-limited relative-uncertainty diagnostic; the lower minimum statistic after candidate-set expansion was interpreted descriptively, without establishing true uncertainty reduction. Candidate errors may be correlated because ENS_Reg is derived from the original products. The framework supports condition-aware regional precipitation reconstruction, while moderate and heavy rainfall remain priorities for refinement.

Abstract (英文): Accurate spectral and radiometric calibration is essential for reliable greenhouse gas retrieval from satellite hyperspectral observations. However, long-term in-orbit operation may introduce wavelength shifts and radiometric response inconsistencies, particularly within narrow atmospheric absorption bands, reducing the reliability of Level-1 radiance products. This study proposes an observation-dependent adaptive reference spectrum framework for spectral and radiometric calibration of the Greenhouse Gas Monitoring Instrument (GMI) onboard the GaoFen-5B (GF-5B) satellite. The proposed framework integrates the SCIATRAN radiative transfer model with the instrument line shape (ILS) of GMI to generate physically consistent reference spectra under varying observation conditions. Sensitivity analysis was performed to identify dominant parameters affecting simulated radiance spectra, and an adaptive reference spectrum library was constructed by considering surface reflectance, solar zenith angle, aerosol conditions, and geographic characteristics. The generated reference spectra were subsequently used to estimate spectral shifts and correct radiometric response deviations between observed and simulated spectra. GF-5B GMI observations acquired from September 2021 to December 2024 over Dunhuang, Golmud, and Railroad Valley (RRV) were used for validation. Results demonstrate that the proposed framework effectively reduces spectral offsets, improves radiometric consistency, and enhances spectral agreement for the O2, CO2-1, and CO2-2 bands. The multi-site validation confirms the robustness of the proposed approach under different surface and atmospheric conditions. This study provides a physically constrained and transferable calibration framework for improving the long-term spectral and radiometric consistency of greenhouse gas hyperspectral observations and supporting more reliable atmospheric composition retrievals.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Global climate change increases the frequency of synoptic forcing, across the Mediterranean Basin. These systems often trigger convective storms characterized by intense rainfall, resulting in the formation of linear erosion features (LEFs), such as gullies. Previous studies focused on the morphological characteristics of LEFs, whereas the relationships among LEF geomorphology, landscape topography, and rainfall were poorly studied. This knowledge gap is attributable to the multitude of topographic and rainfall variables, which make manual analysis both challenging and time-consuming. Recent advances in high-spatial-resolution satellite imagery, together with machine learning techniques, provide a potential alternative. Accordingly, the following objectives were defined: (1) design a framework for quantifying temporal changes in fresh herbaceous vegetation across different natural and human-induced linear erosion features (LEFs) in arid hilly loess landscapes using satellite imagery data; (2) develop a predictive model for geomorphological patterns of LEFs based on rainfall and topographic parameters. Three sites were studied, all in the northern Negev, with a semiarid climate and loess deposits covered with a dense LEF net. A unique framework was developed, including the use of acquired PlanetScope® imagery datasets, the allocation of elevation segments along the LEFs, and the normalization of the median NDVI value of each segment by that of the reference plot. Using ‘Random Forest’, the importance, and the directionality of the factors affecting the LEF fresh herbaceous over the elevation segments was determined by a linear correlation. The finding indicates that the NFVR of the LEF baseline (Intercept) was affected only by <1% from the aspect, while there was a 26% increase in NFVR downslope. The LEF (or segment) length affects the Intercept of the NFVR correlation, changing by 21% and 37%, respectively. The steepness affects the NFVR Intercept only by (0–8%), while there was a 43% effect on the NFVR change. In practice, the framework enables the prediction of segments vulnerable to land collapse, and adequate management of gullied areas.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Seabed pockmarks are important geomorphological indicators of shallow fluid migration, shallow gas activity, and gas hydrate-related processes. However, automatic pockmark detection from shaded-relief bathymetric imagery remains challenging because many pockmarks have small image-space footprints, occur densely, have weak boundaries, and are embedded in complex seabed backgrounds. To address these issues, this study proposes FSLA-RTDETR, an improved four-scale RT-DETR network for dense small-object seabed pockmark detection. The model introduces SAC-C2f to enhance high-level feature representation, the LPEA Encoder to improve global contextual modeling, and a DGCST-based bidirectional feature fusion network to integrate shallow spatial details and deep semantic information. In addition, the high-resolution P2 feature is incorporated into the decoder to improve small-object localization. A pockmark detection dataset was constructed from publicly available BOEM bathymetric data from the northern Gulf of Mexico, containing 880 images and 8013 annotated pockmark instances. Experimental results show that FSLA-RTDETR achieves mAP50, mAP75, and mAP50–95 values of 0.9218, 0.6221, and 0.5485, respectively. Compared with the baseline RT-DETR, mAP75 and mAP50–95 increase by 6.01 and 2.22 percentage points, respectively. Under the present no-pretraining setting, comparative experiments further demonstrate that FSLA-RTDETR achieves superior high-IoU localization performance compared with the representative object detection models evaluated in this study, providing an effective method for automatic seabed pockmark detection and geomorphological mapping.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The erosion of coastal chalk cliffs is a significant concern for communities in Northern Europe, with rates of retreat varying widely depending on local conditions. This study presents six years of UAV photogrammetry monitoring of chalk cliffs in Sussex, UK, producing 2.5D change detection maps to quantify erosion at Telscombe and Peacehaven. The results demonstrate a strong correlation between rates of lower-cliff erosion and wave energy at Peacehaven, with an r2 value of 0.82. However, periods of intensive mass-wasting can render these relationships unreliable. The relationship for Telscombe, which experiences much more mass-wasting, demonstrates an r2 of 0.37, which improves to 0.48 with the removal of an outlier. Cumulative erosion patterns follow a gradualistic trend, superimposed by episodic mass-wasting, with lower-cliff erosion rates increasing by approximately 3.2 times during storm-forced high-energy intervals compared to stable periods. A similar pattern is seen for mass-wasting events, although erosion intensities may be more than 6 times greater during periods of storminess. These findings highlight the role of storminess in accelerating erosion in the coastal zone.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Although deep learning-based approaches have significantly advanced intelligent radar image interpretation, existing methods predominantly rely on single-view inputs, which inherently limits target recognition performance. In practical scenarios, multi-view radar imagery provides richer structural and feature representations of targets compared to single-view observations. To effectively exploit this comprehensive information, we propose a new Multi-view Laplacian Propagation Fusion Network (MLPFN) for radar image target recognition. First, a Reparameterization Perspective-Aware (RPA) backbone is designed, incorporating a Dual-level Multi-path Rep-Kernel (DMPK) and a Multi-level Dual-path Attention Module (MDAM). This architecture mitigates the sensitivity of recognition performance to aspect angle variations and enhances the extraction of discriminative target features. Second, a Laplacian Multi-view Feature Propagation Algorithm (LMFPA) is introduced to encode structural correlations among different views, thereby augmenting the feature discriminability for multi-view targets. Third, a Multi-view Feature Adaptive Fusion Module (MFAFM) is developed to integrate global contextual information across views into a unified representation, reducing redundancy while maximizing feature distinctiveness. Furthermore, a Stage-wise Guided Constraint Training Mechanism (SGCTM) is devised to supervise the feature extraction process across different network layers during training, refining the network’s feature learning and recognition capabilities. Extensive experiments conducted on multi-view radar image datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed MLPFN.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Monitoring the dynamics of gullies in Mediterranean agrosystems is critical but severely limited by agricultural practices that generate continuous machinery compaction and tillage of the soil, camouflaging topographic features and disturbing erosion analyses. This study discusses strategies to quantify erosion by distinguishing natural erosion from agricultural disturbance, integrating multitemporal LiDAR data (2014, 2020, 2024) with gully inventories using orthophoto maps (2011–2022). The volumetric changes were assessed using DoD for the periods 2014–2020 and 2020–2024, and the morphometric performance of the TNO index was further validated using ROC curves in R. Both approaches performed consistently higher in detecting permanent gullies. The TNO index has proven its usefulness for semi-automated delimitation in large or low-data areas. By intersecting DoD surfaces with the gully inventory, localized erosion processes were successfully isolated from the surrounding agricultural landscape matrix. The quantified rate of erosion in the gullies refers strictly to their internal dynamics, which have been close to the natural threshold for soil formation, indicating a risk of soil degradation.

Abstract (英文): Mountain forest greenness varies across seasons, yet growing-season averages can mask season-specific relationships with climate variability. We assessed seasonal and elevational variation in historical forest greenness within areas classified as forest in 2024, temporal variation in spring NDVI–climate relationships, and the association between nighttime light (NTL) intensity and forest greenness in Changbai Mountain from 2000 to 2024. We used MODIS-derived normalized difference vegetation index (NDVI), 1 km temperature and precipitation datasets, elevation zones, and annual nighttime light (NTL) data. We applied a forest mask derived from the 2024 Annual China Land Cover Dataset (CLCD) and retained 1 km pixels with at least 70% forest cover. NDVI increased significantly over the aggregate growing season (slope = 0.0011 NDVI yr−1, R2 = 0.584, p < 0.001), summer (slope = 0.0006 NDVI yr−1, R2 = 0.188, p = 0.030), and autumn (slope = 0.0019 NDVI yr−1, R2 = 0.280, p = 0.007). Autumn showed the largest estimated slope; this ranking alone does not establish statistical differences among seasonal slopes. Spring showed no significant long-term trend (slope = 0.0011 NDVI yr−1, R2 = 0.087, p = 0.152), but spring NDVI showed a strong positive correlation with temperature at the regional scale (r = 0.760, p < 0.001), with positive correlations maintained across all elevation zones. In spring regression models, temperature showed a larger standardized coefficient magnitude than precipitation across elevation zones, with all variance inflation factors ≤ 1.22. Exploratory five-year sliding-window correlations suggested temporal variation in spring NDVI–climate relationships, although each estimate was based on only five annual observations. At low elevation, the NTL–NDVI regression was significant (R2 = 0.198, p = 0.026), whereas associations at middle and high elevations were not statistically significant; this raw association should be interpreted cautiously because both variables may contain concurrent temporal trends. These results describe seasonal and elevational greenness patterns within the 2024 forest mask, highlighting the distinct spring temperature association and elevation-dependent relationships between NTL intensity and forest greenness.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Timely and accurate mapping of flood inundation extents in rice-growing regions is essential for flood damage assessment. However, fragmented fields and heterogeneous land cover in complex terrain continue to pose challenges. This study proposes a two-stage mapping framework that integrates multi-resolution remote sensing imagery with deep-learning-based semantic segmentation. First, a dual-attention and edge-aware attention network (DAEA-Net) was developed to derive a high-accuracy paddy rice map from very high-resolution (VHR) imagery. Second, the flood extent in paddy fields was delineated using the prior-histogram marker-controlled watershed (PH-MCW) method on SAR imagery with area-of-interest (AOI) filtering based on terrain slope and on the permanent water bodies and potentially inundated land types of the land-type survey data. On the test set, DAEA-Net achieved the highest OA, IoU, F1-score, and Kappa among the compared semantic segmentation models, including U-Net, HRNet, and DeepLabV3+, with values of 0.9205, 0.8747, 0.9331, and 0.8352, respectively. PH-MCW segmentation with AOI filtering, which removes terrain-induced false alarms while retaining water surfaces, improved flood-water detection, achieving F1-scores of 0.821 and 0.764 on two flood-validation reference datasets (NDRCC and AIR-PolSAR-Seg-2.0), respectively. The proposed framework provides a semi-automated solution for mapping flood inundation over paddy rice fields in complex terrain, in which the prior water proportion is refined by local inspection before application.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Marine gravity is traditionally derived from altimeter data using physics-based methods, which are sensitive to altimeter data noise. Although deep learning methods can capture nonlinear relationships between marine gravity and multi-source data, purely data-driven approaches depend strongly on the quality and spatial distribution of training ship-borne gravity data. To combine the advantages of both methods, this study proposes a hybrid marine gravity recovery method that integrates the Inverse Vening Meinesz (IVM) method into a convolutional neural network (CNN) framework. Specifically, the IVM-derived marine gravity is incorporated into the CNN loss function as a constraint, and distance- and topography-dependent hyperparameters are introduced to control its contribution during training. The gravity model derived by the proposed IVM-constrained CNN method is validated using independent ship-borne gravity observations and a recognized marine gravity model. Assessed by ship-borne data, the proposed method improves accuracy by 28.5% and 6.8% compared with the IVM-derived and CNN-derived gravity models, respectively. Comparisons with the recognized model also indicate that the gravity model derived by the proposed method achieves the highest accuracy among the three models. These results show that embedding IVM constraints into deep learning frameworks can significantly improve the accuracy and robustness of marine gravity recovery.

Abstract (英文): Lake water color provides an integrated indicator of aquatic environmental change, yet its long-term phenology and resilience remain poorly understood in arid regions. In this study, Landsat-derived hue angle (α) records were reconstructed for 350 lakes larger than 1 km2 across arid Northwest China from 1984 to 2025. Regional mean α declined significantly over the past 42 years (R2 = 0.51), accompanied by pronounced spatial heterogeneity. Three lake water color phenological types were identified: Aseasonal (75.0%), Summer Greening (13.7%), and Spring Greening (11.3%). Aseasonal lakes were the most stable, whereas seasonal lakes showed greater temporal variability and a tendency to transition toward aseasonality. Lake water color resilience increased in 43% of lakes, declined in 21%, and remained unchanged in 36%, with resilience-loss lakes showing distinct spatial clustering. Lake morphology was most strongly associated with water color change, topography and thermal conditions with its phenology, and watershed population dynamics with its resilience. In situ observations further showed significant relationships between α and optically active constituents and their derived parameters, reflecting the combined optical effects of absorption and scattering and suggesting its potential as a proxy for broader water-quality assessment with more extensive field sampling and validation. These findings highlight contrasting controls on lake-color dynamics, phenology, and resilience, and demonstrate the value of hue angle for monitoring environmental change in vulnerable arid-zone lakes. Overall, these results advance understanding of the long-term environmental responses of arid-zone lakes and provide a scientific basis for targeted monitoring and adaptive management in arid regions.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Vendor-provided rational polynomial coefficients (RPCs) for high-resolution optical satellite imagery often contain systematic biases that are commonly corrected using field-surveyed ground control points (GCPs). Acquiring such points can be costly or infeasible in inaccessible regions. This study proposes an RPC bias compensation framework for KOMPSAT-3 (K3) optical imagery based on the geometric accuracy and stability of KOMPSAT-5 (K5) X-band SAR enhanced high-resolution (EH) imagery, without measuring image coordinates of control points. Three-dimensional ground control data (GCD) are projected into both sensor image spaces via the K5 Range–Doppler (RD) model and the K3 RPC model. A GCD-driven projective transformation provides coarse alignment, followed by fine registration using FFT-based phase correlation (FPC). Registration-derived correspondence coordinates are then used to estimate a strongly regularized image-space affine bias model, yielding a predominantly translational correction. Three GCD sources—Korean public control points (PCPs), ICESat-2 altimetry, and the 30 m SRTM DEM—were evaluated using one K3–K5 image pair over Suncheon, Korea. A sensitivity analysis showed that control points on flat terrain (elevation ≤60 m, slope ≤6°) yielded stable results, whereas points on steep or forested terrain degraded the registration, and increasing the number of GCD points did not systematically improve accuracy. Under these conditions, all three sources reduced the RMSE at independent GNSS checkpoints from 7.1 and 10.6 pixels in the column and row directions to 1.6–2.0 and 1.6–2.1 pixels, respectively. This accuracy is comparable to that of conventional GCP-based compensation (1.7/1.3 pixels), whereas two structural-feature-based matching methods (HOPC and CFOG) did not improve on the original RPCs under the tested implementations. These results demonstrate the potential of SRTM-derived control data for meter-level directional refinement of optical imagery when reliable SAR–optical registration is achieved. Generalization beyond the tested image pair requires further validation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Weakly supervised remote sensing change detection reduces annotation costs but suffers from incomplete class activation maps, unreliable pseudo-label propagation, and insufficient discrimination between adjacent change regions. To address these limitations, we propose PRIWCD, a weakly supervised framework for remote sensing change detection. PRIWCD introduces a dynamic prototype comparison module to enhance high-level differential features by modeling unchanged regions and diverse change patterns with multiple dynamic prototypes. A dual relationship propagation guidance module further refines class activation maps by jointly exploiting changes in bi-temporal attention relationships and feature similarity. In addition, instance separation regularization improves feature compactness within pseudo-instances and enhances the separation between changed regions and the background. Experiments on WHU-CD, CLCD, and DSIFN demonstrate the effectiveness of PRIWCD. The proposed method achieves the best mean F1 scores of 79.10%, 62.33%, and 62.68%, respectively, outperforming the compared weakly supervised methods. The results demonstrate that PRIWCD effectively improves pseudo-label quality and provides robust change detection under image-level supervision.

</details>
